- **SR-12** — Runs MUST be deterministic given a seed: on the same pinned software environment and hardware class, the same seed and config reproduce reported metrics within 0.5 percentage points; across environments, reproduction is required only within the reported interval. Hardware, CUDA builds and numerical kernels are potentially result-affecting and are never assumed interchangeable. The selected `execution_profile_id`, exact lock hash, CUDA and driver versions, and exact GPU identity MUST be recorded with every new scientific result. `params.environment.deterministic_backend` supports exactly the explicit mappings `cudnn_deterministic` → `torch.backends.cudnn.deterministic` and `cudnn_benchmark` → `torch.backends.cudnn.benchmark`; every other key MUST fail, and each assigned value MUST be read back and verified (AM-75, AM-83). *(verify: repeat-run test on each qualified profile plus an archived environment manifest; cross-profile qualification report; unit tests for both mappings, value verification, exact profile authentication and unsupported-key rejection)*
- **SR-13** — Every result row MUST record its `run_id`, `git_commit`, `git_dirty` and `config_hash`, and artifacts MUST be written under the directories in `params.artifacts`. *(verify: schema test on emitted CSV)*
- **SR-14** — The learned model MUST NOT exceed `params.learned_system.max_params_millions` million parameters. The binding justification is `params.learned_system.max_params_policy`: encoder, decoder and head together MUST NOT exceed the parameter count of `params.reference_classifier`, so the learned arm is never larger than the network scoring the classical arm. This replaces the original rationale — "keeping the RPi tier plausible" — which rested a Tier 1 constraint on a stretch tier that DEC-14 makes explicit upside and HR-5 forbids any Tier 1 requirement from depending on. The numbers happen to coincide: a ResNet-18 at ten classes is about 11.2M parameters, so the 10M cap is already below its own comparator and the re-justification costs nothing (AM-36). *(verify: unit test on parameter count, asserting both the absolute cap and that it does not exceed the measured reference-classifier count)*
- **SR-15** — The downstream task head MUST sit behind a registry so a different task can be added without touching the encoder, channel or training loop (FW-4). *(verify: unit test registering a stub task head)*
- **SR-16** — Peak-to-average power ratio MUST be measured and reported per `params.learned_system.papr_report_required` for **all three systems**, not the learned one alone — adaptive 16-QAM is not constant modulus either, so reporting the learned arm's PAPR in isolation invites the reader to assume the digital arms are at 0 dB. A peak-power/clipping constraint MUST be available per `params.learned_system.peak_power_constraint_available`, and per `params.learned_system.papr_constrained_variant_required` a PAPR-constrained learned variant MUST be trained and reported as a secondary curve at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` over `params.learned_system.papr_constrained_variant_seeds` seed. Rationale: an average-power constraint lets a learned encoder buy SNR with peaky symbols that a constant-modulus scheme cannot use and a real amplifier cannot deliver; unreported, this is an unearned advantage in simulation and a Tier 2 discrepancy on hardware. Disclosure alone answers "did you know?" but not "is the comparison fair?", and this is the first objection a reader with a communications background reaches for — one extra training run converts it into a figure this project produced itself (AM-44). Reported PAPR is the **symbol-domain** peak-to-average ratio; oversampled waveform PAPR after pulse shaping is a different and larger quantity, and the two MUST NOT be conflated in the report. *(verify: PAPR logged in every result row for every system, a unit test that the clipping constraint bounds measured PAPR, and the constrained variant present as a distinct `system` value)*
- **SR-17** — A validation split MUST be carved per `params.evaluation.split_rule` from each dataset's `val_images` count and used for **every** selection decision — baseline configuration, λ, training checkpoint, operating ratio, architecture (DEC-12). The test split MUST NOT be read by any selection code path. **A seed is not a portable split specification (AM-58).** Loader ordering and library behaviour can change between versions, so the split MUST be materialised as a committed manifest under `params.datasets.manifest_dir` in `params.datasets.manifest_format`, listing every `params.datasets.stable_sample_id_rule` identifier with its label and split assignment, and hashed into the run metadata. **The hashed bytes are the exact original per-sample payload bytes before image decode, colour conversion or preprocessing, not the canonical RGB pixels (AM-71);** for Imagenette-160 they are the exact encoded JPEG file bytes; for STL-10 the exact per-image record from `train_X.bin` or `test_X.bin` before Torchvision's axis transpose or PIL conversion; and for CIFAR-10 the exact 3,072-byte CHW record from the batch payload before HWC or PIL conversion. A path, member name, position, label, archive, whole container file/batch, PIL serialization, decoded RGB or canonical/resized pixels MUST NOT enter that payload hash. **Manifest bytes are canonical (AM-77):** `params.datasets.manifest_encoding` UTF-8, `params.datasets.manifest_newline` LF, exactly one `params.datasets.manifest_columns` header, decimal zero-based labels, only `params.datasets.manifest_splits`, rows globally sorted per `params.datasets.manifest_sort`, no path or source index, and SHA-256 per `params.datasets.manifest_hash` over the exact committed CSV bytes; duplicate stable IDs within a dataset fail per `params.datasets.manifest_duplicate_id_policy`. The carve MUST be stratified per `params.datasets.split_carve_stratified`, ordered per `params.datasets.split_pre_shuffle_order` before drawing, and drawn without replacement per `params.datasets.split_draw` using exactly one `params.datasets.split_rng` generator seeded by `params.evaluation.split_seed`, iterating labels ascending — an unordered starting point makes the seed reproduce nothing. Class indices come from the dataset-specific authoritative metadata named by `params.datasets.class_index_source`: sorted authoritative class directory identifiers for Imagenette, `class_names.txt` for STL-10 and `batches.meta` label names for CIFAR-10, each mapped deterministically to contiguous zero-based labels rather than inherited from arbitrary loader enumeration. *(verify: unit test asserting split disjointness, exact counts and stratification against `params.datasets`; reversed/shuffled-enumeration invariance; exact canonical CSV bytes; duplicate/malformed-row failures; a test that every committed `manifest_sha256` reproduces and remains unchanged when preprocessing parameters change; a test that each authoritative class-to-index mapping matches the manifest; and an instrumentation audit that provenance-only test enumeration makes zero decoder, canonicalization, preprocessing, model-facing loader, inference and accuracy calls)*
- **SR-18** — Every evaluation run MUST emit a per-image outcome file matching `params.artifacts.per_image_schema` under `params.artifacts.per_image_dir`. **Identity and pairing are separate keys and MUST NOT be conflated (AM-58).** `run_id` identifies *one system's run* and MUST be unique per `params.artifacts.run_id_key`, formed per `params.artifacts.run_id_form`; `params.artifacts.pair_id_key` builds a **system-independent** `pair_id` that both arms of a comparison share, excluding everything in `params.artifacts.pair_id_excludes`; `params.artifacts.noise_id_key` identifies the channel realisation a row was drawn under; and `params.artifacts.analysis_cell_id_key` names the compound replicate. `dataset_version` is derived from the dataset archive SHA-256 and `analysis_version` is the explicitly bumped value under `params.config` — bumped to 2 by AM-81, whose redefinition of the BR-11 byte columns and their aggregate denominator is an analysis-implementation change under `params.config.analysis_version_bump_rule`; `config_hash` and `checkpoint_id` remain runtime-computed by design (AM-69). ER-10 joins on `pair_id`, so one learned per-image row participates in both the learned-versus-classical and the learned-versus-ER-9 comparisons without being duplicated. This replaces "ER-10 pairs per-image outcomes across systems at matched (image, SNR, seed) *entirely through `run_id`*" (AM-37), which was unsound in both directions: `run_id_key` contains `system`, so the two arms never shared an ID, while it omitted `split`, the config and checkpoint hashes, the classifier variant and every system-specific setting — so a validation run and a test run, or the two BR-12 classifier variants, or two cells of ER-9's width search, **collided on the same ID**. `params.artifacts.rng_stream` requires counter-based keyed streams over `params.artifacts.rng_purposes`: a shared integer seed with conditionally consumed draws does not guarantee common noise across systems whose control flow differs, which is exactly what happens when one system outages on an image and another does not. **Each purpose accepts exactly its declared identity and rejects missing or extra fields (AM-78):** `channel_noise` takes `noise_id`; `outage_label` takes `split_manifest_hash`, `stable_sample_id`, `channel_seed`; `augmentation` takes `stable_sample_id`, `train_seed`, `epoch`; `init` takes `train_seed`, `component_path`, where `component_path` is a stable model-qualified name under `params.artifacts.init_component_path_rule`; and `batch_order` takes `train_seed`, `epoch`. *(verify: schema test; a test that recomputing `top1_acc` from the per-image file matches the aggregate row; a test that no two result rows share a `run_id` with differing `params.artifacts.run_id_key` values; a test that row reordering and batching change no `noise_id` and no draw; a test that every RNG purpose accepts exactly its declared identity and rejects missing/extra fields; a test that two systems in one comparison cell share `pair_id` and `noise_id` while holding distinct `run_id`s; a test that changing split, checkpoint or config always changes `run_id`; a test that changing system never changes `pair_id`; and a test that the ER-10 join is one-to-one and rejects missing or duplicated trajectories)*
- **SR-19** — One canonical image MUST be defined per `params.preprocessing` and produced **before** either pipeline sees the data: `canonical_image`, `resize_interpolation`, `antialias`, `train_crop`, `eval_crop`, `colour_space`, `bit_depth` and `tensor_range`, with `eval_augmentation_permitted` false and `channel_normalisation` applied inside the model rather than in the pipeline. **Every free parameter of that pipeline is now fixed rather than left to a library default (AM-58),** because each one changes a reported number and none of them were specified: `params.preprocessing.train_crop_scale` and `train_crop_ratio` for the random resized crop, `train_hflip_p` for the flip, `channel_mean` and `channel_std` for the in-model normalisation, and `params.preprocessing.canonical_and_augmentation_are_separate_stages` settling that canonicalisation and training augmentation are two stages rather than one fused transform — which is what makes "the codec compresses the same pixels the encoder receives" checkable instead of merely asserted. The codec's own resampling is fixed by `params.preprocessing.codec_downsample_interpolation`, `codec_upsample_interpolation` and `codec_resize_preserves_aspect`, so the receiver-side upsample is not an implementer's choice inside a comparison BR-3 depends on. **Source binding is structural (AM-74):** `canonicalize_source(source_bytes, dataset)` is the sole supported factory, decoding occurs only through a private data-layer registry, and the public `CanonicalProduct` is a read-only protocol backed by an unexported implementation; production code has no supported product constructor or decoded-pixel injection path. Real dataset decoders are registered only with the later loader batch; tests may inject a private fixture decoder. **This requirement is accepted at G-1 and named in W1's schedule row**: it existed but was scheduled nowhere, so W1 data could have flowed before the canonical-pixel contract was accepted. The classical codec MUST compress `params.preprocessing.codec_input` — the same canonical pixels the learned encoder receives — so BR-3's equal-channel-uses claim is not quietly comparing two different images. Reconstructions MUST be clipped per `params.preprocessing.reconstruction_clipped_before_metrics` before scoring. PSNR MUST use `params.preprocessing.psnr_data_range`; SSIM MUST call `params.preprocessing.ssim_impl` with every public result-affecting argument explicit: that same `data_range`, `ssim_channel_axis`, `ssim_gaussian_weights`, `ssim_sigma`, `ssim_k1`, `ssim_k2`, and `ssim_use_sample_covariance` (AM-74). Scikit-image 0.26.0's effective Gaussian support is recorded as `params.preprocessing.ssim_effective_gaussian_support_samples` and bound by a numerical fixture; code MUST NOT pass undocumented `truncate` or claim explicit `win_size` control. Rationale: none of this was specified anywhere, and every item on the list changes a reported number — an unclipped reconstruction inflates PSNR, a mismatched `data_range` shifts it by decibels, and a codec fed differently-resized pixels from the encoder makes the whole comparison invalid rather than merely noisy (AM-28). *(verify: AST/import-boundary test proving production products come only from `canonicalize_source`; unit test proving source bytes reach only the registry-selected decoder and the canonical tensor is bit-identical on codec and encoder paths; analytical PSNR, clipping, identical-image SSIM and fixed Gaussian-SSIM numerical fixtures)*
- **SR-20** — **Dataset provenance MUST be pinned, not assumed.** Each dataset entry in `params.datasets` MUST name its `loader`, `loader_size_arg`, normative `source_url`, `archive_filename`, exact `archive_bytes`, `archive_sha256`, `manifest_filename`, `manifest_sha256`, `source_payload`, `class_index_source`, `licence` and `published_val_becomes`; archives live under `params.datasets.archive_dir`, verified extractions under `params.datasets.extracted_dir`, and neither is committed. The exact configured URL MUST be requested; a different Torchvision URL or mirror is not a silent substitute. SHA-256 and byte length MUST be verified before extraction or any sample use and recorded in run metadata. Rationale: the split counts and seed alone do not identify the data — a re-released archive, a different loader version or a changed class ordering all reproduce the counts while changing the images behind them, and every accuracy in this project is denominated in that data. Two corrections to previously held beliefs, both checkable in minutes and both wrong (AM-58): **`torchvision` does ship an Imagenette loader**, so `params.datasets.imagenette160.loader` uses it at `loader_size_arg` rather than a hand-rolled fetch, and the working note claiming otherwise was stale; and the checksums MUST be this project's own recorded values rather than an assumption that the loader verifies integrity, which its documentation does not state. No pending archive or manifest checksum may authorize dataset use; first bootstrap may only fetch and measure archives until the measured values have been written into this specification, regenerated and verified on a second pass (AM-77). *(verify: integrity tests asserting each configured archive's exact URL, filename, byte length and SHA-256 before use; mismatch and pending-value failures; a test that every dataset instantiates through the one registry path of SR-2; and a G-1 acceptance check that no archive or manifest provenance remains pending)*
- **SR-21** — **Each execution profile environment MUST be reproducible within its declared provisioning boundary (AM-75, AM-83).** The legacy scalar fields under `params.environment` remain the exact `local_4060_cu130` identity for old consumers and historical artifacts; `params.environment.execution_profiles` is the additive registry for all new science and fixes each profile's Python dependencies, CUDA wheel index, lock source/file and exact lock SHA-256. Those hashed locks reproduce Python packages. OpenJPEG remains outside that boundary: version `2.5.4` is externally provisioned per `params.environment.openjpeg_provisioning` and verified at load, not installed or hashed by this repository. Run metadata MUST record `openjpeg_version` as `null` when unavailable and irrelevant, or exact when used; any J2K-dependent path MUST validate it before creating artifacts. Installation MUST assert `params.environment.cuda_assertion`, and new science MUST additionally authenticate every field applicable from `params.environment.execution_profile_record_fields`; a cu126 environment claiming cu130, a wrong Torch/Sionna/lock version, or a wrong GPU identity MUST fail closed. The CPU-only path and the resolver/hash/index/tooling constraints from AM-65, AM-66, AM-67 and AM-73 remain unchanged. *(verify: clean hashed installation of each eligible profile; profile-identity mutation tests for lock, Torch, CUDA, Sionna, GPU class and UUID; existing CPU-lock verifier; OpenJPEG preflight tests)*
- **SR-23** — **Execution-profile selection is scientific provenance, not opportunistic scheduling (AM-83, AM-86).** Every new experiment, sweep, characterization campaign, training run or evaluation freezes exactly one eligible profile at the time and scope in `params.compute.execution_profile_policy`; no host may silently continue that task on another profile. An interruption can move only through explicit supersession or a new run. A competing hyperparameter sweep uses one profile throughout; architecture alternatives use one profile or a preregistered balanced mapping; one training run never crosses profiles; multi-seed final training uses one profile preferably, or a preregistered seed-to-profile mapping applied identically to every compared system; and the complete paired final test campaign uses one frozen profile unless a balanced design was registered before test access. The selected profile's host is the sole scientific writer for that task, and live shared scientific filesystems and concurrent cross-host mutation are forbidden. The final or owner-selected publication handoff follows authenticated verify/reconcile/commit/push/fetch custody. Per `params.environment.scientific_writer_authentication`, authenticated GitHub HTTPS write access, internal artifact hashes/contracts, durable commits, successful pushes and local/remote commit parity are mandatory at every actual Git publication and at final handoff; Git commit signing is optional prospectively. **Narrow Pascal-successor custody exception:** for the owner-authorized BR-17 `confessor_pascal_cu126` G8_C successor only, immutable authenticated per-unit evidence may accumulate continuously in its separate mutable runtime on the sole scientific writer, and Git publication may be deferred until the unattended campaign exits or until an owner-selected manual checkpoint. Loss before Git publication is an explicitly accepted custody risk and does not make unauthenticated or missing bytes valid; scientific validity remains determined by the surviving campaign-bound request/result/state bytes and final complete-coverage verification. Before BLER-table freeze or any handoff to another phase, the complete accepted runtime MUST still be reconciled, committed, pushed by authenticated HTTPS and proved at local/remote parity. This exception changes publication cadence only: it permits no second writer, profile switch, weakened hash/contract, old-result ingest, fabricated recovery or later-phase access. Historical signing requirements, signed commits and signing incidents remain historical facts and are not rewritten. Qualification is explicitly non-scientific per `params.environment.qualification`, contributes zero campaign coverage, uses no test data, and must test each device plus cross-profile RNG, paired numerical and relevant OpenJPEG parity before a profile becomes eligible. *(verify: profile-selection/freeze and mid-run-switch rejection tests; balanced-mapping tests; writer/authentication contract tests; Pascal production contract and mutation tests binding continuous local custody, owner-selected publication timing, accepted prepublication-loss risk and mandatory final publication/parity; generic qualification verifier and mutation tests; AST/test-access guard remains zero)*
- **SR-22** — **Test access MUST be guarded by a committed freeze manifest.** No model-facing test sample may be loaded until `params.artifacts.freeze_manifest_file` exists and covers `params.evaluation.freeze_manifest_covers`; the guard MUST make the load *fail*, not warn. `params.evaluation.test_access_gate` names the gate that releases it. **The sole pre-G-12 exception is the AM-77 provenance-only scan:** it may verify the archive, read each original test payload solely to compute its stable ID, read the published label, and write or verify the committed split manifest. It MUST NOT decode a test image, call `canonicalize_source`, construct a `CanonicalProduct`, apply preprocessing, expose a model-facing test dataset, display/log/save pixels, run inference or accuracy, or influence any selection/configuration. Ordinary registry loading supports train/validation only and MUST point test callers back to this guard; `src/data/test_access.py` remains the sole guarded model-facing test boundary. Rationale: DEC-12's rule is enforced by a structural guard rather than by discipline, because the failure it prevents is invisible after the fact — a curve seen early cannot be unseen, and no audit of the finished repository can distinguish a configuration chosen before test from one chosen after. If a genuine code defect is discovered after test opens, the affected campaign MUST be recorded as invalidated and **rerun complete**; selectively replacing favourable or unfavourable cells is prohibited under ER-8 (AM-58). *(verify: a test that loading a test sample without a valid freeze manifest raises; the existing AST guard that no other production module imports `data.test_access`; instrumentation proving manifest construction over published test payloads makes zero registered-decoder, canonicalization, preprocessing, model-facing loader, classifier and inference calls; a CI check demonstrating that G-1 has zero test decoder, canonicalization and inference calls and the W10 rehearsal has zero model-facing test-loader reads; and an archived freeze manifest whose hashes resolve to committed code, config, manifests and checkpoints)*

## 6. Baseline requirements (BR)

The baseline exists to be beaten honestly. Every requirement here is a defence against an unfair comparison.

- **BR-1** — The classical chain MUST be `params.baseline.source_codec` → `params.baseline.channel_code` → modulation → the same channel implementation the learned system uses. The codec MUST emit `params.baseline.j2k_container` and MUST be driven per `params.baseline.j2k_rate_control` via `params.baseline.j2k_rate_control_method`: the encoder is asked for a compression ratio, the emitted file is measured, and per `params.baseline.j2k_emitted_size_authoritative` **that measured size — not the requested ratio — is what counts against the budget**. The requirement is a codestream that does not exceed the payload budget, never one that exactly equals it; residual slack MUST be carried as filler and reported, not discarded (AM-8). **The codec configuration is frozen here rather than left to defaults (AM-51),** because BR-4's codec-rate axis is the outer loop of the largest cached artifact in the project and was previously unspecified in every respect: `params.baseline.j2k_impl` at `params.baseline.j2k_impl_version` driven through `params.baseline.j2k_binding`, with `params.baseline.j2k_wavelet`, `params.baseline.j2k_progression_order`, `params.baseline.j2k_resolutions`, `params.baseline.j2k_code_block_size` and `params.baseline.j2k_tile_size` fixed. The search is `params.baseline.j2k_search_method` within `params.baseline.j2k_search_bounds`, to `params.baseline.j2k_search_tolerance_bytes` and at most `params.baseline.j2k_search_max_iters` iterations, with `params.baseline.j2k_nonmonotone_policy` covering the case where emitted size does not fall monotonically with the requested ratio. **The search MUST be cached by `params.baseline.j2k_cache_key`**, which is content-addressed over the canonical pixels *and* the complete codec configuration, so the cache is reusable across SNRs, seeds and every system sharing that budget; without it a naive implementation re-encodes on the order of 200,000 times in ER-1 alone, and the binding exists so that cost is not paid as process spawns. **The key was `[image_hash, budget_bytes]` and that was insufficient (AM-58):** emitted bytes also depend on the encode resolution, the codec flags, the library version and the search settings, so two different configurations could collide on one cache entry and silently serve the wrong codestream — a cache poisoning that would show up as an inexplicable baseline result rather than as an error. A **downsampling axis** `params.baseline.downsample_axis_px` is selected per `params.baseline.downsample_selection`: low-bitrate classical codecs often gain substantially from encoding a smaller image and upsampling at the receiver, and withholding that from the baseline would be exactly the artificial handicap DEC-16's governing rule forbids. **The axis is now dataset-specific (AM-58)**, because a single `[160, 128, 96, 64]` list *upscaled* STL-10 at two of its four rungs and offered CIFAR-10 no native resolution at all; **CIFAR-10's ladder is now the single native 32 px rung (AM-80)**, because a flat `params.baseline.j2k_resolutions` of 6 requires every tile dimension to be at least `2**5 = 32` px, so the 24 px and 16 px rungs were invalid codec configurations that OpenJPEG rejects before rate control rather than useful low-rate candidates; `params.baseline.downsample_axis_never_upscales` states the invariant, since encoding an upscaled image spends bits on interpolated detail and nothing is "downsampling" that makes the image larger. The JPEG secondary curve is likewise pinned by `params.baseline.jpeg_impl`, `params.baseline.jpeg_chroma_subsampling` and `params.baseline.jpeg_optimise_huffman`. `params.baseline.source_codec_secondary` MUST be reported as a labelled second curve, not substituted for the first (DEC-9), and it is sufficient to report it at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` for one entry in `params.evaluation.train_seeds` — it is context for what practitioners deploy, not a hypothesis-bearing curve (AM-13). It previously pointed at the retired core_ratio, which after AM-20 was selected by no gate at all (AM-26). *(verify: integration test asserting the shared channel object; a test that every emitted codestream is ≤ its budget with the slack logged; and both codec curves present in the results CSV)*
- **BR-2** — The LDPC chain MUST be validated two ways before any learned-vs-classical comparison is reported. **Bit-exactly**: encoder output and rate-matched output MUST reproduce `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_file`, a fixture generated from an implementation independent of `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` whose provenance is fixed at `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_gate` by descending `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_ladder` until one rung is confirmed available. The ladder exists because the MATLAB licence is being attempted but is **not assured** (AM-22), and BR-2 gates G-2 which gates every reported comparison, so a single-sourced fixture would put the whole comparison behind a licence decision the project does not control. **Rung 2 is confirmed and demonstrated at W0 (AM-25),** and `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_rung` records it as the rung in use. Two corrections to what this requirement previously assumed. First, srsRAN does **not** commit its vector data to git: the `.dat` files are published per release as `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_asset`, and `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_url` pins the immutable release named by `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_release`. Second, the licence question is answered by **not vendoring**: `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_vendored` is false, so the fixture is built by a committed fetch-and-convert script that verifies `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_sha256` and writes `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_file`, which stays out of git. Checksums are facts about a file rather than a copy of it, so this keeps byte-exact reproducibility without redistributing AGPLv3 data in a submitted artifact, and `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_licence_check_required` is thereby satisfied rather than deferred. Because that path needs the network, `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_offline_floor` MUST also be committed and MUST run unconditionally: a hand-derived small-lifting-size case, wholly this project's own work, so the bit-exact test never silently skips when a fetch fails or upstream disappears. The rung actually used MUST be recorded with the fixture. **The comparison is not bit-for-bit naive, and the alignment MUST be implemented as follows** — established by running it at W0, where a wrong alignment agreed with the reference at 0.50, which is chance and is exactly what a broken fixture would look like: srsRAN stores the codeword with the first 2Z systematic bits **already punctured** (BG1's codeword is 68Z, the stored buffer is 66Z), and marks filler positions with the byte 254, whereas `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` deletes filler positions *before* puncturing. Aligning therefore means dropping the 254-marked positions and nothing else, then truncating to a length whose rate stays above the base graph's minimum, then pinning the lifting size rather than letting the library infer it from (k, n). With that alignment the two agree **exactly**, on every case where the comparison is structurally valid. **Statistically**: measured BER/BLER MUST match published `params.baseline.ldpc_standard` curves **for every entry in `params.baseline.modulations` that BR-4 may select**, with every reference curve converted to this project's SNR convention using `params.channel.snr_conversion` and the conversion shown in the archived artifact. Higher-order modulations need soft-demapping to log-likelihood ratios rather than a sign decision, so an unvalidated 16-QAM demapper would silently degrade the baseline — the opposite of BR-4's intent and a direct threat to DEC-16. Rationale: a curve overlay is a judgement call and a fixture is a test, so the golden vectors are what turn G-2 into a red/green signal; and separately, a missed Eb/N0-to-Es/N0 conversion shifts rate 1/3 and rate 5/6 by about 4 dB *relative to each other*, which inverts BR-4's rate selection rather than merely offsetting the curve. *(verify: passing golden-vector test, plus an archived BER-vs-SNR plot with reference curve overlaid and the conversion arithmetic stated)*
- **BR-3** — The baseline MUST receive exactly the same number of complex channel uses `k` as the learned system, per `params.baseline.budget_rule`. Bandwidth matching is counted in channel uses, not in bytes. *(verify: unit test counting emitted symbols for both systems)*
- **BR-4** — At each SNR the baseline MUST be tuned in its own favour — sweeping codec target rate (or `params.baseline.jpeg_quality_grid` for the secondary curve) × `params.baseline.ldpc_rates` × `params.baseline.modulations` per `params.baseline.modulation_tuning`, and taking the **best feasible** configuration per `params.baseline.tuning` — with the selection made **on the validation split** and then frozen for the test evaluation (DEC-12). Modulation is an adaptive axis rather than a fixed choice (DEC-16): capping it would leave the baseline unable to exploit a clean link, which is an artificial handicap rather than a fair comparison. Reporting a single fixed configuration across all SNRs is prohibited. The sweep MUST be computed as a cached feasibility table plus per-(rate, SNR, blocklength) block-error characterisation composed analytically, not as a full per-image channel simulation of every cell. **The composition function is written down here rather than left to the implementer (AM-51),** since every selection in the project flows through it. Under a transport-block CRC one failed code block kills the transport block, so P(TB success) is the product over code blocks of (1 − BLER(E_r, K_r, SNR, modulation)), and expected accuracy at an SNR is P(TB success) × acc_clean(codec configuration) + (1 − P(TB success)) × acc_outage, where the outage term follows BR-13 and MUST use the **measured validation accuracy of the frozen constant class** rather than `1/classes` — the two are not equal in general, and assuming the lower one understates the digital arms, which is the direction ER-8 forbids (AM-58). That makes `acc_clean` at each candidate codec configuration a **required measured input** — the classifier's accuracy on the validation split at each cached quality — which is a real artifact and MUST be produced rather than assumed. Block-error rates are characterised over `params.baseline.bler_characterisation_trials` trials per cell. **Selection runs in two passes and then stops (AM-54):** pass one selects under BR-8's clean-trained classifier, which is the only one that exists at that point; BR-12 then trains on the deterministic balanced sample from the complete authority-projected support fixed by `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_corpus`: pass-one references at or below the training SNR anchor only the already-required dataset/ratio scope, the complete candidate-authority projection supplies breadth without reading winner score, rank or margin, and the frozen sampler assigns `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_variants_per_training_image` distinct supported qualities to every training stable ID without using any observed outcome; BR-12 re-scores the **cached** sweep table and re-selects once; and iteration terminates there, per `params.reference_classifier.br4_selection_terminates_after_pass` (AM-87, AM-88). Rationale: selecting under a classifier that is then replaced leaves the baseline tuned for a scorer nobody reports, and because the artifact-finetuned classifier tolerates heavier compression it would plausibly prefer a different point — spending the savings on stronger channel coding. Reporting the classical arm at a configuration chosen by the weaker scorer understates it, which is the direction ER-8 and DEC-16's governing rule both forbid. The second pass is cheap because `params.baseline.j2k_cache_key` makes it classifier forward passes over already-emitted codestreams rather than a re-encode, and terminating at two passes is preregistered so the loop cannot be run until it flatters a result. **Analytic composition is for selection only.** Once the per-SNR configuration is frozen, the reported test evaluation MUST be an actual per-image channel simulation sharing BR-7's noise draws, because BR-7 requires identical noise realisations across systems and ER-10 requires per-image paired outcomes — neither of which an analytic block-error rate can supply (AM-16). *(verify: sweep artifact showing the selected config per SNR, its provenance on the validation split, and the composition method; plus a test that every row feeding ER-10 carries a per-image outcome traceable to a simulated noise draw)*
- **BR-5** — If no codec configuration produces a file fitting the payload budget, the transmission MUST be recorded as infeasible, counted in `infeasible_rate`, and scored per BR-13, never silently skipped. Two distinct causes MUST be distinguished in `outage_reason`, because they are different findings: **codec infeasibility**, where a transport block exists but no codestream fits inside it, and **structural infeasibility**, where the channel budget cannot carry `params.baseline.tb_crc` plus a single payload byte, so there is no transport block to fill. The second is not hypothetical — adding `r_1_48` created one such cell, on the smoke dataset at BPSK rate 1/3, where a 21-bit budget leaves 5 bits after the CRC (AM-41, recomputed under AM-49's conditional CRC, which rescued the second of the two cells the unconditional 24-bit CRC had starved). A configuration in that state MUST be reported as structurally unavailable rather than as a codec failure, since reporting it as the latter would attribute a packetisation limit to JPEG 2000. *(verify: unit test at the smallest ratio where codec infeasibility is expected, plus a test asserting that a budget below the transport-block CRC is reported as structurally infeasible and never reaches the codec)*
- **BR-6** — A file that cannot be decoded after LDPC decoding MUST be scored per BR-13 and counted in `decode_failure_rate`. *(verify: unit test injecting an undecodable block)*
- **BR-7** — Both systems MUST see identical test images and identical noise realisations at a given seed and SNR. *(verify: test asserting bitwise-identical noise draws across the two pipelines)*
- **BR-8** — A frozen `params.reference_classifier` trained from scratch on clean images (DEC-15) MUST meet the headline dataset's `clean_acc_floor`, and the same instance MUST score every system in `params.reference_classifier.shared_by` — the classical reconstructions and the semantic reconstruction ablation (ER-4). It MUST NOT score ER-9, whose input is a feature stream rather than an image (AM-5). The smoke dataset's `clean_acc_floor` is advisory: DEC-1 makes CIFAR-10 a plumbing path, nothing in §2 depends on its accuracy, and gating the schedule on it buys risk for no result (AM-13). Its training recipe is `params.reference_classifier.{optimizer, lr, momentum, weight_decay, lr_schedule, lr_warmup_epochs, epochs, batch_size, label_smoothing, augmentation}` and MUST be read from config, not chosen in source: this classifier gates G-1, defines the "clean-image accuracy" that is the denominator of ER-3's entire selection rule, and scores one whole arm of the headline comparison, yet it carried no recipe at all — a direct SR-1 violation, and 0.88 from scratch on 8469 images is recipe-dependent rather than what a default loop returns (AM-27). *(verify: measured clean accuracy per dataset, archived, with a training log showing no pretrained initialisation, and a test that every element of the recipe is config-derived)*
- **BR-9** — The headline classical curve is the adaptive one produced by BR-4 over `params.baseline.modulations`. `params.baseline.core_modulation` defines the **fixed-modulation reference curve**, which MUST also be reported so the contribution of adaptive modulation is separable, and which is the like-for-like comparator for ER-9. It is **not** a cap on ER-9: ER-9's transport is tuned by BR-4's rule per `params.digital_semantic_control.transport_tuning`, over the same `params.baseline.modulations` and `params.baseline.ldpc_rates` the baseline may select. Rationale: ER-9 exists to *deny* the learned system credit for joint coding under H4, so pinning it to one modulation while the classical baseline may drop to BPSK at the noisy end — roughly 3 dB of reach, inside H1's region — would handicap the control in the one direction that makes H4 easier to support. That is the failure mode DEC-16's governing rule and ER-8 exist to prevent, and it applies to the control exactly as it applies to the baseline (AM-15). *(verify: config test, plus the adaptive classical curve, the fixed-modulation classical curve and ER-9's selected transport config per SNR all present in the results CSV)*
- **BR-10** — Bit accounting MUST be complete and normative: `params.baseline.tb_crc`, `params.baseline.cb_crc_bits`, code-block segmentation at `params.baseline.code_block_max_bits`, per-code-block rate-matching budget distribution (TS 38.212 §5.4.2.1 `E_r`), base-graph selection per `params.baseline.ldpc_base_graph`, and filler and rate matching per `params.baseline.rate_matching` all consume budget before any source byte does, exactly as in `params.baseline.budget_rule`. Partial final blocks and padding MUST be accounted, not discarded. The steps in `params.baseline.ldpc_impl_local` are implemented in this project rather than taken from `params.baseline.ldpc_impl`, which does not provide them (DEC-10). Rationale: without this the "equal channel uses" claim is disputable and the payload figure is wrong by a variable margin. Segmentation is unavoidable rather than optional at these budgets — Imagenette at the headline ratio gives k=25600 symbols → 51200 channel bits → 42,624 information bits at rate 5/6, which against `code_block_max_bits` 8448 and a 24-bit code-block CRC is six code blocks; even the low-ratio operating point needs two. **Three structural rules the solver MUST obey, all of them corrections (AM-58).** *(i)* `params.baseline.payload_solver` chooses the largest **byte-aligned** A, with `params.baseline.payload_solver_clamp` naming the only permitted escape, so the optimum is unique and `source_bytes` is exactly `A/8` rather than a floor with a bit remainder. *(ii)* Per `params.baseline.segmentation_requires_exact_division`, `K' = B'/C` is an **exact** division: TS 38.212 §5.2.2's own procedure assumes it, and NR obtains it upstream from the TS 38.214 transport-block-size quantisation, which this project does not use because it supplies arbitrary source sizes. A configuration whose `B'` is not divisible by `C` is therefore not a legal transport block here and MUST be excluded by the solver, never rescued with `ceil` — rounding leaves `C·K' > B'` and the surplus bits accounted nowhere, in a requirement whose whole purpose is complete accounting. *(iii)* Filler is `K − K'` per block and `C·(K − K')` in total, where `K` comes from `params.baseline.systematic_length_rule` — **22Z on BG1 and 10Z on BG2**, independent of which `K_b` selected the lifting size. Both the per-block and the total figure MUST be recorded, because a segmented transport block's total filler is not its per-block filler. **Verified arithmetically across the whole grid (AM-23, corrected and broadened by AM-58):** the §5.2.2 segmentation and §5.4.2.1 `E_r` allocation are computed against `params.generated.yaml` for **every ladder ratio on both headline datasets — 144 configurations, not the 72 that today's three provisional selections happen to name** — and every one distributes the channel budget with **exactly zero slack**, with every `E_r` a valid multiple of the modulation order. Broadening the obligation is not cosmetic: ER-3 may select any rung at G-8, so a proof scoped to the provisional selections proves nothing about the configuration actually used. The canonical case reproduces this requirement's worked example: A = 42,624 bits (5,328 source bytes), B = 42,648, C = 6, B' = 42,792, K' = 7,132, Z = 352, K = 7,744, filler 612 per block and 3,672 in total, E = [8532, 8532, 8534, 8534, 8534, 8534] summing to 51 200. Worth preserving because it is the trap: a naive `floor(G/C)` split yields 8533, which is **odd** and therefore not a legal `E_r` at all, since TS 38.212 requires a multiple of `N_L·Q_m`. **Confirmed against the library at W0 (AM-24), with the predicate corrected (AM-58):** the info-bit budget MUST be clamped up per `params.baseline.payload_solver_clamp` whenever the nominal payload would put a code block below its base graph's minimum rate. The predicate is `params.baseline.min_coderate_predicate` — checked on the **worst** block, `K'/max(E_r)`, and with **equality accepted**. Both halves were wrong before. Checking `K_r/E[0]` tested the *smallest* `E_r`, hence the *highest* per-block rate, so the floor was evaluated on the block least likely to violate it; the standard's constraint binds on every block, and the library enforces it per codeword. And the comparison was strict, where `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` raises only on `r < floor`: BG1's mother code is 22Z systematic over 66Z transmitted, which is exactly 1/3, so rate 1/3 is legal and the strict test manufactured three clamps the library never required. Correcting both reduces the recorded clamps from **six to three** — all on Imagenette, all at nominal rate 1/3, landing at 0.333125 against a limit of 0.333333, and one of them at `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point`, so it is not a corner case. The three that disappear are STL-10 configurations sitting at exactly 1/3. The clamp moves the budget by one byte and is therefore below any effect this project measures; what it does is make those configurations realizable at all. **Three conformance corrections (AM-49), none of which move a measured number but all of which the accounting claim depends on.** *(a)* The transport-block CRC is **conditional**, per `params.baseline.tb_crc`: TS 38.212 §7.2.1 uses CRC24A only when the payload exceeds `threshold_payload_bits` and CRC16 below it, where this specification carried an unconditional 24. Recomputed across the grid, 17 of the 72 live configurations fall below the threshold and are entitled to CRC16 — a byte of extra payload each, changing no segmentation and no `E_r`. *(b)* `params.baseline.code_block_max_bits` is **per base graph** — 8448 on BG1 and 3840 on BG2 — where a single 8448 was applied unconditionally; 14 of the 72 live configurations select BG2 under the standard. That this changed no segmentation is luck rather than design: every BG2 configuration here is small enough for one code block under either limit. *(c)* Base-graph selection is a property of the **transport block**, decided from (A, R) per `params.baseline.base_graph_selection_rule` *before* segmentation, and then applied to every code block. The W0 spike derived it *after* segmentation from the per-code-block rate, which is also what `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` does internally when left to infer — so `params.baseline.base_graph_pinned_at_seam` requires the graph to be selected here and passed explicitly through the BR-14 seam rather than inferred per codeword. `params.baseline.bg2_min_coderate` is recorded alongside `params.baseline.ldpc_bg1_min_coderate` because BG2 reaches 1/5 where BG1 stops at 1/3, and which floor applies now depends on a selection made before segmentation rather than after. AM-24's BG1 clamp is unaffected in kind: every clamped configuration has A above the CRC threshold and a rate above 0.25, so BG1 is correct for them under the standard's own rule — though AM-58 reduced their count from six to three. **Control plane (AM-43):** `params.baseline.control_plane_policy` declares modulation, code rate, payload length and quantiser width to be signalled out of band and excluded from the budget for **every** system. An adaptive receiver cannot decode without them, so leaving them unstated while claiming complete accounting is the one option that cannot be defended; excluding them symmetrically favours the classical baseline, which re-tunes per SNR and needs the signalling, over the learned system, which is frozen and needs none — the permitted direction under DEC-16's governing rule. *(verify: unit test reconciling emitted symbol count against k for several payload sizes including a single-block case, a partial final block, and a multi-block case; a test that the CRC width and base graph selected for each configuration match TS 38.212 §7.2.1 and §7.2.2; assertions over the whole grid that `A % 8 == 0`, `B' % C == 0`, `C·K' == B'`, `B' == A + TB CRC + per-block CRCs`, `K == 22Z` on BG1 and `10Z` on BG2, `filler_total == C·(K − K')`, `Σ E_r == G`, every `E_r % Q_m == 0`, `K'/E_r ≥ the configured floor for every block`, and that A is maximal under the nominal predicate or minimal under the named clamp; boundary vectors around A=292, A=3824, both `K_cb` values, base-graph switches, lifting-size switches, segmentation-count changes and `E_r` distribution changes; and an archived per-configuration record carrying A, source bytes, CRC type, base graph, lifting size, code-block count, `B'`, `K'`, `K`, every `E_r`, filler per block and in total, effective code rate, and the hashes of the params and script that produced it)*
- **BR-11** — Container and header bytes MUST count against the budget per `params.baseline.container_policy`, and every baseline result row MUST report `header_bytes` and `payload_bytes` separately so the fraction of the budget spent on format overhead is visible at every operating point. Rationale: at these budgets container overhead is a first-order term, and an uncontrolled overhead difference would let a low-SNR "cliff" be a file-format artifact rather than a channel effect (DEC-9). **The two columns were named but never defined arithmetically, and were implemented over a delivered-only denominator (AM-81).** `bytes_sent` equals `source_bytes` equals `A/8`: the complete byte-aligned transport payload capacity placed on the channel. For an emitted raw JPEG 2000 codestream, `header_bytes` MUST mean **all structural codestream bytes** — SOC, every main-header marker segment, every SOT marker segment, every tile-part header through and including SOD, EOC, and the equivalent structural bytes of every tile-part — and `payload_bytes` MUST mean **all tile-part data bytes after SOD and before the next tile-part boundary**. `payload_bytes` MUST NOT be described more narrowly as pure entropy-coded sample data, because tile-part data may also carry packet-header information. The required identity is `emitted_codestream_bytes = header_bytes + payload_bytes`, and transport filler `payload_filler_bytes = bytes_sent − emitted_codestream_bytes` MUST be reported separately rather than folded into either column. **Aggregate denominator:** both columns are means over every row that emitted a codestream — `delivered` **and** `decode_failure` — excluding `structural_infeasibility` and `codec_infeasibility`, where no codestream exists; they are null only when the aggregate contains no emitted codestream. A cell whose rows all failed to decode therefore still reports its overhead, which is the case where format overhead matters most and where a delivered-only denominator silently reported nothing. *(verify: schema test on the two columns; a known-answer codestream parser test over a handcrafted codestream with a known non-zero `Psot`, a multi-tile-part case and a `Psot = 0` case, asserting exact header, payload and total byte counts rather than only that they sum to the codestream length; a decode-failure-only aggregate that retains both byte columns; exact reconciliation of `header_bytes + payload_bytes == emitted_codestream_bytes` and `header_bytes + payload_bytes + payload_filler_bytes == bytes_sent`; and an archived overhead-fraction table per dataset × ratio × rate)*
- **BR-12** — A second `params.reference_classifier` instance, fine-tuned on codec-artifacted images, MUST also score the classical reconstructions, and both scores MUST be reported. It MUST be trained at `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_gate`, **not earlier**, on `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_corpus`, built from `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_corpus_split` images and fine-tuned by the fully config-derived `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_recipe`. **Before F2 optimizer step 1, the nested recipe is self-contained rather than silently inheriting output-affecting clean-training defaults: it initializes the distinct artifact-finetuned descendant from the exact frozen G1 best clean checkpoint, applies the configured keyed augmentation to exact authenticated F1 reconstruction pixels, freezes the warmup/cosine endpoint, assignment-row permutation, validation/checkpoint rule and epoch-boundary resume policy, and keeps test access prohibited (AM-89).** **The corpus split is named because leaving it open closed a loop (AM-59):** BR-4's pass-one configurations are chosen on validation, and if the artifact corpus were then built from those same validation images, the pass-two re-selection would be scoring configurations with a classifier that had trained on the images it is selecting over — training leaked into selection, on the artifact whose scores carry the headline. The corpus is therefore **training-split only**. **Its support breadth was corrected after pass one and before G8_F (AM-87), and its execution multiplicity was then reduced by a deterministic balanced sampler post-AM-87 and pre-F0 (AM-88); neither is represented as pre-pass-one preregistration.** The earlier `artifact_finetune_corpus` and `artifact_finetune_corpus_breadth` phrases named selected qualities plus a “preregistered feasible quality band” but fixed no quality identity, axis, endpoints, feasibility rule, projection, deduplication, PHY multiplicity or image-level infeasibility policy; the two phrases were also inconsistent whenever pass one selected a codec-infeasible outage candidate. Because pass-one results are now visible, choosing a narrow winner-centred width would be outcome-adaptive. The corrected rule therefore uses pass-one selected authority references at or below `params.channel.train_snr_db_fixed` **only** to recover the original dataset/ratio scope across every frozen pass-one mode; it then takes every authority candidate in that scope and SNR region, requires its frozen structural mapping to carry a positive reconciled payload budget, projects it onto exactly `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_quality_identity_fields`, and includes the complete exact-deduplicated projection. No score, rank, margin, selected PHY tuple, manual quality or newly chosen width may affect membership. `codec_configuration_id` is the complete G8_D JPEG 2000 configuration identity, including its output-affecting snapshot, hash and runtime version. Dataset and source codec remain explicit; payload budget and encode axis alter the pre-channel artifact; candidate ID, composition identity, ratio, SNR, modulation, LDPC rate and packet ID are projected away because, once the payload budget is fixed, they do not alter the JPEG 2000 search or codestream. Exact projection equality gives one quality and PHY aliases give no extra support multiplicity. AM-87's qualities remain ordered by quality ID and its complete 120-quality set remains the legitimate support universe. **AM-88 replaces only the exhaustive image × support materialization rule:** before F1, the frozen seed/version/algorithm deterministically key-order stable IDs within each training class, seed-permute the 120 quality IDs once, and concatenate ascending-class attempt chunks over that cyclic quality order. Every training stable ID receives exactly `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_variants_per_training_image` distinct quality assignments; every assignment is in AM-87 support; global and within-class quality counts differ by at most one, the arithmetically minimum bound; and no image-quality pair repeats. Membership and assignment may read only `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_assignment_information` and MUST NOT read anything in `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_assignment_forbidden_information`. Structural infeasibility or a non-positive/unreconciled budget is refused before the support plan. A typed per-image codec-infeasible result omits only that **assigned** image-quality pair, records per-quality and per-class attempted/materialized/omitted coverage, and performs no resampling, outage-image, neighbouring-quality or other substitution; any decode failure, exception, foreign identity or outcome other than typed codec infeasibility or a verified reconstruction puts G8_F on HOLD. Validation E4 feasibility counts are bound for audit and storage estimation only and MUST NOT add, remove or reweight a quality or assignment. The required order remains: freeze and owner-audit this exact plan; obtain separate F0 authorization; materialize training pairs; fine-tune; re-score validation artifacts; execute pass two once; freeze BR-4/BR-16/H2 outputs; close G-8. `params.reference_classifier.headline_scorer` names which score carries the headline, and BR-4's second selection pass runs under it (AM-54). Rationale: scoring heavily-artifacted reconstructions with a classifier trained only on clean images measures domain shift as well as delivered information, while the complete broad support removes post-hoc discretion and generally strengthens the comparator's scorer against codec domain shift; its net effect on a finite classifier remains empirically ambiguous. Representative codec-domain training does not require every image under every legitimate quality: AM-88's six variants per training image retain complete support and exact balance while reducing the auxiliary corpus burden 20-fold. *(verify: AM-87's metadata-only support-plan derivation still reproduces the exact quality set/order and complete training-ID set without image decode; an independently implemented AM-88 verifier reproduces all assignments and exact ordered/set digests; mutation tests reject seed/version/multiplicity, missing/extra/reordered/foreign support, training-ID/class-label drift, forbidden performance inputs, duplicate pairs, imbalance and validation/test IDs; F1 coverage proves the exact no-resampling omission rule; both scoring paths are distinct; and the training log postdates separate F0 authorization)*
- **BR-13** — An outage — infeasible (BR-5) or undecodable (BR-6) — MUST be scored per `params.baseline.outage_policy`: a **single constant class**, selected on the validation split per `params.baseline.outage_class_selection` with `params.baseline.outage_class_tie_break`, and frozen per `params.baseline.outage_class_frozen_before`. The outcome stays **binary** per image. Assigning a fractional accuracy remains prohibited: it is not a per-image outcome and would break the paired inference in ER-10. **This reverses AM-38, whose stated reason was false (AM-58).** AM-38 rejected a deterministic fallback because it "costs the property that makes ER-10 work, since the random draw is reproducible from `channel_seed` and therefore identical across systems". A **constant** prediction is trivially identical across systems — it is the most pairable fallback available — so the rejection rested on a claim that is simply not true, and it bought avoidable Monte Carlo variance for nothing. The random draw's own reproducibility was also weaker than claimed: systems outage on *different* images, so a sequential stream keyed only by `channel_seed` desynchronises between arms exactly when one of them fails and the other does not. Selecting the best constant class on validation and freezing it is deterministic, no-information, and **comparator-favourable** — it can only raise the digital arms' outage accuracy above `1/classes`, which is the permitted direction under DEC-16's governing rule. BR-4's analytic composition MUST use that class's **measured validation accuracy** rather than assuming exactly `1/classes`. `params.baseline.outage_policy_sensitivity` retains the random draw as a labelled sensitivity only, keyed by `params.baseline.outage_rng_key` rather than by conditional consumption of a shared stream. The reporting half of AM-38 stands: every affected row MUST carry `coverage_rate` and `acc_given_delivery` alongside end-to-end `top1_acc`, so a reader can separate "the link failed" from "the link worked and the task failed" (AM-38). *(verify: unit test that fallback predictions are invariant to row order, system, batching and outage frequency; a test that the frozen class is selected on validation and never re-derived at test; a test that the analytic and simulated outage accuracies reconcile with the frozen validation class prior; and a test that end-to-end accuracy reconciles with coverage and conditional accuracy)*
- **BR-14** — `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` MUST sit behind an adapter exposing only the operations in `params.baseline.ldpc_impl_provides`, with every step in `params.baseline.ldpc_impl_local` implemented above that seam and unit-testable against a stub encoder. Substituting `params.baseline.ldpc_impl_fallback` MUST NOT require any change above the seam. Two library-facing details are fixed here rather than at the call sites, both measured at W0 (AM-24). **Spelling:** this specification names the check-node update `params.baseline.ldpc_decoder`, which the library rejects; `params.baseline.ldpc_decoder_impl_spelling` is the string it accepts, and the mapping between them lives at the seam. **Sign:** `params.baseline.ldpc_llr_convention` records that the library reads log-likelihood ratios as log(p(x=1)/p(x=0)), so the demapper MUST emit a **negative** LLR for the mapping x = 1−2c. This is the seam's most dangerous detail because it fails silently — with the sign inverted the decoder still runs, still returns exactly k bits, and still reports no error, while producing a measured BER of 0.77 at an SNR where the correct sign gives 0.00. An adapter conformance test MUST therefore assert clean decoding at high SNR, not merely that decoding returns. **Modulation bit interleaver:** per `params.baseline.modulation_bit_interleaver_required` the seam MUST pass `params.baseline.modulation_bit_interleaver_arg` — the modulation order `Q_m` — to the encoder, and MUST invert it consistently at the decoder. `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` applies TS 38.212 §5.4.2.2's output interleaver **only when that argument is given**, and the W0 probe never gave it, so nothing measured so far exercised the interleaver at all. Omitting it changes which coded bits land on 16-QAM's unequally reliable bit channels and **silently weakens the baseline** — the same failure class as the LLR sign, and forbidden in the same direction by ER-8 (AM-58). **Decoder settings:** `params.baseline.ldpc_decoder_offset`, `ldpc_decoder_llr_clip`, `ldpc_decoder_schedule` and `ldpc_decoder_vn_update` MUST be passed explicitly and asserted, not inherited. G-2 says the offset is pinned, but no parameter pinned it and the library's default is 0.5 — a value the project would have been reporting without ever having chosen it (AM-58). **CRCs:** `params.baseline.cb_crc_bits` is **CRC24B**, named by `params.baseline.cb_crc_polynomial` — the transport-block and code-block CRC24s are different polynomials and TS 38.212 uses CRC24B for segmentation — and all three are pinned in `params.baseline.crc_spec` with polynomial, width, initialisation, final XOR and bit order, so a conformance test can be written from this file alone (AM-58). **Mapping and demapping:** `params.baseline.constellation_mapping`, `demapper` and `demapper_noise_variance_convention` are fixed here for the same reason the LLR sign is. **Base graph:** per `params.baseline.base_graph_pinned_at_seam` the seam MUST pass the graph selected by BR-10 explicitly rather than letting the library infer it. `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` selects a base graph per *codeword* from its own (k, r), which reproduces TS 38.212 §7.2.2 arithmetic but applies it at the wrong granularity — the standard decides once per transport block and then uses that graph for every code block, so a small final block can diverge from the rest of its own transport block if inference is left on. The library accepts an explicit base-graph argument, so this costs one parameter at the seam and removes an entire class of silent non-conformance (AM-49). Rationale: this is what makes DEC-10's fallback ladder real rather than aspirational, and it mirrors the registry pattern already required for channels (SR-5) and task heads (SR-15). *(verify: unit test exercising the segmentation layer against a stub encoder, plus an adapter conformance test run against both implementations)*
- **BR-15** — The **adaptation asymmetry MUST be disclosed** wherever the headline comparison appears: the classical system is re-tuned at every SNR across codec rate, `params.baseline.ldpc_rates` and `params.baseline.modulations`, while the learned system is trained once per DEC-11 and evaluated frozen. Rationale: the asymmetry is deliberate and favours the baseline, which is what BR-4 exists to do — but it is also a large part of why any crossover appears, and an advantage handed to the baseline is still a confound if it goes unstated. Disclosed in advance it reads as rigour; discovered by an examiner it reads as a thumb on the scale. The asymmetry cuts **both** ways and the disclosure MUST say so: at high SNR the baseline's re-tuning lets it climb to 16-QAM, but at low SNR it lets the baseline fall back to BPSK rate 1/3 for roughly 3 dB of extra reach — squarely inside H1's region — while the learned model is frozen at a 7 dB training point and evaluated down to −8 dB, a 15 dB mismatch. That is the mechanism by which the primary hypothesis could fail for a design reason rather than a physical one, which is why ER-2 now **requires** the SNR-randomised variant that answers it with a curve rather than a paragraph (AM-45). *(verify: the asymmetry stated in the thesis methods section and in the caption of every headline figure, and the fixed-modulation reference curve of BR-9 present for comparison)*

- **BR-16** — A **fully fixed separated curve** MUST be reported: source codec configuration, LDPC rate and modulation all selected at `params.baseline.fixed_mcs_design_snr_db` on the validation split and then frozen across the whole of `params.channel.test_snr_grid_db`. This curve, named by `params.evaluation.cliff_reference_system`, is the reference for H2; BR-4's adaptive curve remains the fairness baseline for H1 and the headline comparison. Rationale: BR-4 re-tunes codec rate, code rate and modulation at every SNR, so its curve is the upper envelope of many configurations rather than one system's waterfall — an envelope degrades in *steps* as it falls back to more robust settings and only cliffs when it runs out of them. H2's `params.evaluation.cliff_drop_pp` threshold was set against a sharper curve than BR-4 will actually produce, and the classical cliff-versus-graceful comparison in the DJSCC literature is against a fixed separated configuration evaluated away from its design point, which is what this curve is. BR-9's fixed-*modulation* curve is insufficient for this purpose because source rate and LDPC rate still adapt within it. The accompanying prose MUST be narrowed to match: the claim is that *the selected fixed-rate, single-packet separated baseline* exhibits a waterfall, not that separated coding has a cliff in general — progressive JPEG 2000 packetisation or unequal error protection can itself degrade gracefully (AM-53). BR-16 carries `params.baseline.fixed_mcs_packet_count` = 1 deliberately: it is H2's narrow waterfall reference by construction. **Narrowing H2's wording does not answer the fairness objection to H1, so a sensitivity is required (AM-58).** The headline reconstruction baseline puts an entire JPEG 2000 codestream behind one transport-block success condition, and the specification itself concedes that progressive packetisation degrades more gracefully — so a reader may reasonably ask whether the classical arm's cliff is a property of separated coding or of a packetisation choice this project made for it. A sensitivity over `params.baseline.packet_count_grid` MUST therefore be reported at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` for one entry in `params.evaluation.train_seeds`, independently protecting progressive prefixes under the same total `k`, with every CRC and packet overhead counted (BR-11) and the packetisation tuned on validation. Its design is frozen at `params.baseline.packet_count_gate`. It MUST be shown **immediately beside the headline curve**, not in an appendix: if a multi-packet baseline materially closes the gap or changes H1's verdict, that is a first-class finding and burying it would be the sort of selective presentation ER-8 exists to prevent. *(verify: the fixed-MCS curve present as a distinct `system` value across the full grid, its frozen configuration archived with its validation-split provenance, H2 computed against it rather than against the adaptive curve, and the packet-count sensitivity present at the headline ratio with its overhead accounted and its figure adjacent to the headline)*
- **BR-17** — **The owner-directed G8_C compute migration is an additive supersession, never a mixed continuation (AM-84, AM-86).** The existing `local_4060_cu130` campaign remains valid, authenticated, immutable historical evidence at its actual durable boundary, including accepted units and trailing request-only attempts, but its continuation status is `superseded` and it contributes zero coverage to the final G8 `BlerTable`. A new successor campaign MUST begin at zero accepted coverage, bind `confessor_pascal_cu126`, preserve every physical identity and scientific rule of BR-4 and the frozen G8 contracts — all 3,213 required identities, 5,000 trials each, LDPC/base-graph/lifting/rate/modulation/SNR/decoder/demapper/counting/interval semantics unchanged — and accept only successor results. Its two explicit workers bind `cuda:0` and `cuda:1` plus their enumerated UUIDs, partition canonical authority ordinals modulo two without duplicates or omissions, and recover solely from authenticated durable request/result/state. `confessor` is the sole scientific writer for this successor; full-strength execution cannot open before the implementation is merged, main parity is established, the committed cu126 lock is recreated, final non-scientific qualification passes, authenticated GitHub HTTPS push succeeds, and the zero-coverage successor marker is pushed. After owner authorization, authenticated per-unit runtime evidence may accumulate continuously on `confessor` without per-batch Git publication; the owner accepts the custody risk of losing bytes not yet published, while surviving evidence remains eligible only through exact authentication and final verification. Git publication may occur after the unattended campaign or at an owner-selected manual checkpoint, but complete reconciliation, authenticated commit/push and local/remote parity remain mandatory before successor-table freeze and G8_D release. *(verify: supersession artifact authenticates the old campaign/state/source epochs and accepted prefix; old-tree aggregate hash remains unchanged; successor manifest starts 0/3213 and rejects every old result; production custody contract binds deferred-publication scope and mandatory final publication; table verifier accepts successor sources only; dual-GPU mutation/interruption tests; full read-only G8 verifier suite; zero validation, selection, training, inference and test-access counters)*

## 7. Experiment requirements (ER)

- **ER-1** — **Core comparison.** Learned, classical and ER-9 control systems, full test split, every SNR in `params.channel.test_snr_grid_db`, at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` — which resolves to `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio`, because a crossover claimed from a one-seed sweep figure while the full-strength intervals sit at a different budget would rest the project's most contested result on its weakest evidence (AM-20) — repeated over the seed cells defined by `params.evaluation.seed_pairing` — `params.evaluation.train_seeds` **zipped with** `params.evaluation.channel_seeds`, giving three cells, not their nine-cell cross product — reported with `params.evaluation.ci` intervals, measuring `params.project.primary_metric`. Rationale: the conjunction was ambiguous and the two readings differ threefold in evaluation cost; §16's decode-throughput estimate was written against three cells, and a third channel realisation of an already-frozen model buys far less than a third training run does (AM-17). Models are trained per DEC-11. Hypotheses are decided by §2 via ER-10. *(verify: results CSV + accuracy-vs-SNR figure, with exactly one row group per seed cell)*
- **ER-2** — **SNR mismatch.** Train once at `params.channel.train_snr_db_fixed`, evaluate across the whole test grid, and report the degradation profile of both systems to evidence the cliff-versus-graceful contrast (H2). An **SNR-randomised learned variant** trained over `params.channel.train_snr_db_set` MUST also be reported, at one entry in `params.evaluation.train_seeds` and at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` — one training run. Rationale: BR-15 discloses that the baseline re-tunes at every SNR while the learned model is frozen, and that asymmetry favours the baseline at *both* ends of the grid, including inside H1's region where the baseline may drop to BPSK for roughly 3 dB of extra reach. This was previously OPT-4, non-blocking and therefore unlikely to be run; the objection it answers — "your learned system was not allowed to adapt and the baseline was" — is one an examiner will raise, and `params.channel.train_snr_db_set` already existed in this file serving nothing else (AM-45). *(verify: results CSV + figure, with the fixed-SNR and SNR-randomised learned curves present as distinct `system` values)*
- **ER-3** — **Operating-point selection and bandwidth sweep.** Before the headline run, sweep every entry in `params.bandwidth.ratios` **with the classical system only**, on the **full validation split** at one seed, and select **two** operating points at G-8 by this preregistered, **learned-blind** rule applied at two thresholds: *the smallest ratio at which the classical system's high-SNR ceiling comes within `t` percentage points of its clean-image accuracy*, evaluated at `t` = `params.bandwidth.efficiency_ratio_threshold_pp` to give `params.bandwidth.efficiency_ratio`, and at `t` = `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio_threshold_pp` to give `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio`. **"Ceiling" and "within `t`" are now executable (AM-59).** The ceiling is `params.bandwidth.ceiling_definition` — BR-4's already-required error-free codec validation accuracy — rather than "the 18 dB point", "the maximum grid point" or "a fitted plateau", which are three different numbers; and the comparison is `params.bandwidth.ceiling_rule`, a one-sided lower bound on (codec ceiling − clean accuracy) at or above `−t`, rather than an unsigned "within `t`" that did not say whether a ceiling *above* clean satisfies it. Using the error-free codec score keeps the rule learned-blind, removes channel-seed noise from a selection decision, treats a codec score above clean as satisfying the rule, and errs toward handing the baseline more budget — the permitted direction. `params.bandwidth.upper_grid_saturation_check` additionally requires the upper grid to be extended, before test and without consulting any learned test outcome, if the adaptive validation baseline has not saturated by 18 dB: truncating the grid before 16-QAM reaches its plateau would cap the baseline's ceiling by measurement rather than by physics. Both are lookups against the same classical sweep table, so the second threshold costs nothing to evaluate, and the two MAY select the same ratio — in which case there is one operating point and `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` resolves to it unchanged. **If no ratio satisfies the crossover threshold while some ratio satisfies the efficiency threshold**, `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio` resolves to the largest ratio in `params.bandwidth.ratios`, `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` resolves to `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio_unsatisfiable_fallback` instead, and the absence of a 2 pp ratio is reported as a first-class finding under DEC-16's fallback. This is preregistered here rather than settled at G-8 because G-8 holds the classical sweep table in hand, and 2 pp is a demanding bar for a lossy codec scored by a clean-trained classifier — the asymmetric failure is the likely one, and G-8's existing fallback covers only the symmetric case where the rule fails at every threshold (AM-41). Rationale for two: the looser threshold locates the **efficiency point**, the tightest budget at which the baseline is still healthy, which is where the bandwidth claim lives; but "within 5 points of clean" admits a classical ceiling around 0.83 while the learned ceiling is estimated at 0.85–0.87, so that threshold can select a ratio at which no crossover exists at all. The tighter threshold locates the **crossover point**, the smallest budget at which the classical ceiling can actually overtake the learned one. Reporting both shows the trade-off as a function of budget rather than asserting it at a point (AM-20). The rule inspects only the classical system at either threshold, so it cannot be accused of selecting for the hypothesis; it places the comparison where the baseline is healthy rather than where it is starved, and tightening a threshold hands the baseline *more* budget, which DEC-16's governing rule expressly permits. Because BR-4 now adapts modulation as well (DEC-16), the classical ceiling rises faster with SNR than it would under a fixed modulation, so the rule is expected to select a *smaller* ratio than it otherwise would — the crossover is bought with better use of the budget rather than with more budget. The sweep also reports `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point` as the bandwidth-starved regime where dominance rather than crossover is expected. That point is resolved at G-8 by `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_rule` rather than read as a fixed value: it was hard-coded at `r_1_12` while `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` is selected, so if the rule selects a smaller ratio than `r_1_12` the "starved" point sits *above* the operating point and the regime it names does not exist — leaving §2's expectation of low-ratio dominance with no experiment behind it (AM-41). **The rule's wording was wrong and is corrected (AM-59):** it read "smallest ratio at least two rungs below the headline", which literally selects the *bottom* of every eligible rung — `r_1_48`, not the `r_1_12` its own provisional value records — so the parameter and the rule that resolves it disagreed. It now names exactly two ordered ladder rungs below, with `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_boundary_rule` covering the case where two steps do not exist. That boundary matters architecturally: the automatic downward extension is **not** free below `r_1_48`, where the 4× encoder already emits a single complex channel, so an honest left-censored result ("selected at or below `r_1_48`") is preferable to an unbounded G-8 architecture redesign, and ER-9's admissible (dimension, width) pairs fall to one on STL-10 at that rung. For the same reason the ladder MUST NOT be allowed to saturate at its bottom: per `params.bandwidth.ladder_bottom_saturation_rule`, if the rule selects the smallest ratio in `params.bandwidth.ratios` the ladder MUST be extended downward and the classical-only sweep re-run before the selection is recorded. `r_1_48` was added for this reason and is free architecturally — it is exactly one complex channel at the 4x-downsampled grid on all three datasets, where `r_1_32` would need 1.5 and is therefore not usable. Because the rule is learned-blind, learned systems MUST be trained at `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio`, `params.bandwidth.efficiency_ratio` and `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point` **only** — at most three ratios, and two if the thresholds coincide — nothing in §2 needs a learned model at every ratio, and training five would spend roughly nine runs to inform a decision that cannot see them (AM-12).
