The sweep uses the full validation split rather than `params.evaluation.test_subset_size`: that parameter sizes a subset of the *test* split under ER-6, and at 2000 it exceeds the `val_images` count of every dataset in `params.datasets`, so the requirement as previously written could not be executed at all (AM-14). *(verify: results CSV + figure, an archived record of the rule's evaluation at every ratio, and an assertion that no learned-system row exists at a ratio outside the ratios named here — previously "the two named here", which contradicted this requirement's own body naming three, a stale clause AM-20 left behind (AM-26))*
- **ER-4** — **Task-training ablation.** The semantic reconstruction head MUST also be scored through the frozen `params.reference_classifier` (BR-8), separating gain attributable to joint coding from gain attributable to a task-trained classifier. Reported as a distinct `system` value. *(verify: results CSV containing the ablation rows)*
- **ER-5** — Every experiment MUST emit rows matching `params.artifacts.csv_schema` exactly — same columns, same order — alongside the per-image file required by SR-18. *(verify: schema validation script over all result CSVs)*
- **ER-6** — Evaluation on `params.evaluation.test_subset_size` images is permitted for sweeps only; `params.evaluation.full_test_split_required_for` MUST use the full split, and the `test_subset` column MUST record which was used. **Sweep strength is a size concession, not a timing one (AM-60):** every experiment that reads the test split, whatever its strength, runs inside the single opening of `params.evaluation.test_campaign_is_single` at G-12, and `params.evaluation.test_campaign_covers` enumerates them. Before AM-60 this requirement was read as permitting test subsets whenever convenient, which is how ER-11 and ER-12 came to sit at W10 alongside a rehearsal that had just been moved to validation to keep test sealed — the subset would have opened the split three weeks early and made the move pointless. *(verify: schema test asserting the flag, and a test that no result row carrying `split = test` predates the committed freeze manifest)*
- **ER-7** — Every number appearing in the thesis MUST be traceable to a `run_id` and `git_commit` in a committed CSV. *(verify: audit script resolving each reported figure to its rows)*
- **ER-8** — If a hypothesis in §2 is unsupported, the negative result MUST be reported with the same rigour as a positive one, together with the diagnostic evidence. Weakening the baseline to manufacture support is prohibited. *(verify: review against BR-4 sweep artifacts and BR-11 overhead tables)*
- **ER-9** — **Task-aware digital control.** A third system MUST be built and evaluated at matched `k`, sharing every element in `params.digital_semantic_control.shared_with_learned` with the learned JSCC system and differing from it **only** in `params.digital_semantic_control.differs_only_in`: features taken at `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_layer` are quantised per `params.digital_semantic_control.quantiser` at a width selected from `params.digital_semantic_control.quantiser_bits_grid` on the validation split (DEC-12), entropy-coded per `params.digital_semantic_control.entropy_coder`, and carried over the same `params.baseline.channel_code` chain as the classical baseline with its transport configuration tuned per `params.digital_semantic_control.transport_tuning`. It MUST be scored by `params.digital_semantic_control.scored_by` — a task head architecturally identical to the learned system's — **not** by the frozen `params.reference_classifier`, which consumes images and cannot read a feature stream (AM-5). **The transmitted dimension is ER-9's own and is selected, not inherited (AM-55).** `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_dim_grid`, in `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_dim_units`, is chosen jointly with the quantiser width on the validation split per `params.digital_semantic_control.width_selection`, realised per `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_dim_realised_by`, and searched per `params.digital_semantic_control.selection_search` — two-stage, with `params.digital_semantic_control.selection_search_is_cross_product` false, so W9's training count stays bounded. Matched `k` then fixes the budget, and the budget MUST be sized against a **proven** bound rather than an expected one: per `params.digital_semantic_control.budget_sized_against`, transmitted dimension × quantiser width **plus `params.digital_semantic_control.framing_selector_bits`** MUST fit the same `params.baseline.budget_rule` payload the classical baseline receives, and `params.digital_semantic_control.raw_escape_required` mandates `params.digital_semantic_control.raw_escape_rule` — the transmitter sends whichever of the range-coded and fixed-width raw streams is shorter, and the counted selector bit says which. Rationale: a finite-precision range coder can **expand** on unfavourable data, so a control sized on its expected compressed length is not guaranteed to fit, and an overflowing control would show up as an outage — silently weakening the very control that exists to make H4 hard (AM-58). **The feasibility margin is thin at the bottom of the ladder and MUST be watched:** across every ladder rung on both headline datasets the control is feasible, but the number of admissible (dimension, width) pairs falls from 23 at `r_1_3` on Imagenette to **7** at STL-10's `r_1_12`, **3** at STL-10's `r_1_24` and **1** at STL-10's `r_1_48` — so if ER-3 selects or `params.bandwidth.ladder_bottom_saturation_rule` extends to the bottom rung, `params.digital_semantic_control.selection_search` has no search space left to run over. That only became visible once the proof obligation was broadened past the three provisional live ratios (AM-58). **This replaces a specification that was arithmetically infeasible at every operating point but one.** `transmit_feature_dim` was previously shared with the learned system, pinning the dimension at 2k real values while the digital budget supplies only `Q_m × R / 2` bits per value — 1.67 at 16-QAM rate 5/6, 0.5 at QPSK rate 1/2, and 0.167 at BPSK rate 1/3, against a 2-bit floor in `params.digital_semantic_control.quantiser_bits_grid`. Every configuration except 16-QAM at rate 5/6 was therefore impossible, and that one decodes only above roughly 11 dB — outside H1's region entirely — so ER-9 would have been pinned at outage and scored at chance across exactly the range H4 is tested in, making the control unfalsifiable and H4 trivially easy to support. That is the AM-15 failure class and the direction ER-8 forbids. Choosing how many values to send against how precisely to send each one **is** the digital channel interface — an analog system makes no such choice — so this remains a difference in `params.digital_semantic_control.differs_only_in` rather than an addition to it, and `params.digital_semantic_control.channel_interface_comprises` names its three components. Feasibility is now verified rather than assumed: at the tightest live configuration, STL-10 at the low ratio with BPSK rate 1/3 and 94 payload bytes, seven (dimension, width) pairs fit. Rationale: without a control that is task-aware *and* digital, the learned-versus-classical gap conflates task-aware representation with joint coding and §1 claim 2 cannot be attributed (H4); and unless the control shares the learned system's front end, a measured difference could equally be architecture, capacity or training objective rather than the channel interface. This is the primary novelty claim of DEC-13. **The entropy table is not charged per image**, per `params.digital_semantic_control.entropy_table_bytes_counted`: the model is fitted offline on the training split and installs at both endpoints alongside the network weights, exactly like the quantiser scales, the JPEG 2000 coding rules and the LDPC matrices, none of which are charged either. Charging it would weaken the control on every image and make H4 easier to support, which is the wrong direction to be wrong in. Only genuinely per-message metadata counts — stream length, any image-adaptive scale, and framing (AM-55). *(verify: results CSV rows under a distinct `system` value at the same k as ER-1, with the selected `quantiser_bits` and transmitted dimension recorded per row, plus an architecture-diff artifact showing that only `params.digital_semantic_control.differs_only_in` separates the two systems)*
- **ER-10** — **Paired inference.** The §2 hypotheses MUST be decided on **paired per-image outcomes** (SR-18), not on the overlap of two independent intervals, and against the single estimand §2 defines: `params.evaluation.estimand`, aggregated per `params.evaluation.seed_aggregation` and interval-estimated by `params.evaluation.ci` with `params.evaluation.bootstrap_resamples` resamples of `params.evaluation.bootstrap_unit`, drawn once per `params.evaluation.bootstrap_resample_scope`. `params.evaluation.per_cell_resampling_prohibited` forbids resampling images independently inside each seed cell, and a resampled image MUST carry its complete system × SNR × cell trajectory: joins MUST be one-to-one and MUST reject missing or duplicated trajectories. `params.evaluation.paired_test` is retained with the role `params.evaluation.paired_test_role` — a descriptive per-cell, per-SNR check that decides nothing. **This replaces "the image-level bootstrap nested inside the seed hierarchy" (AM-57),** which described a nesting the data does not have: the same test images appear in all three cells, so images are **crossed** with cells, and at least five mutually incompatible readings of the old sentence were available, several of which return different H1–H4 verdicts on identical data. `params.evaluation.estimand_conditioning` states what the resulting interval does and does not cover. Each seed cell is a **compound replicate** per `params.evaluation.seed_cell_interpretation`, and no claim is made that `params.evaluation.train_seeds` and `params.evaluation.channel_seeds` contribute as *separate* variance components. This requirement previously promised exactly that, which AM-17 made unachievable in the same round it was written: `params.evaluation.seed_pairing` zips the seeds into (0,0), (1,1), (2,2), so training luck and channel luck move together in every cell and no analysis can attribute a difference to one rather than the other. Three diagonal cells cannot separate two sources — the honest statement is that each cell is one combined replicate, and the per-seed curves below are what show the spread. Crossing the seeds into nine cells would restore the decomposition at roughly three times ER-1's evaluation cost, and was rejected because two degrees of freedom could not support the claim even then (AM-31). Rationale: both systems see identical images and noise draws, so a paired test is both the natural and the far more powerful analysis — a three-seed Student-t interval has two degrees of freedom and would demand roughly a five-point gap to clear non-overlap. Three further rules make the seed hierarchy honest, since `params.evaluation.bootstrap_resamples` resamples images and cannot manufacture training-run evidence that three seeds do not contain (AM-11): the **classical arm has no training-seed dimension** — it is never trained — so the training-seed variance component belongs to the learned arm alone and a classical result MUST NOT be replicated across seed cells and counted as independent baseline runs; `params.evaluation.paired_test` MUST be computed **within** a seed cell and never pooled across cells, which would treat repeated measurements of the same images as independent; and each seed's accuracy-vs-SNR curve MUST be reported separately alongside the between-seed range, so the reader sees training-run spread rather than inferring it from an interval that mostly reflects image count. ER-10 also computes `params.evaluation.h1_effect_size` and `params.evaluation.gap_trend_test` for §2's H1 and H3. Further computations are required. **H1's run rule MUST be calibrated, and the calibration MUST bind the decision**: `params.evaluation.h1_run_calibration` over `params.evaluation.h1_run_permutation_resamples` resamples gives the null distribution of the longest run of qualified points by applying `params.evaluation.h1_run_calibration_multiplier` multipliers to whole **centered** per-image trajectories, which preserves the across-SNR correlation that makes the analytic argument in §2 a model assumption rather than a bound. Per `params.evaluation.h1_run_calibration_binds_decision` the resulting p-value is a **support condition**, not an archived diagnostic, at `params.evaluation.h1_run_calibration_alpha`; the p-value MUST be the conventional `(1 + #{R_b ≥ R_obs}) / (B + 1)` form (AM-32, AM-57). **H4 MUST be evaluated with the same estimand, qualifier and calibration** against `params.evaluation.h4_comparator`, per `params.evaluation.h4_uses_h1_rule`, and `params.evaluation.h4_mde_simulation` MUST be run and archived by `params.evaluation.h4_mde_gate` (AM-57). **H2 MUST be decided by `params.evaluation.h2_test`** as an intersection-union of the two one-sided margins on `params.evaluation.h2_thresholds_apply_to`, with the difference-in-differences reported per `params.evaluation.h2_did_reported_as` (AM-57). **H3's slope MUST be bootstrapped over `params.evaluation.gap_trend_bootstrap_unit`** — per-image, per-seed trajectories — rather than over 21 aggregated SNR points, which are strongly correlated through shared images and shared models and would give an interval far too narrow (AM-39); its magnitude-contraction clause is bootstrapped over the same unit at `params.evaluation.h3_low_snr_point_db` and `params.evaluation.h3_high_snr_point_db` (AM-57). **Every inferential number MUST be committed** to `params.artifacts.inference_summary_file` under `params.artifacts.inference_summary_schema`: the aggregate CSV cannot store an interval bound, a p-value, a run length, an H2 margin, an H3 slope or a verdict, so ER-7's requirement that every thesis number resolve to a committed artifact was unsatisfiable for exactly the numbers §2 turns on. Per-image outcomes are stored per `params.artifacts.per_image_storage` with `params.artifacts.per_image_manifest` committed, so ignoring `params.artifacts.per_image_dir` no longer leaves the inferential record unreproducible (AM-57). *(verify: inference script reproducing every reported interval from the per-image files; a unit test on synthetic data of known effect size; a fixture with deliberately different per-cell effects returning one predetermined aggregate, one predetermined interval, and unchanged pair membership after row-order shuffling; a test that independent per-cell image resampling and duplicate or missing trajectories are rejected; H1 fixtures covering an all-zero paired case, independent null trajectories, §2's block-dependent counterexample, a planted three-point effect and a planted isolated-point effect, with the empirical false-positive rate at or below 0.05 within Monte Carlo error and the isolated point not supporting H1; H2 fixtures failing each margin separately even when the difference-in-differences is large; H3 fixtures `+5→0`, `+5→−30`, `−5→−30`, flat `+5` and chance→growth→contraction receiving their preregistered verdicts; a test that no pooled-across-seeds McNemar statistic is emitted; and an archived null distribution for the run statistic)*
- **ER-11** — **Bandwidth versus accuracy.** The learned system MUST be evaluated at **every** ratio DEC-11 trains it at — not `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` alone — across `params.channel.test_snr_grid_db`, at sweep strength per ER-6 and one entry in `params.evaluation.train_seeds`, with the classical curve at the same ratio, and reported as the bandwidth-versus-accuracy figure. Rationale: this closes the defect AM-12 fixed once and AM-20 reopened at reduced scale. DEC-11 trains up to three ratios, but ER-1 runs at the headline ratio alone, ER-2 names no ratio and ER-4 is an ablation of existing rows — so the `params.bandwidth.efficiency_ratio` and `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point` models were trained and then consumed by no requirement, no figure and no hypothesis. It matters beyond waste: DEC-16's last-resort fallback reports learned dominance across the whole grid, and the low-ratio regime is where that claim is strongest, so the evidence for it MUST already be a required output rather than something improvised at W12. At `params.evaluation.test_subset_size` and one seed this costs a few percent of ER-1 (AM-42). *(verify: results CSV rows at every trained ratio for both systems, plus the committed figure)*
- **ER-12** — **Label-transmission upper bound.** A reference curve MUST be reported in which the transmitter runs the task head locally and transmits only the predicted label over the same `params.baseline.channel_code` chain at matched `k`, reusing ER-9's encoder trunk, at one entry in `params.evaluation.train_seeds` and at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio`. It MUST be labelled an **upper bound, not a matched comparator**, and MUST NOT appear in any §2 hypothesis. Rationale: for ten-class classification the complete task output is four bits, so an examiner can ask why the transmitter runs a neural encoder and sends thousands of symbols rather than attaching the head locally and protecting four bits heavily. §1 acknowledges that a digital transmitter could send "logits or labels" but ER-9 only builds the feature end of that spectrum, leaving the sharpest conceptual objection to the project unanswered by any experiment. The answer is the fixed-split premise §1 now states — the partition is imposed, the receiver owns the task head — and this curve makes the bound explicit rather than leaving it to be raised at a viva. It is nearly free, reusing a trained trunk (AM-40). *(verify: results CSV row under a distinct `system` value, and a caption stating the fixed-split premise and that the bound assumes a transmitter that already knows the answer)*

## 8. Demo requirements (DR)

- **DR-1** — An SNR slider spanning `params.channel.test_snr_grid_db` MUST drive both pipelines live on the same input image. *(verify: manual demo script walkthrough)*
- **DR-2** — The interface MUST show, side by side, the classical output, the semantic reconstruction, and each system's predicted label with confidence. *(verify: manual walkthrough)*
- **DR-3** — The accuracy-vs-SNR plot MUST update live with a marker at the current slider position. *(verify: manual walkthrough)*
- **DR-4** — The demo MUST render figures through `params.demo.figure_style_module`, the same module that renders thesis figures, using `params.demo.fonts` and `params.demo.palette`, with default framework chrome suppressed (DEC-7). *(verify: visual review that a demo figure and its thesis counterpart come from the same style module — pixel comparison is not required and is not achievable across DPI, backend and font hinting)*
- **DR-5** — The demo MUST run with `params.demo.offline` true and remain usable on CPU only. *(verify: run with networking disabled on a CPU-only machine)*
- **DR-6** — The demo MUST consume frozen checkpoints and committed result CSVs; it MUST NOT train, fine-tune or recompute reported metrics. *(verify: code review)*

## 9. Hardware requirements (HR)

Tier 2 and Tier 3 are stretch goals with a pre-recorded demonstration as the expected outcome (DEC-14).

- **HR-1** — Tier 2/3 hardware MUST satisfy `params.hardware_tier23.needs` at no less than `params.hardware_tier23.min_sample_rate_msps`, within `params.hardware_tier23.budget_inr_range` and subject to `params.hardware_tier23.budget_note`. Devices in `params.hardware_tier23.candidates` are indicative, not selected. *(verify: procurement checklist against the capability list)*
- **HR-2** — No hardware may be purchased before `params.hardware_tier23.purchase_gate` passes. *(verify: gate record)*
- **HR-3** — **Tier 2 (stretch).** Encoder output SHOULD be replayed as IQ through a real link (wired loopback with attenuator) and captured, then decoded offline and compared against the simulated result at matched measured SNR. *(verify: measured-vs-simulated accuracy table, or a recorded decision not to attempt)*
- **HR-4** — **Tier 3 (stretch).** A live encoder/decoder demo on `params.hardware_tier23.edge_node` SHOULD meet `params.hardware_tier23.live_demo_latency_budget_ms` end-to-end. If it cannot, resolve at G-6 by pre-recording per `params.hardware_tier23.expected_demonstration`. *(verify: measured latency distribution, or the pre-recorded artifact)*
- **HR-5** — No Tier 1 requirement may depend on hardware availability. Tier 1 MUST be completable, reportable and defensible with simulation alone. *(verify: review of SR/BR/ER for hardware dependencies)*
- **HR-6** — Any Tier 2 replay MUST specify and implement the full physical-layer wrapper in `params.hardware_tier23.framing`, and MUST state how channel SNR is *measured* rather than assumed, so the measured-versus-simulated comparison in HR-3 is meaningful. Rationale: an IQ replay without framing, timing recovery and frequency-offset correction does not produce a comparable link, and pilot overhead changes the channel-use accounting that BR-3 depends on. *(verify: framing design note plus a loopback capture showing locked timing and residual CFO within tolerance)*

## 10. Programme deliverables (PR)

Graded course deliverables. They are not engineering requirements, but they carry roughly thirty of the hundred available marks and are invisible in an engineering-only schedule, so they are tracked here with the same discipline.

- **PR-1** — A literature review of at least `params.deliverables.literature_review_min_refs` references MUST be drafted before the first review and maintained thereafter, covering DJSCC, separation and finite-blocklength theory, and learned image compression. *(verify: reference list committed and cited in the report draft)*
- **PR-2** — A time plan in the form of `params.deliverables.time_plan_artifact` MUST exist by the first review week in `params.deliverables.review_weeks` and be updated at each subsequent review. *(verify: committed chart artifact with revision history)*
- **PR-3** — A standards and tools register MUST list every entry in `params.deliverables.standards` with where each is used in the implementation, **and MUST state the standards claim in the exact terms of `params.baseline.standards_claim`, together with everything in `params.baseline.standards_claim_excludes`.** Rationale: this project implements a TS 38.212-derived LDPC coding and rate-matching core over an abstract AWGN channel with custom byte-framed payloads. It does **not** implement TS 38.214 transport-block sizing or MCS selection, resource allocation, OFDM or resource mapping, or HARQ, and plain BPSK is a generic simulation choice rather than NR's π/2-BPSK. Claiming "5G NR packetisation" or link conformance without those layers overstates what was built, and an examiner with a communications background will know the difference — so the register states the boundary rather than leaving it to be discovered (AM-58). *(verify: register committed, each entry resolvable to code or a spec requirement, and the claim sentence and its exclusions present verbatim in the register and in the report's methods section)*
- **PR-4** — A poster in `params.deliverables.poster_format` MUST be prepared and submitted. *(verify: submitted artifact)*
- **PR-5** — A plagiarism report MUST be produced per `params.deliverables.plagiarism_report_required` and submitted with the final report. *(verify: submitted artifact)*
- **PR-6** — The final report MUST conform to `params.deliverables.report_format_source`, proof-read and ratified by the guide. *(verify: format checklist signed off)*
- **PR-7** — A written novelty statement MUST name `params.deliverables.novelty_claims` explicitly, state what in each is not present in the prior work **as established by PR-1's completed review**, and be defensible independently of whether §2's hypotheses are supported (DEC-13). It MUST NOT claim novelty for graceful degradation, cliff-avoidance, or the existence of a task-aware digital semantic system — all three have substantial prior art — and any "not reported in the literature" assertion MUST be traceable to the reviewed reference list rather than asserted from this specification (AM-10). *(verify: statement committed, every novelty assertion resolvable to a PR-1 reference, and a negative-check listing the prior art each claim is distinguished from)*
- **PR-8** — Each review in `params.deliverables.review_weeks` MUST have a prepared package matching that review's rubric weighting, and the engineering gates MUST be scheduled so the required evidence exists beforehand. Verified sub-mark weights: First 30 → 10, Second 50 → 30, Third **60** → 40, Report 20 → 20. Three consequences are normative. **(a)** Objectives at every review MUST be stated per `params.deliverables.objectives_stated_as` — in §2's completion terms (build, validate, bandwidth-match, bit-account, evaluate at the learned-blind operating point, report with paired inference) — and MUST NOT be stated as outcomes such as "show that the learned system beats the classical one". The Third Review scores `Objectives Met` at 10 sub-marks, defined as whether objectives set at the first review or modified at the second were met; §2 makes completion independent of which way the result falls, but a rubric does not grade completion, so an objective phrased as an outcome converts DEC-16's fallback — which §2 declares a complete Tier 1 — into an objective visibly not met. Everything §2 gets right can be undone by one slide at W4. **(b)** If G-10 takes DEC-16's fallback, the objectives MUST be restated at `params.deliverables.objectives_modification_point`, which the rubric explicitly sanctions, with the G-8 and G-10 evidence attached. **(c)** The Second and Third packages MUST each carry a one-slide distillation of §17, which is what the `Methodology` rubric asks for — the approach decided *based on the results obtained*, i.e. documented course correction — and which this project has already done the work for (AM-46). **(d) Each package MUST be committed under `params.deliverables.review_package_dir` and derived from a frozen snapshot (AM-64).** The snapshot mechanism is `params.deliverables.review_snapshot_mechanism` — an annotated git tag named per `params.deliverables.review_snapshot_tag_format` — **not a branch**: a long-lived review branch diverges from `main`, reports itself as N commits behind, and invites a merge that should never happen, whereas a tag is an immutable pointer that `git archive` can export and `git show` can attribute. The tag MUST be cut when the review's readiness condition is met, and the package derived from it thereafter, so that what was presented is recoverable exactly rather than reconstructed from memory. **For the First Review that condition is `params.deliverables.review_1_ready_when`** — PR-1 committed, PR-2 committed, and G-1 passed. Rationale: the First Review scores `params.deliverables.review_1_rubric_criteria`, six criteria at `params.deliverables.review_1_submarks_each` sub-marks each, of which **Problem Survey is PR-1 and Time Plan is PR-2** — so a third of the review rests on those two artifacts, and neither existed when this clause was written. G-1 is included because `Subject Knowledge` is assessed by general viva and `Hypothesis` by whether a proposal to achieve the objectives exists: a measured clean-accuracy result makes both concrete rather than aspirational. The slot is `params.deliverables.review_1_slot_minutes`, which bounds the package at roughly ten to twelve slides. Note that `Presentation` is **not** a First Review criterion — it first appears at the Second — so the marks are for substance, not delivery. *(verify: review package committed under `params.deliverables.review_package_dir` before each review week, derived from the tag named by `params.deliverables.review_snapshot_tag_format`, with the objectives slide checked against §2's wording and the §17 slide present)*
- **PR-9** — A **deployment system-design dossier** MUST be produced — `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_artifact`, covering every item in `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_dossier_contents`: deployment architecture, link budget, packet and frame design, latency, compute and energy estimates, and the mapping from this project's simulated finite-blocklength link to a real radio. No hardware. Rationale: the `Application` rubric asks whether the project has real-world applications, and DEC-14 makes Tiers 2 and 3 upside while HR-5 makes Tier 1 standalone — correct engineering that nonetheless leaves those marks resting on a Streamlit demo of a simulation (AM-46). **This was a one-page note and that is too thin for what it is load-bearing for (AM-59).** `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_clause` records the governing sentence of the current circular: projects are *expected* to have a hardware implementation, and failing that must have "significant design aspects with an application to real world problems". Tier 1 can satisfy the alternative, but only if the design work is actually written down as design work — which is what turns a simulation into "significant design aspects applied to a real-world problem" rather than a claim that it is. `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_decision_due_week` requires the guide to be asked before the First Review, and `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_acceptance` makes the recorded acknowledgement this dossier's acceptance criterion — the point being to learn the answer while the schedule can still absorb it, and **without** converting Tier 2 or Tier 3 into required scope. *(verify: dossier committed with every listed section present, cited from the report introduction, and the guide's acknowledgement recorded with its date)*
- **PR-10** — Proposal registration status MUST be verified and recorded, with `params.deliverables.proposal_registration_owner` accountable for it. **Closed 2026-07-28 (AM-63):** `params.deliverables.proposal_registration_status` records registration as complete, confirmed by the author. Rationale, retained because the reasoning is the record: the circular makes submission of the proposal to the guide part of the **registration** process, and the copy in this repository is a blank template — which proves nothing either way, since the attachment would be blank whether or not a completed copy was submitted elsewhere. That ambiguity is exactly why it had to be checked rather than assumed in either direction: an unregistered project is not a specification problem this document can fix, and it was the one failure mode that no amount of engineering recovers from (AM-59). *(verify: registration status recorded in `params.deliverables.proposal_registration_status` with its date, before the First Review)*

## 11. Optional cross-checks (OPT)

Non-blocking. None of these may appear on the critical path (DEC-3). OPT-1 and OPT-3 further depend on a MATLAB licence, which is **being attempted but is not assured** as of 2026-07-25; if it does not land, they are dropped without prejudice and BR-2's golden vectors come from the next available rung of `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_ladder` instead (AM-22). Nothing that gates a comparison may depend on the licence.

- **OPT-1** — MATLAB 5G Toolbox reproduction of the LDPC BER curve as an independent check on BR-2, and as a generator for its golden vectors if the licence is available.
- **OPT-2** — MATLAB or symbolic treatment of channel capacity, the separation theorem and the finite-blocklength bounds behind §1 claim 2, for the thesis mathematics chapter.
- **OPT-3** — An independent MATLAB reimplementation of the JPEG 2000 + LDPC chain to cross-validate baseline accuracy at two or three SNR points.
- *(OPT-4 is retired — see §14. It was SNR-randomised training as an optional comparison; ER-2 now requires it, because the objection it answers is one BR-15's disclosure invites rather than settles.)*

## 12. Future work & mandated extension points (FW)

Not built now; the spec is shaped so each is additive rather than a redesign.

- **FW-1** — Rayleigh block and fast fading, via the channel registry (SR-5). Expected to strengthen the graceful-degradation claim, since classical schemes suffer disproportionately under fading.
- **FW-2** — λ sweep quantifying the accuracy cost of a viewable reconstruction, with λ=0 as the pure-task upper bound, via SR-8. The `lambda` column already exists in `params.artifacts.csv_schema` so this is additive.
- **FW-3** — SNR-adaptive or variable-rate coding, where the transmitter adjusts rate to measured channel state.
- **FW-4** — Alternative downstream tasks (segmentation, detection) via the task-head registry (SR-15).
- **FW-5** — Further digital semantic variants beyond the ER-9 control — learned entropy models, joint quantisation training.
- **FW-6** — Additional datasets beyond `params.datasets`.

## 13. Schedule & gates

Weeks are relative (W1 = first working week) and rescale to the actual semester length. Gates are go/no-go: a failed gate triggers its stated fallback, not an extension. Review weeks are fixed by `params.deliverables.review_weeks` and the engineering schedule is arranged so each review has evidence to show (PR-8).

| Week | Work | Gate |
|---|---|---|
| W0 | Pre-flight: §2 protocol ratified with the supervisor; DEC-9..DEC-15 closed; LDPC spike (install, exact-budget check, throughput, smallest workable payload) and golden-vector provenance fixed; PR-1 literature review begun | **G-9** |
| W1 | Environment lock and clean-install smoke (SR-21); repo scaffold, config plumbing (SR-1); dataset provenance, checksums and committed split manifests (SR-20, SR-2, SR-17); **canonical preprocessing contract before any pixel flows (SR-19)**; test-access guard and freeze manifest (SR-22); reference classifier trained from scratch on clean images (BR-8). The artifact-finetuned variant is **not** trained here — its corpus does not exist until G-8 (BR-12, AM-6) | **G-1** |
| W2 | Channel model, power normalisation and PAPR (SR-4..SR-7, SR-16); DJSCC skeleton; compute profiling | **G-7** |
| W3 | LDPC integration, BER validation with SNR conversion (BR-2), full bit accounting and packetisation (BR-10, BR-11) | **G-2** |
| W4 | Classical baseline end-to-end with budget matching and validation-split tuning (BR-3, BR-4); **First Review** package (PR-1, PR-2, PR-8) | |
| W5 | DJSCC training loop, dual head, checkpoint/resume (SR-8, SR-10); CIFAR-10 plumbing smoke path; results schema and per-image outcomes (ER-5, SR-18) | |
| W6 | ER-3 **classical-only** operating-point sweep across all ratios on the full validation split; `efficiency_ratio` and `crossover_ratio` selected; after owner audit and separate F0 authorization, artifact-finetuned classifier trained on BR-12's deterministic balanced six-variant-per-training-image sample from AM-87's complete authority-projected training-only support rather than either a post-hoc winner neighbourhood or its exhaustive Cartesian product (AM-87, AM-88) | **G-8** |
| W7 | One-seed DJSCC pilot at the selected ratio; λ calibration on the validation split over `params.learned_system.lambda_grid` (SR-9) | **G-4** |
| W8 | Final DJSCC training at the calibrated λ, across `params.evaluation.seed_pairing` cells, at **every ratio DEC-11 names** — `crossover_ratio`, `efficiency_ratio` and `low_ratio_operating_point`, up to three and two if the first two coincide. This row previously said two ratios while DEC-11 mandated three, under-scoping its own training load by half (AM-26) | |
| W9 | **G-10 first, on frozen validation curves**, before any Second Review material is written; then ER-9 task-aware digital control built, trained and validation-tuned, with H4's precision simulation; ER-2 SNR-mismatch experiment including the SNR-randomised variant | **G-10**, **G-11** |
| W10 | Paired inference implemented and tested on fixtures (ER-10); ER-4, ER-11 and ER-12 **built and rehearsed on validation**; **validation-strength pipeline rehearsal** — full grid, all systems, one seed cell, on the **validation** split and labelled as such — so the Second Review has its headline figure and W11 is de-risked without opening test; **Second Review** package (PR-8) | |
| W11 | **Freeze manifest committed (SR-22), then the single test campaign**: full ER-1 at full test split and three seed cells with paired intervals, and every other test-reading experiment in `params.evaluation.test_campaign_covers` run inside the same opening at sweep strength (ER-6) | **G-12** |
| W12 | Results frozen; Tier 1 reported either way (ER-8) | **G-5** |
| W13 | Streamlit demo and shared figure-style module (DR-1..DR-6) | |
| W14 | Stretch: Tier 2 SDR replay (HR-3, HR-6) if G-5 passed and hardware arrived; otherwise pre-recorded demonstration. Poster draft (PR-4) | |
| W15 | Thesis figures, report in prescribed format (PR-6), results audit (ER-7), novelty statement (PR-7), plagiarism report (PR-5). **Internal report freeze** per `params.deliverables.internal_report_freeze_week`. Tier 3 attempt if Tier 2 landed | **G-6** |
| W16 | **Contingency**, per `params.deliverables.contingency_week_allocation`: report completion and results audit. Deliberately allocated, not slack (AM-59) | |
| W17 | **Final Review** (17–21 Nov) and **report plus supporting material due 20 Nov** — both inside this week, which is why W15 carries the internal freeze. Viva preparation | |

- **G-1** — **Validation-only, and it gates W1's durable contracts rather than one scalar (AM-58).** Reference classifier meets the headline dataset's `clean_acc_floor` from scratch (DEC-15), in the **clean variant only**, **measured on validation** — this gate MUST execute with zero test decoder, canonicalization and inference calls, and SR-22's guard MUST be in place to prove it (AM-77). The smoke dataset's floor is reported but is advisory and does not block the gate (AM-13), and the artifact-finetuned variant is gated at G-8 instead because its corpus does not exist yet (AM-6). The gate also accepts, and fails without: dataset download and integrity with no archive or manifest provenance still pending (SR-20); the committed split manifests and their disjointness and count tests (SR-17); registry coverage for all three datasets (SR-2); config round-trip with no experiment constant hidden in source (SR-1); the canonical-pixel identity test (SR-19); the clean-install and accelerator smoke run (SR-21); and the classifier's log, checkpoint and config provenance (BR-8). Rationale: an accuracy scalar was the only thing this gate checked, while W1 produces the data, split, preprocessing and environment contracts that every later number is denominated in — and a contract that is wrong here is retrofitted into config, caches and results everywhere else. **Fallback is an ordered ladder, not a licence (AM-58):** `params.reference_classifier.fallback_ladder`, taken in order, selected on `params.reference_classifier.fallback_ladder_selection_split`, bounded by `params.reference_classifier.fallback_ladder_max_epochs`, stopping at `params.reference_classifier.fallback_ladder_stop_rule`, and subject to `params.reference_classifier.fallback_ladder_param_cap_applies` so SR-14's cap — the learned arm may not exceed the network scoring the classical arm — still binds. "Switch backbone or extend training" named no order, no bound, no selection split and no stopping rule, which permitted architecture and compute changes chosen after inspecting a gate metric.
- **G-2** — Golden vectors pass bit-exactly, LDPC BER matches a named reference curve **within `params.evaluation.ber_match_tolerance_db` on `params.evaluation.ber_match_statistic`** with the SNR conversion shown, and the BR-10 packetisation tests pass. **The reference must be a real, downloadable artifact (AM-58).** `params.baseline.ldpc_bler_reference_source` names the immutable, MIT-licensed implementation fixed in `params.baseline.ldpc_bler_reference`; its archive checksum, source files and literal-port reconstruction bind the independent flooding offset-min-sum reference used at this gate (AM-79). TS 38.212 is a specification and contains no BER/BLER curve to match, so it is not itself the reference. The named source MUST be checksummed and MUST agree with this project on every entry in `params.baseline.ldpc_bler_reference_must_match`. This gate also covers the seam details BR-14 now pins: known-answer vectors for the mapper, demapper and modulation bit interleaver at `Q_m` = 1, 2 and 4, all three CRCs in `params.baseline.crc_spec`, both base graphs, segmentation, rate matching, the LLR sign and `params.baseline.ldpc_decoder_offset`. A sign-flipped or interleaver-disabled 16-QAM fixture MUST fail loudly rather than degrade quietly. `params.baseline.packet_count_gate` also falls here: the progressive-packetisation sensitivity design is frozen at this gate (AM-58), while its execution remains after G-8 because its ratio is the future validation-selected headline ratio. The tolerance and the statistic are named because "matches published curves within tolerance" had no numeric pass condition, no named reference file and no decoder settings — so half of this gate was a judgement call while the other half was a fixture (AM-50). The comparison MUST pin the reference data and its checksum, (K, N), base graph, lifting size, modulation, decoder algorithm and its offset value, and MUST be made on waterfall displacement at a fixed BLER rather than by visual overlay. `params.deliverables.standards` now also carries TS 38.211, which defines the modulation mapping this project uses; PR-3's register MUST state that plain BPSK is a generic AWGN simulation choice and **not** NR's π/2-BPSK, so the standards claim is not read more broadly than it is meant. Fallback: descend DEC-10's ladder — pin an earlier library version, then substitute `params.baseline.ldpc_impl_fallback` behind the BR-14 seam. No comparison may be reported before this passes.
- *(G-3 is retired — see §14. It required a crossover reproduced on the CIFAR-10 smoke path, which cannot happen for the reasons in DEC-1. Superseded by G-8.)*
- **G-4** — λ calibrated per SR-9 over `params.learned_system.lambda_grid`, on one-seed pilot runs, and **before** the final multi-seed training of W8 begins. Rationale: λ is a term in the training objective, so a headline model trained ahead of this gate would have to be discarded and retrained; the schedule previously placed the headline training a week ahead of the gate that sets the objective, and scheduled no retraining afterwards (AM-7). Fallback: relaxed PSNR floor, then DEC-2 reversal to two separate models with a re-planned schedule.
- **G-5** — Tier 1 frozen: ER-1..ER-4, ER-9 and ER-10 complete with paired intervals and every §2 hypothesis decided. Passing unlocks the hardware purchase (HR-2). Failing means Tier 2/3 are abandoned and effort moves to reporting per ER-8. Note that Tier 1 completion does not require the hypotheses to be supported (§2).
- **G-6** — Tier 3 latency budget met, if attempted. Fallback: pre-recorded demonstration per DEC-14. This gate MUST NOT change the dataset: the headline dataset is frozen at G-5 and any demotion here is demo-only.
- **G-7** — Compute profile established per SR-11: achievable batch size, epoch time and peak VRAM measured on the primary device. Fallback: reduce model width or resolution before the training schedule is committed.
- **G-8** — `efficiency_ratio` and `crossover_ratio` both selected per ER-3's learned-blind rule at their two thresholds, and the classical baseline confirmed non-degenerate at each — feasible at more than one LDPC rate, with format overhead below half the budget (BR-11). This gate also **decides whether ER-1 runs at one ratio or both**, using G-9's measured decode throughput and its projected ER-1 wall clock (AM-18): if the projection shows the full-strength evaluation affordable twice within the remaining schedule, run both and report the efficiency point at the same rigour as the crossover point; if not, ER-1 runs at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` alone and the efficiency point is reported at sweep strength with that difference stated in the caption. **The comparison MUST be made on both axes in `params.compute.schedule_cost_compared_as` (AM-59):** `params.compute.max_wall_clock_hours_per_run` is a *per-run* cap that a decomposable evaluation can always satisfy by splitting, while two ratios still consume aggregate calendar time in a week that has none — so a decision made on the per-run figure alone would answer the wrong question. Deferring this to G-8 rather than settling it now is deliberate: the throughput number that decides it does not exist until the W0 spike, and committing either way beforehand would be a guess (AM-20). This gate **does not decide DEC-16** — it cannot. It sits at W6, where ER-3's sweep is classical-only and no learned model exists until W7's pilot, so there is no learned curve here to cross anything; that decision moves to G-10 (AM-33). Passing it releases two things that were previously scheduled earlier than their inputs existed: BR-12's artifact-finetuned classifier and the H2 comparison window, which is frozen from the validation curves at this point (AM-1). The classifier corpus was originally described as the qualities BR-4 selects here (AM-6), but after pass one exposed the missing breadth semantics, AM-87 requires a separately frozen and owner-audited complete authority-projected training-only support plan before F0 execution; pass-one references anchor only the original dataset/ratio/SNR-region scope. AM-88 preserves that 120-quality support and requires owner audit of a second metadata-only plan that deterministically and balanceably assigns exactly six distinct supported qualities to every one of the 8,469 training stable IDs before any materialization; the exhaustive AM-87 image × quality multiplicity is historical, not the execution corpus (AM-88). Fallback: if no ratio in `params.bandwidth.ratios` satisfies the rule on Imagenette, invoke the DEC-1 demotion to STL-10; if none satisfies it there either, report the format-floor finding as a first-class result and run the comparison at the largest available ratio. Two further clauses, both from AM-41: if the rule satisfies the efficiency threshold but no ratio satisfies the crossover threshold, resolve per ER-3's preregistered asymmetric fallback rather than improvising with the sweep table in hand; and if the rule selects the smallest ratio in `params.bandwidth.ratios`, apply `params.bandwidth.ladder_bottom_saturation_rule` — extend the ladder downward and re-run the classical-only sweep before recording the selection. `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point` is resolved here by `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_rule` rather than read as a fixed value.
- **G-11** — **ER-9 built and feasible, and H4's precision established.** At W9: the control trains, its selected transmitted dimension and quantiser width are recorded, and it hits its budget at matched `k` at every SNR in H1's region — sized against `params.digital_semantic_control.budget_sized_against`, so the raw escape is what must fit rather than an expected compressed length. This gate also runs and archives `params.evaluation.h4_mde_simulation` per `params.evaluation.h4_mde_gate`: the prospective paired-precision simulation on validation-derived discordance that says whether H4 can resolve a plausible residual at all **before** W8's final training is spent on it, and by which H4's claim is narrowed if it cannot. It further closes `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_dim_realised_by`, which §16 carries as an open factorisation (AM-56), and MUST record how many admissible (dimension, width) pairs the selected ratio actually leaves: at the bottom of the ladder that number falls to one, and a two-stage search over a single candidate is a selection in name only (AM-59). Rationale: W9 builds an entire third system — a quantiser trained through a straight-through estimator, an offline-fitted entropy model, a range coder, and budget-accounted packing into the LDPC chain — and also runs ER-2, in one week, and until now with **no gate on it at all**, while DEC-13 makes ER-9 the load-bearing half of the novelty claim and §16 warns it must not be the first thing cut. Every other high-risk item in this project has a gate. Fallback, so that a bad week degrades the control rather than deleting it: a fixed transmitted dimension and quantiser width with no entropy coder, with the omission disclosed in the methods section and in every figure caption that carries an ER-9 curve, and H4 reported as conditional on the weakened control (AM-55).
- **G-12** — **Test release.** At the start of W11, and the single most irreversible step in the project: `params.artifacts.freeze_manifest_file` is committed covering `params.evaluation.freeze_manifest_covers`, SR-22's guard is verified to have been active throughout W1–W10, and only then does the test loader open. `params.evaluation.test_campaign_is_single` makes this **one** campaign: every test-reading experiment in `params.evaluation.test_campaign_covers` runs inside it, ER-1 at full strength and the rest at sweep strength per ER-6. Rationale, and why this gate exists at all (AM-60): `params.evaluation.test_access_gate` previously named G-10, which sits at the start of W9 — so the guard would have released three weeks before anything was frozen, and ER-11 and ER-12 were scheduled at W10 while reading the test split, which is precisely the leak AM-58 moved the W10 rehearsal to validation to close. Every other irreversible step in this project has a gate; opening the test split had none. Fallback: none. If the manifest cannot be produced, the campaign does not run — that is the point of it. If a genuine code defect is found after this gate, the affected campaign is recorded as invalidated and rerun **complete**, never cell-by-cell (DEC-12, ER-8).
- **G-10** — **Crossover decision.** At the **start of W9** (moved from W10 by AM-59), on frozen validation curves and with W8's trained models in hand, DEC-16 is decided: whether the adaptive-modulation baseline produces an observable crossover, or whether the last-resort fallback — reporting learned dominance across the whole grid — is taken. Taking the fallback MUST be recorded in DEC-16 together with the evidence that the adaptive baseline was genuinely attempted first, and MUST trigger PR-8's restatement of objectives at the Second Review, which is the rubric's sanctioned modification point. This gate exists because G-8 was asked to make this decision at W6, where the sweep is classical-only and the first learned model does not exist until W7 — a gate cannot observe a crossover when only one of the two curves has been measured (AM-33). **It sits at the start of W9 rather than in W10 (AM-59)** because it can change the objectives that the Second Review package states, and deciding it during the week that package is being written means rewriting the package under review-week pressure — and, before AM-58 moved W10 onto validation, doing so in the same week a test-derived curve was on screen. Fallback: none needed; both outcomes are complete Tier 1 under §2, and this gate exists to make the choice explicit and dated rather than to permit or forbid either branch.
- **G-9** — **PASSED 2026-07-27 (AM-24, AM-25); W1 is open.** All clauses below are satisfied: the spike ran clean on the primary device, `params.compute.ldpc_decode_measured_gate` records the throughput here, the ER-1 projection exists for one ratio and for two, the TS 38.212 version is pinned, and `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_rung` names the rung with its licence question resolved. The MATLAB licence is no longer load-bearing for any of it. Original text follows. Pre-flight decisions closed: §2 dispositioned with the supervisor — completion criterion approved directly, hypotheses and statistical method explicitly delegated to the author (AM-21) — and DEC-9..DEC-15 recorded. The LDPC spike is complete — `params.baseline.ldpc_impl_version` installs and runs on the primary device, `params.baseline.ldpc_decoder` is confirmed available, the encoder is confirmed to hit an exact channel-bit budget (which BR-3 depends on), batched decode throughput and the smallest workable payload are measured and recorded, and golden-vector provenance is named per `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_gate`. The exact document version of `params.baseline.ldpc_standard` within `params.baseline.ldpc_standard_release` is pinned and recorded per `params.baseline.ldpc_standard_version_pin_gate`, since the specification is under change control and a bare title is not reproducible (AM-9). The measured decode throughput MUST be turned into a **projected ER-1 evaluation wall clock** and recorded, **for one operating ratio and for two**: ER-1 spans three systems × every entry in `params.channel.test_snr_grid_db` × the full test split × three seed cells, which is a larger and far less elastic cost than the training runs and lands at W11 with no slack behind it, so a throughput surprise has to surface here rather than there (AM-18). The two-ratio projection is what G-8 uses to decide whether the efficiency point is evaluated at full strength or at sweep strength (AM-20). MATLAB licence availability is resolved either way, settling whether OPT-1 and OPT-3 are live. Fallback: none — W1 does not begin until this passes, because every one of these decisions is expensive to reverse after code exists.

## 14. Retired requirements

Retired IDs keep their numbers reserved. Nothing may reuse them.

- ~~**G-3**~~ — retired W0; see §13.
- ~~**OPT-4**~~ — retired W1; promoted into ER-2 as a required single run (AM-45). It was never going to be run as a non-blocking option, and it is the counterpart BR-15 names for the adaptation asymmetry.

## 15. Non-goals

Explicitly out of scope. Listed so that scope creep is a visible spec change.

- Bit-exact or perceptually-optimal reconstruction as an objective in its own right — reconstruction exists to serve the task and the demo.
- Reinforcement learning of any kind. The system is trained end-to-end by supervised gradient descent.
- Multi-user, MIMO, relay or interference channels; a single point-to-point link is assumed.
- Video, audio or real-time streaming sources.
- On-device training or adaptation; edge nodes run frozen models.
- Adversarial robustness, security or privacy of the learned representation.
- Beating state-of-the-art DJSCC results. The comparison of record is against the classical baseline, not against the literature; the novelty claimed is the attribution decomposition and the overhead-controlled baseline (DEC-13, PR-7), not headline numbers.
- Energy measurement. The systems are matched on channel uses and average power, which is equal transmitted energy by construction; no power-meter claim is made (§1).

## 16. Open items and carried risks

Working state, not normative: what is still provisional, what is pending, and what risk is knowingly being carried. This section shrinks as gates pass. It exists so that a reader — or the author after a gap — can tell at a glance which numbers in §4 are measurements and which are estimates.

**Pending before W1 (all inside G-9).** ~~Pending.~~ **All three closed as of 2026-07-27; G-9 has passed and W1 is open.** Retained struck through rather than deleted, because what was uncertain before W0 is part of the record.

- ~~§2's rewrite needs **supervisor ratification**.~~ **Closed 2026-07-25.** Every part of §2 now has a disposition (AM-19, AM-21). The **completion criterion** — Tier 1 is complete when the protocol has been run properly, regardless of which way the result falls — is **approved directly**, with the added instruction to pursue a crossover as hard as the rules allow and an explicit statement that an unfavourable outcome is acceptable. That was the only part of §2 that originated in the approved proposal and therefore the only part its author could not redefine alone, so this is the item closing rather than shrinking. The **four hypotheses, the paired-inference procedure, the no-weakening rule and the learned-blind operating-point rule** are **explicitly delegated** as methodology. That is an answer, not a gap — but see AM-21 for what it makes load-bearing.
- ~~The **LDPC spike**.~~ **Closed 2026-07-27 (AM-24).** All seven checks pass on the primary device. The load-bearing item held: the encoder hits an exact channel-bit budget across all 180 configurations *of the grid as it then stood*, so BR-3's equal-channel-uses claim survives contact with the library and not merely with arithmetic — subject to BR-10's BG1 clamp. That grid is now 216 configurations and the current arithmetic lives in `spec/evidence/packetisation_record.json`; the spike record is labelled historical accordingly (AM-58, AM-59). Throughput is `params.compute.ldpc_decode_cb_per_s_measured`, which makes ER-1 comfortably affordable at either operating-point count, and the smallest workable payload is 16 information bits, far below anything this project needs. What the spike also bought, and what would have been expensive later, is two silent-failure modes found before any code depends on them: the decoder's LLR sign convention and BG1's minimum coderate, both now fixed at the BR-14 seam.
- ~~**Golden-vector provenance** for BR-2.~~ **Closed 2026-07-27 (AM-25):** rung 2 is confirmed by demonstration and the licensing question is resolved by not vendoring. Original text, kept because the reasoning that got here is the record. No longer contingent on the MATLAB licence (AM-22): the licence is being attempted but not assured, so `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_source_ladder` now names four rungs and G-9 records which one was used. **Rung 2's technical half is confirmed (AM-23):** `srsRAN_matlab` carries `srsLDPCEncoderUnittest.m`, `srsLDPCRateMatcherUnittest.m` **and** `srsLDPCSegmenterUnittest.m`, so vectors exist for the rate-matched output BR-2 also requires — and the segmenter generator is a bonus, since DEC-10 makes segmentation this project's own code with no library to check it against. **Its licensing half is open and is a decision, not a fact:** the generators are BSD-2-Clause but need MATLAB to run, which is the very thing rung 2 exists to avoid, while the already-generated vector data lives in `srsRAN_Project` under AGPLv3. Committing that data into this repository and submitting it academically is distribution. **Resolved 2026-07-27 (AM-25), and the premise was partly wrong.** The vector data is not committed to `srsRAN_Project` at all — it ships as a per-release asset, which is why the licence question has a cheap answer: **do not vendor**. The fixture is built by a committed fetch-and-convert script that pins the release, verifies `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_sha256`, and leaves the derived `.npz` untracked. The offline-reproducibility cost that made this option look expensive is paid off separately by `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_offline_floor` — rung 4, committed and always run — so the bit-exact test never depends on the network being up. Rung 4 was always the floor nobody can block; making it *additional* rather than a fallback is what lets rung 1 through 3 be network-dependent without weakening G-2. The remaining exposure is upstream, not legal, and is recorded below.

**Provisional values that a gate will replace.** Each is an estimate standing in for a measurement; none should be cited as a result.

- `bandwidth.crossover_ratio` = `r_1_3` and `bandwidth.efficiency_ratio` = `r_1_6`, provisional until G-8 selects them by ER-3's learned-blind rule; `bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point` = `r_1_12`, provisional until G-8 resolves it by `bandwidth.low_ratio_rule`. A third key, bandwidth.core_ratio, was listed here until AM-26 removed it: AM-20 replaced it with the two selections above and no gate ever resolved it, so it sat provisional-forever while BR-1 and the W6/W8 schedule rows still pointed at it. **The estimate below is a bitrate intuition applied to an accuracy criterion, and should be read as the weakest number in this section.** It comes from the classical ceiling reaching clean accuracy at roughly 1.5–2.0 bpp for Imagenette — which is the bitrate at which JPEG 2000 becomes *visually transparent*, whereas ER-3's rule asks for top-1 accuracy within `t` points of clean, and classification tolerates several times more compression than transparent reconstruction does. The rule may therefore be satisfied much further down the ladder than these values suggest, possibly at every rung, which is what `bandwidth.ladder_bottom_saturation_rule` exists to catch. Pulling the other way: ER-3's sweep is necessarily scored by BR-8's **clean-trained** classifier, since BR-12's artifact-finetuned variant does not exist until G-8 releases it, and that domain shift depresses the measured ceiling and pushes the selection back toward larger ratios. The two effects are of unknown relative size, which is why this is insured against rather than predicted. Under the original QPSK-only cap that put the crossover near r ≈ 2/5; with DEC-16's adaptive modulation, 16-QAM doubles bits per channel use and halves the requirement to **r ≈ 1/5**, so `r_1_6` (1.67 bpp at 16-QAM rate 5/6) is the provisional **efficiency point** and `r_1_3` (3.33 bpp) the provisional **crossover point**. Those are now two separate selections at two thresholds rather than one (AM-20), because the 5 pp threshold admits a classical ceiling near 0.83 while the learned ceiling is estimated at 0.85–0.87 — so the looser rule locates the tightest healthy budget but not necessarily a budget where the curves can cross. If G-8 finds both thresholds selecting the same ratio, that is a clean outcome and not a failure of the design: it means the classical ceiling rises steeply enough between ratios that no separate crossover point exists, and the project carries one operating point as originally written.
- `learned_system.lambda_core` = 1.0, provisional until G-4 calibrates it per SR-9.
- ~~`deliverables.review_dates_status` — the calendar dates are not confirmed.~~ **Closed 2026-07-28 (AM-59).** The dates were read directly from `params.deliverables.review_dates_source`, which carries no extractable text and had to be rendered and read as an image — which is why this sat open for three amendment rounds while being answerable in ten minutes. `params.deliverables.review_dates` now records all four, and this entry's guess was **half wrong**: W4 and W10 were right, but the Final Review is 17–21 November, which is **W17, not W16**. The slack it anticipated is real, and is now `params.deliverables.contingency_week` allocated per `params.deliverables.contingency_week_allocation` to report completion and the results audit — rather than to W9 and W11 as this entry proposed, since W9's protection came instead from moving G-10 to the start of it. Two consequences stay visible: the report deadline (20 November) falls **inside** Final Review week, so `params.deliverables.internal_report_freeze_week` puts the internal freeze two weeks earlier; and the circular heads its own table "Tentative Dates", so these are dated-and-sourced rather than immutable and MUST be re-checked before PR-2's Gantt is committed.
- `datasets.*.clean_acc_floor`, set to from-scratch estimates under DEC-15. If G-1 shows them wrong, move them **at G-1** as a recorded spec change rather than quietly missing them later.

**Risks carried knowingly.**

- **Sionna 2.0.x is a recent framework rewrite** — PHY/SYS moved from TensorFlow to PyTorch in 2.0.0, with 2.0.1 released March 2026 — so early-version defects are plausible. This is the main risk accepted in DEC-10. Mitigations are already in place: BR-2's golden vectors turn a defect into a failing test, BR-14's seam makes substitution cheap, and DEC-10 states the fallback ladder. Pin the exact version in the lockfile required by SR-12.
- **BR-2's upstream vector source is an archived repository.** **New 2026-07-27 (AM-25).** `srsRAN_Project` transitioned to OCUDU in December 2025 and the GitHub repository is archived, its default branch now carrying only a notice; `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_successor` records where development moved. The release assets and tags remain, and `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_url` pins an immutable one, so nothing is broken today — but an archived repository is a source that can be taken down without notice, and the fetch-and-verify design in BR-2 deliberately does not cache the data in git. This is a carried risk rather than a blocker for exactly one reason: `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_offline_floor` is committed and always runs, so if the asset vanishes G-2 degrades from broad MATLAB-provenance coverage to a single hand-derived case rather than failing outright. If the upstream disappears before the fixture is first built, the mitigation is to descend to rung 3, and to record which rung was used. **Corrected and worsened 2026-07-27 (AM-30).** This entry previously offered "take the vectors from the OCUDU repository under its own licence" as a mitigation. That mitigation does not exist. An external review proposed going further and repointing `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_url` at an OCUDU release asset, on the grounds that the successor ships the same MATLAB-generated vectors under BSD-3 rather than AGPLv3. Checked against OCUDU's own documentation: the licence half is right — OCUDU is a Linux Foundation project under the BSD-3-Clause Open MPI variant — but **the asset does not exist**. Vector tests moved out of the main repository into a separate `ocudu-matlab` companion plugin, and OCUDU's MATLAB tutorial states that running the testing suite "requires a working and licensed copy of MATLAB and its 5G Toolbox". There are no pre-generated vectors to download, so BSD-3 covers *generators*, not data, and repointing would trade a working rung for a licence dependency — precisely what rung 2 exists to avoid. The consequence is that the carried risk is **larger** than recorded, not smaller: the successor project publishes no replacement rung at all, so if the pinned srsRAN release asset is withdrawn, rung 2 is gone permanently. Two actions follow. The pinned asset was verified reachable on 2026-07-27 and MUST be **fetched and archived locally, outside git**, before the fixture is first built rather than at W3 when it is needed. And `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_offline_floor` — rung 4, committed, always run — carries more weight than AM-25 assumed, because it is now the only rung nobody can revoke. This is the second time an assumption about where these vectors live has proved wrong (AM-25 was the first); the lesson recorded for next time is that a claim about a third party's release artifacts is checkable in minutes and MUST be checked rather than inherited.
- **Python 3.14 versus the CUDA wheel set.** **Resized 2026-07-25 (AM-23); confirmed empirically 2026-07-27 (AM-24) — `torch 2.13.0+cu130` and `sionna-no-rt 2.0.1` install and run on Python 3.14.6 with CUDA live on the primary device, so the residual risk is now only that a future resolution silently reverts to the CPU build.** This was recorded as a *minimum*-version concern — Sionna declares 3.11+, PyTorch 2.9+, both satisfied. The real exposure is the opposite end: whether CUDA-enabled wheels are built for `cp314` at all. They are — `torch-2.13.0+cu130-cp314-cp314-manylinux_2_28_x86_64.whl` is present on the cu130 index — **but a plain `pip install torch` resolves to the CPU build**, which is the actual trap and is what pytorch#169929 reported before it was closed as not planned. Installs MUST pass `--index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu130`, and any environment check MUST assert `torch.version.cuda is not None` rather than merely importing successfully. Two corrections to what this entry previously claimed: pinning a 3.12 or 3.13 interpreter does **not** "cost nothing else" — neither exists on the primary device, so the fallback is itself an install; and `sionna-no-rt` is preferable to `sionna`, because this project needs PHY only (the AWGN channel is ours behind SR-5's registry) and that variant omits the ray tracer build entirely.
- **A crossover may not appear even with the adaptive baseline and the tighter threshold.** DEC-16 removes the artificial modulation cap and AM-20 adds a selection threshold aimed squarely at crossover observability — together the strongest legitimate levers available — but both systems are converging on the same wall: the classifier's accuracy on a perfect image. If the learned system also reaches that wall, the curves meet rather than cross, and no threshold can conjure a gap that is not there. The margin in question is 1–3 percentage points, so this remains genuinely uncertain. G-8 decides this; §2 already makes the outcome a complete Tier 1 either way, and the reconstruction-quality crossover is the documented fallback figure. Do not respond to a missing crossover by weakening the learned system.
- **16-QAM adds baseline engineering that must not be skimped.** Soft-demapping to log-likelihood ratios is materially harder than the QPSK sign decision, and a subtly wrong demapper degrades the baseline invisibly — which would defeat the entire purpose of DEC-16 and hand an examiner a real objection. BR-2 now requires per-modulation validation for this reason; budget two to three days at W3, not an afternoon.
- **ER-9 is both a novelty claim and an attribution requirement.** Because DEC-13 leans on it for rubric novelty, it should not be the first thing cut under schedule pressure. Its risk is **not** an absence of prior art: this file previously called it "the one required experiment with no prior art to copy", which is false — quantised task-feature transmission over digital chains is well covered (DeepJSCC-Q, VQ-DeepISC, standards-compatible semantic frameworks, and the channel-adaptive digital semantic line), and that is good news, because there are designs to borrow rather than invent (AM-10). The real risk is narrower: ER-9 only isolates joint coding if it is *matched* to the learned system rather than merely adjacent to it, which is what ER-9's shared-front-end requirement now enforces (AM-5), and PR-1 MUST cite the prior art so PR-7's novelty statement claims the decomposition rather than the system.
- **ER-1's evaluation cost is larger and less elastic than its training cost.** Three systems, **21** SNR points, the full test split and three seed cells, with per-image JPEG 2000 decode and LDPC decode at `params.baseline.ldpc_max_iters` iterations — and it lands at W11 with only W12 behind it. The training budget has slack (gradient accumulation, fewer epochs, the STL-10 demotion); the evaluation budget mostly does not, because ER-6 already forbids subsetting ER-1. G-9 now requires a projected ER-1 wall clock from the spike's measured throughput so this surfaces in W0 (AM-18). **What that projection does and does not cover (AM-29).** `params.compute.er1_projected_ldpc_decode_scope` is on the label for a reason: the figure is LDPC decode alone, and it excludes JPEG 2000 encode and decode, `params.compute.er1_classifier_forwards_two_variants` classifier forward passes, and the 16-QAM soft demapper. The classifier passes are minutes; the JPEG 2000 half is not, if it shells out — at typical process-spawn cost, `params.compute.er1_j2k_test_decodes` decodes is comparable to the whole LDPC budget on its own, which is why BR-1 requires a library binding and a cache rather than a subprocess per image. **All four figures were computed against an 18-point grid and are corrected by AM-59:** AM-52 added three grid points without touching any prose that counted them, so 424k forwards, 212k decodes and the 2.07/4.14 h projections were all understated by a sixth. `params.compute.er1_projected_ldpc_decode_basis` now records what the projection is built from rather than leaving a bare scalar, and `params.compute.er1_projected_ldpc_decode_hours_floor_one_ratio` records the other end of the envelope: the headline figure assumes the **worst-case** 11 code blocks per image that 16-QAM rate 5/6 needs, while the noisy end of the grid — where BR-4 selects BPSK and the channel-bit budget is a quarter as large — needs two. The realised cost lands between 0.44 h and 2.42 h per ratio and is measured at W3/W4, not scaled from a single multiplier. `params.compute.er1_projected_total_hours_status` records that the end-to-end figure is still owed, measured at W3/W4. One thing that does *not* threaten the projection: `params.compute.ldpc_decode_early_termination` is false — the decoder runs a fixed `params.baseline.ldpc_max_iters` iterations — so the measured throughput is worst-case with respect to SNR and the low-SNR half of the grid will not be slower than the high-SNR half.
- **ER-9's transmitted dimension names a mechanism it does not specify.** **New 2026-07-28 (AM-56).** `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_dim_realised_by` says the dimension is reached by output channel count and adaptive pooling, which is underdetermined: at Imagenette's 40×40 encoder grid, a target of 1024 real values admits many (spatial size, channel count) factorisations and the specification names none of them, nor the order the two reductions apply in. This is the same gap class AM-48 closed for the learned encoder, left open here because ER-9 is not built until W9 and the two-stage selection in `params.digital_semantic_control.selection_search` may itself constrain the answer. It MUST be closed before W9 and is a natural clause for G-11 to check. Recording it rather than solving it now is deliberate — but note that `conv_downsample_to_k_symbols` sat unspecified for six amendment rounds before AM-48, which is how long this class of gap survives when nobody writes it down.
- **H4 may sit at or under its own detection floor.** **New 2026-07-28 (AM-57); timing corrected by AM-70.** With `params.datasets.imagenette160.test_images` paired outcomes at one-sided 2.5% and 80% power, the minimum detectable paired difference is roughly 1.4 pp at 10% discordance and 3.2 pp at 50% — and the residual H4 exists to measure is plausibly 1–2 pp. This is not fixable by resampling: images and channel draws are already averaged over, and only genuinely independent training runs buy power, which the three-cell design and an 8 GB laptop GPU do not supply. It is therefore **declared rather than solved**: `params.evaluation.h4_mde_simulation` establishes the achievable precision on validation before the test split is opened at G-12, and H4's claim is narrowed at `params.evaluation.h4_mde_gate` if the design cannot resolve a plausible residual. The risk being carried is that H4 returns "unsupported" for a power reason rather than a scientific one; the mitigation is that this will be known, in writing, before the test split is opened, so the null is interpretable instead of ambiguous. DEC-13 leans on ER-9 for the novelty marks, so an *uninterpretable* H4 is the outcome to avoid — an honestly underpowered one is still a finding.
- **H3's full-grid slope can under-detect a humped gap.** **New 2026-07-28 (AM-57).** H3 fits `params.evaluation.gap_trend_test` over the whole grid, which is what lets it claim something about the noisy half. The cost, accepted deliberately in preference to refitting over the high-SNR region alone: if both systems sit near chance at −8 dB, the gap can *grow* before it contracts, and a single line through that shape can read flat — failing H3 while the systems genuinely converged. The alternative was to fit only above `params.channel.train_snr_db_fixed` and rename H3 to net high-SNR contraction, which is immune to the hump but abandons the half of the grid where the effect is largest, over six grid points. The chosen form keeps the broader claim and accepts the false-negative risk, which is the direction a secondary hypothesis should err in. If the measured curve is visibly humped, report the shape rather than only the slope.
- **ER-9's search space collapses at the bottom of the ladder.** **New 2026-07-28 (AM-58).** Broadening BR-10's proof obligation from the three provisional live ratios to every ladder rung showed that admissible (dimension, width) pairs fall from 23 at Imagenette's `r_1_3` to 7 at STL-10's `r_1_12`, 3 at `r_1_24` and **1** at `r_1_48`. The control stays feasible everywhere, which is the load-bearing result — but `params.digital_semantic_control.selection_search` is a two-stage search, and at the bottom rung it has one candidate, so ER-9's "chosen on validation" claim would be vacuous there. This interacts with `params.bandwidth.ladder_bottom_saturation_rule`: extending the ladder downward to protect ER-3's selection can strand ER-9. G-11 records the count for the selected ratio so this surfaces as a measured fact rather than as a surprise at W9.
- **The W14 stretch row assumes hardware ordered after G-5 arrives in time.** DEC-14 already makes this upside rather than plan, so the risk is to the stretch goal only, never to Tier 1 (HR-5).

## 17. Amendment record

What changed in this document after it first said something else, and why. Permanent and normative as history: `AM` IDs are retired in place, never renumbered or deleted (§8). Each entry names the items it touched, the defect that motivated the change, and who raised it — so a later reader can tell what an external review caught from what an internal audit caught, and so no settled question is re-litigated from scratch.

This section is append-only in one direction: an amendment that is itself later amended gets a **new** `AM` entry citing the old one, rather than an edit to the old entry.

**On the `EXT-n` identifiers.** They are labels, not an ordinal count of review passes. Numbering begins at `EXT-3` because the review passes behind this document's pre-§17 revision — the four that converged on five blockers and took it to 97 requirements — predate the amendment record and are not individually numbered here. So `EXT-3`..`EXT-6` are the four reviews the `AM` record covers, not the third through sixth reviews the project has had. Stated because two summaries in this repository counted them differently.

**Process rule (AM-47, mechanised by AM-62 and broadened by AM-76).** An amendment that changes what a parameter *means* — not merely its value — MUST state which sections were re-read against it, and **§13 and every `verify` clause are always on that list**. `tools/check_doc_consistency.py` MUST pass afterwards: it enforces the back-reference half of this rule across the **current hand-written documentation** — root Markdown, `configs/**/*.md`, `docs/**/*.md`, `spec/evidence/**/*.md`, `spec/SPEC.md`, `requirements.txt`, and `requirements*.in`; generated `requirements*.lock` files, generated specification views, external audits and course material are never scanned. A historical plan is excluded only when its first line is exactly `<!-- capstone-doc-status: historical-plan -->`, its next line carries the exact visible historical-snapshot banner defined by AM-76, and that banner's Markdown link resolves relative to the plan to repository-root `NEXT.md`; otherwise it stays in current-document scanning and produces an explicit banner finding.
