Rationale: AM-14 established that the generator's arithmetic check cannot catch cross-section inconsistency, because a stale citation still resolves. That lesson has since cost four further defects across two rounds — AM-26, AM-31, AM-33 and the schema half of AM-38 — and every one was an amendment correctly applied where it was noticed and never propagated to the schedule or to a verify clause. Naming the places most likely to go stale is cheaper than finding them by review.

**Amendment round 1 — 2026-07-25, pre-W0.** Sources: `EXT-3`, a full-specification review by ChatGPT returning a "commit but do not freeze" verdict; and `INT-1`, the internal audit run while adjudicating it. Roughly a third of `EXT-3` was adopted. Its central statistical objection (that H1's run rule has an inflated false-positive rate) was rejected on the arithmetic in AM-4, and three of its recommendations restated decisions already settled in DEC-10, DEC-14 and G-9. `INT-1` found five further defects, two of which — AM-14 and AM-15 — were more consequential than most of `EXT-3`.

- **AM-1** — **H2's comparison window was undefined.** Changed: §2 H2 now selects the window on the validation split, using the classical curve, over the endpoint pairs the grid admits at `params.evaluation.cliff_window_db`, then freezes those endpoints for the test evaluation; added `cliff_window_db` and `cliff_window_selection` to the evaluation parameters. Why: "the steepest 4 dB window of the grid" named neither the curve that defines steepness, nor the split it is chosen on, nor how an irregularly-spaced grid admits a 4 dB window at all — so H2 could have been settled after seeing test results, which is the one place DEC-12's validation-only selection rule had a hole. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-2** — **H3 had no executable pass condition.** Changed: §2 H3 replaces "contracts monotonically (within interval width)" with a negative weighted-least-squares slope of the paired gap against SNR whose bootstrap interval excludes zero; added `gap_trend_test`. Why: strict monotonicity is refuted by any single point of measurement noise, and "within interval width" did not say how much deviation was tolerated, so H3 had no red/green answer and would have been argued rather than decided. Rejected alternative: EXT-3 proposed demoting H3 to exploratory, which discards a claim that costs one regression to state properly. Raised by: EXT-3 (diagnosis), INT-1 (remedy).
- **AM-3** — **H1 reported no effect size.** Changed: §2 H1 keeps the three-consecutive-point run rule as the decision procedure and adds `h1_effect_size`, the mean paired accuracy difference across the whole region at or below `params.channel.train_snr_db_fixed`, reported with a `params.evaluation.ci` interval. Why: the run rule answers whether there is separation but never how much, and a single preregistered scalar over a fixed region is both the number a reader wants and immune to any objection about run structure or window selection. Raised by: EXT-3 (as a replacement for H1; adopted as an addition instead, per AM-4).
- **AM-4** — **H1's run rule was defended rather than replaced.** Changed: §2 gains a family-wise error note stating the bound explicitly; H1's decision rule is unchanged. Why: EXT-3 argued the rule scans 11 candidate runs and therefore inflates the false-positive rate, and recommended replacing it with a simultaneous bootstrap band or a single scalar. The arithmetic is the reverse — under the global null the probability that any run of three clears a one-sided 0.025 interval is at most 11 × 0.025³ ≈ 1.7 × 10⁻⁴ under independence and 0.025 under perfect dependence, both stricter than a single-point test — so requiring a run *is* the multiplicity control and replacing it would loosen the primary hypothesis, not tighten it. EXT-3 also described the grid as 16 points; it holds 18, of which 13 are in H1's region. Recorded here because this objection is likely to recur. Raised by: EXT-3 (rejected), INT-1 (arithmetic).
- **AM-5** — **ER-9 could not be evaluated as specified.** Changed: ER-9 rewritten to share `params.digital_semantic_control.shared_with_learned` with the learned system and differ only in the channel interface, scored by its own task head; `digital_semantic_control` added to the parameters; `digital_semantic_control` removed from `params.reference_classifier.shared_by` and from BR-8. Why: ER-9 was required to be "scored by the same frozen classifier", but that classifier is a ResNet-18 over images while ER-9 transmits a feature stream — the two cannot be connected. The only readings that type-checked were that ER-9 secretly reconstructs an image, which makes it not a task-feature system, or that its features are activations of the frozen classifier itself, which shares no architecture with the learned system and so still leaves H4 confounded by capacity, objective and architecture rather than isolating the channel interface. Entropy coding was retained rather than dropped: it sits inside the LDPC-protected payload under the TB CRC, so the stream-desynchronisation objection is already absorbed by BR-6 and BR-13, and removing it would weaken the one system whose purpose is to deny joint coding credit — making H4 easier to support, which is the error direction ER-8 prohibits. It is bounded instead by `entropy_model_learned_permitted: false`, leaving learned entropy models in FW-5. Raised by: EXT-3 (architecture underspecified), INT-1 (the scoring contradiction and the entropy-strength argument).
- **AM-6** — **BR-12's classifier was scheduled before the data it trains on exists.** Changed: BR-12 gains `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_gate` (G-8) and `artifact_finetune_corpus`; §13 W1 no longer trains it; G-1 no longer gates on it; G-8 releases it. Why: BR-12 requires fine-tuning on artifacts *at the operating quality*, but the operating ratio is not selected until G-8 in W6 and the per-SNR codec configurations not until BR-4 in W4, so the W1 training had no corpus to use. Separately, because BR-4 selects a different codec configuration at each SNR, "the operating quality" was not a single quality at all; the corpus is now the union across H1's region, which costs one training run rather than one per SNR point. Raised by: EXT-3 (the ordering), INT-1 (the which-quality ambiguity).
- **AM-7** — **λ was calibrated after the training it governs, and the retraining was never scheduled.** Changed: §13 reordered so W7 is a one-seed pilot plus λ calibration at G-4 and W8 is the final multi-seed training; SR-9 cites the new `params.learned_system.lambda_grid` and states that final training follows the gate; G-4 records the ordering constraint. Why: the schedule ran headline training at W7 and calibrated λ at W8, but λ is a term in the loss, so the W7 models were trained against an objective the project had not yet chosen — and no week afterwards was allocated to retraining them, while W9–W11 all assume trained models exist. Separately, SR-9 referred to "the calibration search" without bounding it, leaving G-4's training count open-ended; `lambda_grid` fixes it at five pilot runs. Raised by: EXT-3 (ordering), INT-1 (missing retraining slot, unbounded search).
- **AM-8** — **JPEG 2000 exact-byte rate control does not exist.** Changed: `j2k_rate_control` renamed from "exact_target_bytes" to `largest_codestream_within_budget`, with `j2k_rate_control_method`, `j2k_emitted_size_authoritative` and `j2k_container` added; BR-1 now drives and verifies it; DEC-9's convenience clause corrected in place. Why: verified against the OpenJPEG `opj_compress` manual — the tool exposes a per-layer compression ratio and a per-layer PSNR target, mutually exclusive, and nothing that guarantees an exact emitted byte count, so a spec built on that guarantee would have failed at W4. The fix costs a cached search and is scientifically neutral, because BR-3 only ever needed the codestream to fit the budget, not to equal it. `j2k_container` was added at the same time because raw codestream and JP2 container overheads differ by tens of bytes against a budget of hundreds, which BR-11 reports and which was previously unspecified. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-9** — **The channel-coding standard was cited without a version.** Changed: `ldpc_standard_release` and `ldpc_standard_version_pin_gate` added; G-9 pins the exact document version. Why: TS 38.212 is under change control, so a bare title does not identify what was implemented — a gap for PR-3's standards register and for SR-12's reproducibility claim. The exact version is pinned at G-9 rather than guessed here, alongside the golden-vector provenance that gate already fixes. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-10** — **The novelty position asserted things about a literature that had not been reviewed.** Changed: DEC-13 restated as a single contribution sentence, with its "not reported in the literature" clause made conditional on PR-1; PR-7 requires every novelty assertion to resolve to a PR-1 reference and forbids claiming graceful degradation, cliff-avoidance or task-aware digital semantics as novel; §16's "the one required experiment with no prior art to copy" deleted as false. Also corrected a stale cross-reference in DEC-13 pointing at §13 for the state-of-the-art disclaimer, which lives in §15. Why: quantised task-feature transmission over digital chains is well covered by prior work, and an examiner who knows the field would find the claim in one search. Narrowing the claim costs nothing — the controlled three-way comparison and the bit accounting are still defensible — while overclaiming puts the rubric's novelty marks at risk on a point that cannot be defended. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-11** — **The seed hierarchy could have been reported as stronger than three seeds support.** Changed: ER-10 gains three rules — the classical arm carries no training-seed variance component and MUST NOT be replicated across cells as independent baseline runs; McNemar is computed within a seed cell and never pooled across cells; per-seed curves and the between-seed range are reported. Why: 10,000 image-level bootstrap resamples estimate test-image uncertainty well and say almost nothing about training-run uncertainty, which rests on three seeds; and pooling per-image outcomes across seeds would treat repeated measurements of the same images as independent observations, inflating the effective sample size threefold. Three seeds remain acceptable for a capstone — what changes is that the spread is shown rather than absorbed into an interval that mostly reflects image count. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-12** — **The learned system was to be trained at five ratios to inform a decision that cannot see it.** Changed: ER-3 states the sweep is classical-only and that learned models are trained at params.bandwidth.core_ratio (de-backticked by AM-26, which removed that key; the entry's claim is unchanged) and `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point` only; DEC-11 reworded from "per bandwidth ratio". Why: ER-3's operating-point rule is learned-blind by construction, and nothing in §2 requires a learned model at every ratio, so three of the five ratios bought roughly nine training runs that no hypothesis or gate consumes. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-13** — **Two gates and one curve were more expensive than the questions they answered.** Changed: BR-1 scopes the secondary JPEG curve to the core ratio at one seed; BR-8 and G-1 make the smoke dataset's `clean_acc_floor` advisory rather than blocking. Why: the JPEG curve is context for what practitioners deploy and bears no hypothesis, and DEC-1 already makes CIFAR-10 a plumbing path whose accuracy nothing in §2 depends on — so gating W2 on a from-scratch 93% CIFAR-10 classifier bought schedule risk for no result. Raised by: EXT-3.
- **AM-14** — **ER-3 required more validation images than any dataset has.** Changed: ER-3 now sweeps on the full validation split instead of `params.evaluation.test_subset_size`. Why: that parameter is 2000 and sizes a subset of the *test* split under ER-6, but ER-3 cited it as a count of *validation* images — and `val_images` is 1000 for Imagenette, 500 for STL-10 and 5000 only for the smoke dataset. The requirement was arithmetically impossible on both headline datasets and would have failed the first time anyone ran it. The generator's arithmetic check does not cover this class of error, so §4 and §7 must be read against each other by hand. Raised by: INT-1.
- **AM-15** — **The H4 control was handicapped in the direction that inflates H4.** Changed: BR-9 no longer pins ER-9 to `params.baseline.core_modulation`; ER-9's transport is tuned by BR-4's rule over the same modulations and code rates the baseline may select, and the fixed-modulation curve is retained as a comparator rather than a cap. Why: BR-4 lets the classical baseline adapt down to BPSK at the noisy end — roughly 3 dB of extra reach, squarely inside H1's region — while ER-9 was fixed at QPSK. ER-9 exists to deny the learned system credit for joint coding, so weakening it makes H4 easier to support; that is precisely the asymmetry DEC-16's governing rule and ER-8 forbid on the baseline, and the rule applies to a control for the same reason. Raised by: INT-1.
- **AM-16** — **BR-4's analytic sweep and ER-10's paired inference could not both hold.** Changed: BR-4 states that analytic composition is for selection only, and that the frozen configuration is evaluated on test by per-image simulation sharing BR-7's noise draws. Why: BR-4 forbade "a full per-image channel simulation of every cell", while BR-7 requires identical per-image noise realisations across systems and ER-10 requires per-image paired outcomes — an analytic block-error rate supplies neither. Left unresolved, the headline comparison would have had no per-image outcomes to pair and §2 could not have been decided. Raised by: INT-1.
- **AM-17** — **The seed conjunction was ambiguous by a factor of three.** Changed: `params.evaluation.seed_pairing` added and ER-1 states that train and channel seeds are zipped into three cells, not crossed into nine. Why: "repeated over train_seeds and channel_seeds" admits both readings, and they differ threefold in evaluation cost — the dominant cost in the project. §16's own estimate of roughly 1.1M code-block decodes for ER-1 was computed against three cells, so the spec already assumed the zipped reading in one place while implying the crossed reading in another. Raised by: INT-1.
- **AM-18** — **The dominant compute cost was unbudgeted and unmeasured.** Changed: G-9 requires the LDPC spike's measured throughput to be turned into a projected ER-1 evaluation wall clock; §16 records the risk. Why: attention was on the training budget, which has slack in epochs, batch size and the STL-10 demotion. ER-1's evaluation has almost none — ER-6 forbids subsetting it — and it lands at W11 with a single week behind it. Measuring throughput at W0 without projecting ER-1 against it leaves the one inelastic cost undiscovered until the week it has to be paid. Raised by: INT-1.
- **AM-19** — **The success criterion was ratified by the supervisor.** Changed: DEC-16 records the supervisor's position — pursue a crossover as hard as the rules allow, fall back to reporting dominance across the grid if it does not appear — together with an explicit statement of what that instruction does *not* authorise; §16's pending ratification item closed and replaced with the narrower question of whether the sign-off covered §2 as a whole or the crossover clause alone. Why: the "curves must cross" wording originated in the accepted proposal, so §2's rewrite could not be self-ratified by its author without looking, in retrospect, like a target moved to fit a result; raising it before any data existed was the entire defence and that defence has now been spent successfully. The guardrail is recorded alongside it because "try your best for a crossover" is the sentence most likely to be misread later, under schedule pressure, as permission to weaken the learned system — which ER-8 prohibits, which DEC-16's governing rule prohibits, and which would make a crossover worth less than none. Raised by: supervisor (ratification), INT-1 (the guardrail).
- **AM-20** — **One selection threshold could not serve both the efficiency claim and the crossover claim.** Changed: ER-3 now applies its learned-blind rule at two thresholds, `efficiency_ratio_threshold_pp` (5) and `crossover_ratio_threshold_pp` (2), selecting `efficiency_ratio` and `crossover_ratio`; `headline_ratio` added and resolved to the crossover point; ER-1 evaluated there; DEC-11 permits up to three training ratios; G-8 selects both and defers the question of whether ER-1 runs at one ratio or two; G-9's wall-clock projection is computed for both cases. Why: the original 5 pp threshold admits a classical ceiling near 0.83 while the learned ceiling is estimated at 0.85–0.87, so the rule could select a ratio at which no crossover exists — and §16 in fact expected it to select `r_1_6`, which is roughly that ratio. Since ER-1 is the only full-test-split, multi-seed, paired-interval experiment and runs at a single ratio, a crossover living one ratio above the selection would have appeared only in a one-seed sweep figure, resting the most contested result on the weakest evidence in the project. Both thresholds are lookups against the same classical sweep table, so evaluating two costs nothing at selection time, and they may coincide. Tightening a threshold hands the baseline more budget, which DEC-16 permits expressly; the rule stays learned-blind at both settings. The residual cost — whether ER-1 doubles — is deferred to G-8 because the decode-throughput number that decides it does not exist until the W0 spike. Raised by: INT-1 (the tension), user (the decision to carry both points).
- **AM-21** — **§2 fully dispositioned; the preregistration record became load-bearing.** Changed: §16's pending ratification item closed outright; G-9's §2 clause now records the disposition rather than requiring a blanket ratification. Why: the completion criterion is approved directly — with the added instruction to pursue a crossover as hard as the rules allow, and an explicit statement that an unfavourable outcome is acceptable, which independently endorses ER-8 from outside the project. That clause was the only part of §2 inherited from the approved proposal, so it was the only part its author could not legitimately redefine alone; approving it is what closes the item. The four hypotheses, the paired-inference procedure, the no-weakening rule and the learned-blind operating-point rule were **delegated** as methodology, which is a disposition and not an omission — methodology is the author's to choose, and every one of them is stricter than the proposal required. **The consequence, which is the reason this entry exists:** with no external check on H1–H4, this file and its commit history are now the *sole* preregistration record for them, so §17's append-only rule stops being bookkeeping and becomes the evidence. Any change to a hypothesis after data exists MUST be entered as a new `AM` citing the old one, and will be visible as post-hoc in the git history — which is exactly the property that makes delegated methodology defensible at a viva rather than merely convenient. Raised by: supervisor.
- **AM-22** — **BR-2's golden vectors were single-sourced behind a licence the project does not control.** Changed: `ldpc_golden_vector_source_ladder` and `ldpc_golden_vector_licence_check_required` added; BR-2 descends the ladder and records the rung used; §11 states the MATLAB licence is attempted but not assured; DEC-10's rejection of AFF3CT narrowed explicitly to the runtime implementation, since a one-shot fixture generator emits a committed `.npz` and never touches CI or runtime, so DEC-3 is untouched; §16's provenance item rewritten. Why: the supervisor will attempt the licence but asked for a contingency, and BR-2 gates G-2, which gates every reported comparison — so leaving the fixture single-sourced would have put the entire comparison behind a decision outside the project's control. Rung 2 is the discovery that makes this cheap: the srsRAN Project ships committed binary test vectors covering all base graphs and lifting sizes, generated from MATLAB's 5G Toolbox as its own trusted reference, which yields MATLAB-provenance vectors with no MATLAB licence and satisfies the only thing BR-2 actually demands — independence from Sionna. Two things the W0 spike must still confirm rather than assume: whether srsRAN's vectors cover the rate-matched output as well as the encoder output, and whether their licence permits committing the fixture into this repository. Rung 4 — hand-verifying one small-lifting-size codeword against the TS 38.212 tables — is the floor that nobody can block. Raised by: supervisor (the contingency instruction), INT-1 (the ladder and the DEC-10 scope clarification).
- **AM-23** — **W0 spike, documentary half: three unknowns closed and one risk mis-stated.** Changed: `ldpc_standard_version` and `ldpc_standard_version_date` added, pinning TS 38.212 to **V17.13.0 (2026-02)**, the latest Release 17, which satisfies AM-9; §16's Python 3.14 entry rewritten; §16's golden-vector item updated with rung 2's status; BR-10 records the arithmetic verification. Why, item by item. **(a)** AM-9 required a version pin because a spec under change control is not reproducible from its title alone; V17.13.0 is now that pin. **(b)** The Python 3.14 risk was recorded against the wrong end of the version range — it worried about declared *minimums* (Sionna 3.11+, PyTorch 2.9+, both satisfied) when the real exposure is whether CUDA wheels are built for `cp314` at all. They are, on the cu130 index, but a bare `pip install torch` silently yields the CPU build, so the mitigation is an explicit `--index-url` plus an assertion on `torch.version.cuda`; and the old fallback line claiming a 3.12/3.13 pin "costs nothing else" was wrong, because neither interpreter exists on the primary device. **(c)** Rung 2 of the golden-vector ladder is technically confirmed — `srsRAN_matlab` ships rate-matcher *and* segmenter generators, not just an encoder generator — but its licensing half split open: generators are BSD-2-Clause yet require MATLAB, while the pre-generated data is AGPLv3, so vendoring it into a submitted repository is a distribution decision that must be made explicitly rather than by default. **(d)** BR-10's segmentation and rate-matching arithmetic was verified across all 72 live configurations at zero slack before any implementation exists, which is BR-3's equal-channel-uses claim proven as arithmetic independently of whether any library honours it. Measured findings — CUDA availability on the device, whether Sionna's encoder emits exactly `E_r`, the accepted `cn_update` spelling, decode throughput and the ER-1 projection — are **not** in this entry and follow separately once the spike runs. Raised by: INT-1.
- **AM-24** — **W0 spike, measured half: G-9's last item closed, and two silent-failure modes found.** Changed: `ldpc_decoder_impl_spelling`, `ldpc_llr_convention` and `ldpc_bg1_min_coderate` added, and `ldpc_decode_cb_per_s_measured`, `ldpc_decode_batch_measured`, `ldpc_decode_measured_at_iters`, `ldpc_decode_measured_gate`, `er1_projected_hours_one_ratio` and `er1_projected_hours_two_ratios` added to `params.compute`; BR-10 records the base-graph clamp; BR-14 fixes the spelling mapping and the LLR sign at the seam; §16's spike item closed and its Python 3.14 item confirmed. **What was measured.** `torch 2.13.0+cu130` and `sionna-no-rt 2.0.1` run on Python 3.14.6 with CUDA live on an RTX 4060 Laptop (8 GB); the encoder hits an exact `E_r` across **all 180 configurations** — five bandwidth ratios × three datasets × `params.baseline.modulations` × `params.baseline.ldpc_rates`, of which 72 are live — so BR-3's equal-channel-uses claim now holds against the library and not only as arithmetic; batched decode peaks at 634 code blocks/s at `params.baseline.ldpc_max_iters`, which projects ER-1 at about 4.1 hours for two operating ratios on the worst-case modulation, so **G-8's one-ratio-or-two decision is not compute-constrained**; the smallest workable payload is 16 information bits, far below anything the low ratios need. **Why the two failures matter more than the measurements.** *(a)* The check-node update is spelled `offset_min_sum` in this document and `offset-minsum` in the library, which is a one-line adapter concern but would have surfaced as a runtime error at W3. *(b)* Far worse, the library reads LLRs as log(p(x=1)/p(x=0)), the opposite sign to the mapping x = 1−2c — and getting it backwards is **completely silent**: the decoder runs, returns exactly k bits, raises nothing, and yields BER 0.77 at an SNR where the correct sign yields 0.00. Had this reached W3 behind a working-looking pipeline, it would have presented as "the classical baseline is weak", which is precisely the failure this project must never make, because it manufactures the result ER-8 forbids. *(c)* Separately, three live configurations at nominal rate 1/3 were **unrealizable as specified**: `floor(G × rate)` puts the per-code-block rate at 0.333281 against base graph 1's limit of 0.333333, and BG1 cannot go lower without repetition coding. The fix is a one-bit clamp, evaluated after segmentation; the finding is that a rate named in `params.baseline.ldpc_rates` did not actually exist at three of the operating points, one of them `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_operating_point`. Also corrected: the ER-1 projection now quotes a worst-case modulation figure as well as the QPSK 5/6 one, because DEC-16 makes modulation adaptive and 16-QAM needs up to 11 code blocks per image against the core case's 6 — quoting only the core case would have understated the run by 1.8×. Raised by: INT-1 (the spike itself); the three defects were found by running it.
- **AM-25** — **BR-2's golden vectors: rung 2 confirmed by demonstration, and the licensing question dissolved rather than decided.** Changed: `ldpc_golden_vector_source_rung`, `ldpc_golden_vector_vendored`, `ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_release`, `ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_asset`, `ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_url`, `ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_successor`, `ldpc_golden_vector_sha256` and `ldpc_golden_vector_offline_floor` added; rung 2 of `ldpc_golden_vector_source_ladder` **renamed** from `srsran_project_committed_testvectors` to `srsran_project_release_testvectors`, because the old name asserted the very thing this amendment found to be false; DEC-10's "emits a committed `.npz`" corrected to match, since the fixture is now generated and untracked; BR-2 rewritten with the non-vendoring design, the always-run offline floor and the alignment recipe; §16's provenance item closed, its pending-before-W1 block closed out, and a new upstream-availability risk carried. **The premise AM-22 and AM-23 relied on was wrong in a useful way.** srsRAN does not commit its vector data: the `.dat` files ship as a per-release asset (`phy_testvectors.tar`, 250 MB, of which the three LDPC sets are about 1 MB), not as repository content. That dissolves the AGPL question instead of answering it — the fixture is built by a committed fetch-and-convert script pinned to an immutable release and verified against `ldpc_golden_vector_sha256`, with the derived `.npz` untracked, so no third-party data is redistributed in a submitted artifact and byte-exactness is still provable. Checksums are facts about a file, not a copy of one. The offline-reproducibility cost that made this the "cheapest but lossy" option in AM-23 is paid off by promoting rung 4 from *fallback* to *always-run floor*: a hand-derived small case, wholly this project's own work, committed and unconditional, so the bit-exact test can never silently skip. **Rung 2 is confirmed by demonstration, not by inspection.** Sionna's encoder was checked against the MATLAB-generated vectors at W0 and agrees **bit-exactly, with zero mismatches**, across lifting sizes 2 through 288 on both base graphs. That check is also where the alignment recipe now in BR-2 came from, and it is worth recording that the first three attempts agreed at 0.50 — chance — because srsRAN stores the codeword already 2Z-punctured (BG1's codeword is 68Z, the stored buffer 66Z) and marks fillers with byte 254, while Sionna deletes fillers *before* puncturing. A fixture built on the naive alignment would have failed G-2 while looking like a library defect. Two limits recorded rather than hidden: the comparison can only cover the portion above each base graph's minimum coderate, since Sionna refuses to encode below it; and the lifting size must be pinned rather than inferred from (k, n), which is why 85 of the 102 upstream cases were skipped in the W0 probe rather than compared — that is a limitation of the probe, not a disagreement, and building the real fixture means pinning Z. Carried forward as risk: the upstream repository is archived, srsRAN having become OCUDU in December 2025. Raised by: INT-1; the licensing decision was delegated by the supervisor with no stated preference.

**Amendment round 2 — 2026-07-27, W1 open.** Sources: `EXT-4`, a full-specification review by Claude returning "commit, after seven blocking edits"; `EXT-5`, a second independent review returning "project GO, specification NO-GO/HOLD"; and `INT-2`, the internal adjudication run against both. Neither review was sufficient alone and neither verdict was adopted as given. `EXT-4` is strong on cross-reference hygiene and mostly correct, but its single external claim — that BR-2's golden vectors can be repointed at OCUDU — is **false**, its Third Review sub-mark denominator is wrong (60, not 55), and it missed the three most serious defects in the document. `EXT-5` found those three, but its blanket hold on implementation was over-broad: exactly one of its findings touches W1's work and it is a parameter addition. `INT-2` recomputed every contested number against `params.generated.yaml` and the committed W0 evidence — the packetisation across all 180 configurations, the ER-9 rate budget, the ER-1 projection, H1's error bound, and the rubric weights — and refuted two claims outright. This round is **spec-only and unblocks W1**; the packetisation and baseline work it uncovered is deferred to a third round that lands together with its re-run evidence.

- **AM-26** — **`core_ratio` was orphaned by AM-20 while three sections still pointed at it.** Changed: bandwidth.core_ratio and its status key deleted from §4; BR-1's secondary JPEG curve repointed at `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio`; §13's W6 row rewritten to name `params.bandwidth.efficiency_ratio` and `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio`, and its W8 row to name every ratio DEC-11 trains; ER-3's verify clause corrected from "the two named here" to the ratios named here, which its own body contradicted by naming three; §16's provisional block rewritten; AM-12's now-dangling citation de-backticked in place per §17's own convention for a removed key; and `tools/gen_spec_views.py`, which hard-coded a core_ratio check, given selector validation instead. Why: AM-20 replaced the single selection with two, but nothing downstream was updated, so a key that no gate resolved was still cited by BR-1, by two schedule rows and by §16 — it would have stayed provisional forever. W8 was the expensive half: it named two ratios across three seeds, six runs, while DEC-11 mandates up to three ratios, nine, so the schedule under-scoped its own training load by half. The generator could not catch this, because the key still *resolved*; what was missing was any check that a selector is ever selected. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-27** — **The single most load-bearing artifact in the project had no training recipe.** Changed: `params.reference_classifier` gains optimizer, lr, momentum, weight_decay, lr_schedule, lr_warmup_epochs, epochs, batch_size, label_smoothing and augmentation; `params.learned_system` gains augmentation; BR-8 states the recipe is config-derived and its verify clause tests it. Why: the reference classifier gates G-1, defines the clean-image accuracy that is the denominator of ER-3's entire selection rule, and scores one whole arm of the headline comparison — yet §4 gave it an architecture and nothing else, which is a direct SR-1 violation, and 0.88 from scratch on 8469 images is recipe-dependent rather than what a default training loop returns. Compounding it, `params.digital_semantic_control.shared_with_learned` listed `augmentation` among the things ER-9 shares with the learned system while no such parameter existed anywhere, so ER-9's "differs only in the channel interface" claim — the whole basis of H4 — could not be checked against it. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-28** — **There was no canonical image.** Changed: new `params.preprocessing` section fixing the resize and crop policy, interpolation, antialiasing, colour space, bit depth, tensor range, where channel normalisation happens, whether evaluation augmentation is permitted, whether reconstructions are clipped before scoring, and the PSNR data range and SSIM implementation; new SR-19 requiring it and requiring the codec to compress the same canonical pixels the learned encoder receives. Why: none of this was stated, and every item changes a reported number — an unclipped reconstruction inflates PSNR, a mismatched data range shifts it by decibels, and a codec fed differently-resized pixels from the encoder makes the comparison invalid rather than merely noisy. BR-3's equal-channel-uses claim is about channel uses, not about the two pipelines having seen the same image; nothing until now required that. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-29** — **A headline measurement was faster than the evidence committed to prove it, and a projection was named for a cost it does not measure.** Changed: `params.compute.ldpc_decode_cb_per_s_measured` corrected from 634 to 625.2, the figure in `spec/evidence/g9_spike_record.json`, with `ldpc_decode_cb_per_s_observed_range` recording the re-run spread and `ldpc_decode_early_termination` recording that the decoder does not stop early; the ER-1 projections renamed to `params.compute.er1_projected_ldpc_decode_hours_one_ratio` and `..._two_ratios` and recomputed to 2.07 and 4.14, with `er1_projected_ldpc_decode_scope` naming what they exclude and `er1_projected_total_hours_status` recording that the end-to-end figure is owed at W3/W4; §16's ER-1 cost risk updated. Why: ER-7 requires every reported number to be traceable to a committed artifact, and 634 was not — the committed run measures 625.2 at batch 32 and peaks at 626.8 at batch 64, so the spec was optimistic against its own evidence while §16 claimed to record the slow end. Separately the projection reconstructs exactly as LDPC decode alone and excludes JPEG 2000 encode and decode, about 424k classifier forward passes across two classifier variants, and the 16-QAM soft demapper — the JPEG 2000 half alone is comparable to the entire LDPC budget if the codec is driven by subprocess. One good outcome: the library documents that no early stopping is used, so the fixed 50 iterations make the measurement worst-case with respect to SNR, and the low-SNR half of the grid will not be slower than the half it was measured on. Raised by: EXT-4 (the projection's scope), INT-2 (the throughput discrepancy and the early-termination check).
- **AM-30** — **A proposed upgrade to BR-2's vector source is factually wrong, and the mitigation already in §16 was wrong too.** Changed: §16's archived-upstream risk rewritten — its "take the vectors from the OCUDU repository under its own licence" clause deleted as unavailable, the risk restated as *larger* than previously recorded, and an instruction added to fetch and archive the pinned release asset locally before the fixture is first built. Why: EXT-4 recommended repointing `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_upstream_url` at an OCUDU release asset, on the grounds that the successor ships the same MATLAB-generated vectors under BSD-3 rather than AGPLv3, thereby closing a carried risk and permitting vendoring. Checked against OCUDU's own documentation: the licence half is correct — it is a Linux Foundation project under the BSD-3-Clause Open MPI variant — but the asset does not exist. Vector tests moved out of the main repository into a separate `ocudu-matlab` companion plugin, and OCUDU's MATLAB tutorial states that running the suite requires a working and licensed copy of MATLAB and its 5G Toolbox. There are no pre-generated vectors to download, so BSD-3 covers generators rather than data, and adopting the recommendation would have traded a working rung for the licence dependency rung 2 exists to avoid. The consequence runs the other way from the recommendation: the successor publishes no replacement rung at all, so if the pinned srsRAN asset is withdrawn, rung 2 is gone permanently and `params.baseline.ldpc_golden_vector_offline_floor` carries more weight than AM-25 assumed. The asset was verified reachable on 2026-07-27. This is the second time an assumption about where these vectors live has proved wrong — AM-25 was the first — and the lesson recorded is that a claim about a third party's release artifacts is checkable in minutes and must be checked rather than inherited. Raised by: EXT-4 (rejected), INT-2 (the verification and the correction).
- **AM-31** — **ER-10 promised a variance decomposition that AM-17 had already made impossible.** Changed: ER-10 no longer claims `params.evaluation.train_seeds` and `params.evaluation.channel_seeds` contribute as separate variance components; `params.evaluation.seed_cell_interpretation` added, recording each cell as a compound replicate; the existing per-seed-curve and between-seed-range rules retained as what actually shows the spread. Why: `params.evaluation.seed_pairing` zips the seeds into (0,0), (1,1) and (2,2), so training luck and channel luck move together in every cell and no analysis can attribute a difference to one rather than the other — three diagonal cells cannot separate two sources. AM-17 introduced the zipping and AM-11 wrote the decomposition claim, and neither was read against the other. Crossing the seeds into nine cells would restore the claim at roughly three times ER-1's evaluation cost and was rejected, because two degrees of freedom could not support it even then. This is the AM-14 failure class for the third time: an amendment propagated into one section and not the one that depended on it. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-32** — **H1's family-wise error bound is not a bound, though its conclusion survives.** Changed: §2's family-wise error note now states the two calculations as examples rather than bounds, gives the counterexample, names the dependence model under which the interpolation actually holds, and points at a calibration; `params.evaluation.h1_run_calibration` and `h1_run_permutation_resamples` added; ER-10 requires the null distribution of the longest run to be computed by sign-flip permutation over whole per-image trajectories. Why: AM-4 argued that because adjacent points are positively dependent, the realised run probability must lie between 11 × 0.025³ and 0.025. That does not follow. Four independent blocks of three consecutive points, each block all-or-nothing Bernoulli(0.025), has every marginal at 0.025, is positively dependent, and yields a run probability of 1 − (1 − 0.025)⁴ ≈ 0.096 — about four times the "perfectly dependent" figure. What is true is narrower: under exchangeable or AR-1 Gaussian-copula dependence, which is what neighbouring SNR points sharing models and images actually produce, the probability does interpolate between the two. That is a modelling assumption, so it is now measured instead of argued, using per-image outcomes SR-18 already requires and a resampling loop that already runs. AM-4's decision — keep the run rule, do not replace it — is **unchanged and was re-affirmed**: EXT-5's proposed replacement is a scalar low-SNR effect size that AM-3 already added and reports, so adopting it would discard the decision rule and keep only what the document already has. Raised by: EXT-5 (the objection, partly upheld), INT-2 (the counterexample and the calibration remedy).
- **AM-33** — **A gate was required to decide something it cannot observe.** Changed: G-8 no longer decides DEC-16 and says why; new G-10 added at W10, deciding the crossover-or-fallback on validation curves once W8's models exist; DEC-16's fallback clause repointed from G-8 to G-10; §13's W10 row carries the gate. Why: G-8 sits at W6, where ER-3's sweep is classical-only by construction and the first learned model does not exist until W7's pilot — so a gate there has one of the two curves and cannot see a crossing. The clause had been in G-8 since before the schedule placed the pilot after it, and nothing re-read the two against each other. G-8 keeps what it can actually do, which is select ratios by a learned-blind rule. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-34** — **Two decision rationales became arithmetically false when DEC-16 added 16-QAM.** Changed: DEC-1's claim that CIFAR-10's budget is below any container floor "at every ratio" replaced with a resolution-and-representativeness argument plus the narrower and true claim about the small ratios; DEC-9's claim that JPEG's container floor consumes "most of Imagenette's budget at the core ratio" rescoped to the small ratios and low code rates where it holds. Why: CIFAR-10 at `r_1_2` with 16-QAM at rate 5/6 receives 640 bytes for a 32×32 image, which is 5 bpp and far above any container floor; Imagenette at `r_1_3` under the same configuration receives about 10.7 kB, against which a 290-byte JPEG header is 2.7% rather than "most". Both decisions are correct and unchanged — CIFAR-10 stays a smoke path, JPEG 2000 stays the codec of record — but this document's convention is that rationales are load-bearing and re-litigation-proof, and a checkable false one is a liability an examiner can find with a calculator. Raised by: EXT-4 and EXT-5 independently.
- **AM-35** — **SR-9 mandated exactly the magic numbers SR-1's lint rule exists to catch.** Changed: `params.learned_system.lambda_calibration_snr_db`, `lambda_psnr_eval_snr_db` and `lambda_calibration_ratio` added; SR-9 cites them in place of the phrases "the core SNR" and "15 dB SNR", and states that λ transfers from the calibration ratio to the others DEC-11 trains. Why: there is no core_snr parameter and never was, and "15 dB" was a bare numeric SNR literal in a requirement whose sibling SR-1 requires a lint rule flagging numeric SNR literals outside the config directory — the requirement and its own linter disagreed. Separately the ratio at which λ is calibrated was unstated while DEC-11 trains up to three, and the PSNR floor is materially harder to clear at the smallest of them, which is part of why DEC-2's relaxed floor exists. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-36** — **A Tier 1 constraint was justified by a stretch goal that HR-5 forbids depending on.** Changed: `params.learned_system.max_params_policy` added; SR-14's rationale replaced — the learned encoder, decoder and head together must not exceed the parameter count of `params.reference_classifier` — with the verify clause testing both the absolute cap and the comparison. Why: the cap was justified as "keeping the RPi tier plausible", but DEC-14 makes that tier explicit upside and HR-5 forbids any Tier 1 requirement from depending on hardware, so a constraint bounding the headline result's capacity rested on something the document elsewhere declares optional. The replacement is defensible in one sentence at a viva, is independent of HR-5, and happens to be the same number: a ResNet-18 at ten classes is about 11.2M parameters, so the learned system was already capped below its own comparator. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-37** — **`run_id` was the only thing joining the paired inference, and its uniqueness was undefined.** Changed: `params.artifacts.run_id_key` added; SR-18 requires uniqueness against it and tests for collisions; ER-10's verify clause depends on it. Why: SR-18 keys per-image files by `run_id` and ER-10 pairs per-image outcomes across systems at matched image, SNR and seed — but `params.artifacts.per_image_schema` carries none of those columns, so the entire join runs through `run_id` alone. Undefined, the pairing is unspecified and can be silently wrong in a way that no schema test would catch. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-38** — **The results schema could not represent the control system, and ER-5 forbids adding columns.** Changed: `params.artifacts.csv_schema` extended with `classifier_variant`, `quantiser_bits`, `transmit_dim`, `entropy_stream_bytes`, `entropy_table_bytes`, `side_information_bytes`, `tb_crc_type`, `base_graph`, `lifting_size`, `num_codeblocks`, `filler_bits`, `effective_code_rate`, `model_param_count`, `coverage_rate` and `acc_given_delivery`, appended so existing column order is stable; `params.artifacts.system_values` added enumerating the legal `system` values; `per_image_schema` extended with `outage_reason` and `source_bytes`; `params.evaluation.metrics` extended to match; BR-13 requires coverage and conditional accuracy alongside end-to-end accuracy. Why: ER-5 requires rows to match the schema exactly, same columns and same order, while ER-9's selected quantiser width — the control's single most important configuration parameter — had nowhere to go, and neither did the entropy table bytes that `entropy_table_bytes_counted` says must be counted. ER-7 requires every number in the thesis to be traceable to a row, and ER-9's operating point was not. The `system` column now has to carry at least eleven distinct values and ER-7's audit script will be written against that list whether or not it is written down first. EXT-5's proposal to replace BR-13's uniform random outage draw with a deterministic fallback was **rejected**: the draw is reproducible from `channel_seed` and therefore identical across systems at a matched image, which is exactly the property ER-10's pairing needs, and it costs about half a percentage point of standard deviation. Its reporting half was adopted, because one number conflating "the link failed" with "the link worked and the task failed" is genuinely less informative than three. Raised by: EXT-4 (the schema), EXT-5 (the additional columns and the outage reporting).
- **AM-39** — **H3 could be supported by a gap moving in the opposite direction to the one it claims.** Changed: §2's H3 requires the low-SNR gap to be positive as well as the slope negative; `params.evaluation.h3_requires_positive_low_snr_gap` and `gap_trend_bootstrap_unit` added; ER-10 bootstraps the slope over per-image, per-seed trajectories rather than over aggregated SNR points. Why: a paired gap starting at −5 pp and falling to −30 pp has a negative slope with an interval excluding zero, and would have satisfied H3 as written while describing divergence rather than convergence. H1 already establishes the sign at the low end, so the extra clause costs nothing to evaluate. Separately, a weighted least-squares fit over 18 aggregated points treats measurements sharing images and models as independent and would produce an interval far too narrow to be honest. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-40** — **The sharpest conceptual objection to the project had no answer in the document.** Changed: §1 gains an explicit premise paragraph framing the work as remote inference at a **fixed encoder/decoder partition**, with the claim narrowed to "the best way to transmit at this partition" rather than globally optimal task communication; new ER-12 requires a label-transmission upper-bound curve reusing ER-9's trunk, labelled a bound and excluded from every §2 hypothesis. Why: for ten-class classification the complete task output is four bits, so a transmitter permitted to run the whole classifier could attach the head locally and protect four bits heavily — and no amount of joint coding beats that. §1 already acknowledged that a digital transmitter could send "logits or labels", but ER-9 builds only the feature end of that spectrum, leaving the objection acknowledged and unanswered. The answer is the premise rather than a measurement, and the bound is reported so a reader sees what the premise costs instead of wondering whether it was noticed. Reconstruction cannot do this work, because §15 makes it a non-goal. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-41** — **The operating-point ladder had no bottom, no relative definition and no answer to a half-satisfied rule.** Changed: `r_1_48` added to `params.bandwidth.ratios` with k of 1600, 576 and 64; `params.bandwidth.crossover_ratio_unsatisfiable_fallback`, `ladder_bottom_saturation_rule`, `low_ratio_rule` and `low_ratio_operating_point_status` added; ER-3 preregisters the asymmetric case and the downward extension; G-8 carries both clauses and resolves the low ratio by rule. Why: three separate gaps. `params.bandwidth.headline_ratio` resolves to `crossover_ratio`, which is undefined if no ratio meets the 2 pp threshold — and G-8's existing fallback covers only the symmetric case where the rule fails at every threshold, not the likely asymmetric one where 5 pp is satisfiable and 2 pp is not, which had to be settled before W6 rather than with the sweep table in hand. `low_ratio_operating_point` was hard-coded at `r_1_12` while the headline ratio is selected, so a selection below it would put the "bandwidth-starved" point *above* the operating point and leave §2's low-ratio dominance expectation with no experiment behind it. And the ladder could saturate at its smallest rung, in which case the honest answer is "smaller than anything we tried" and the ladder must be extended. `r_1_48` is free architecturally — exactly one complex channel at the 4x-downsampled grid on all three datasets — where `r_1_32` would need one and a half and is not usable. **Verified before adoption rather than after**, by re-running the W0 packetisation arithmetic over the expanded grid: 216 configurations instead of 180, the live set unchanged at 72, the six BG1 minimum-rate clamps unchanged, and every `E_r` still summing to `G` with zero slack. The new rung is structurally sound on both headline datasets — Imagenette receives 63 to 663 bytes across the modulation and rate axes, STL-10 21 to 237 — which is small enough that the classical system will mostly fail BR-5 there, and that is precisely the point of a bottom rung whose job is to detect ladder saturation. It also surfaced one genuine defect: two smoke-dataset cells at BPSK receive 21 and 32 bits against a 24-bit transport-block CRC, so no transport block can be built at all. BR-5 now distinguishes that structural infeasibility from codec infeasibility, because reporting the former as the latter would blame JPEG 2000 for a packetisation limit. Raised by: EXT-4 (the rung), INT-2 (the verification and the BR-5 distinction).
- **AM-42** — **AM-12's defect recurred at reduced scale: models trained that no requirement consumes.** Changed: new ER-11 requires the learned system to be evaluated at every ratio DEC-11 trains, across the test grid at sweep strength with the classical curve alongside, reported as the bandwidth-versus-accuracy figure. Why: DEC-11 trains up to three ratios, but ER-1 runs at the headline ratio alone, ER-2 names no ratio and ER-4 is an ablation of existing rows — so the efficiency-point and low-ratio models were trained and then consumed by no CSV row, no figure and no hypothesis. This matters beyond waste: DEC-16's last-resort fallback reports learned dominance across the whole grid, and the low-ratio regime is where that claim is strongest, so its evidence must already be a required output rather than something improvised at W12 under the exact schedule pressure that would make the fallback necessary. At `params.evaluation.test_subset_size` and one seed this costs a few percent of ER-1. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-43** — **"Complete bit accounting" did not account for the control plane.** Changed: BR-10 declares a common out-of-band control plane, excluded for every system, and states that the exclusion favours the baseline. Why: an adaptive digital receiver must know the modulation, code rate, payload length and quantiser width before it can decode anything, and none of that was counted while the document claims complete accounting and DEC-13 names that accounting as a novelty component. Either it is counted in every digital packet or it is declared excluded; leaving it unstated is the one option that cannot be defended. Excluding it symmetrically is the honest choice here because the classical baseline re-tunes at every SNR and therefore needs the signalling, while the learned system is frozen and needs none — so the exclusion hands the advantage to the baseline, which is the permitted direction under DEC-16's governing rule. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-44** — **PAPR was disclosed but never constrained, and was reported for one system out of three.** Changed: SR-16 requires PAPR for all three systems, requires a PAPR-constrained learned variant as a secondary curve at the headline ratio, and distinguishes symbol-domain peak-to-average ratio from oversampled waveform PAPR; `params.learned_system.papr_constrained_variant_required` and `papr_constrained_variant_seeds` added. Why: SR-16 made a clipping constraint *available* and required nothing to use it, so the headline learned curve could run at unconstrained PAPR against a digital arm that a reader would assume is constant-modulus — and §1's "equal average symbol power implies equal transmitted energy" is true but incomplete under that reading. This is the standard objection to learned JSCC and the first one a reader with a communications background reaches for; disclosure answers "did you know?" but not "is the comparison fair?". One extra training run converts it into a figure this project produced itself. Reporting PAPR for the digital arms costs nothing and prevents the silent assumption that adaptive 16-QAM is constant modulus, which it is not. Raised by: EXT-4 (the constrained variant), EXT-5 (all-systems reporting and the waveform distinction).
- **AM-45** — **The counterpart BR-15 names for its own disclosure was optional and would not have been run.** Changed: OPT-4 retired in §14 and promoted into ER-2, which now requires the SNR-randomised learned variant at one seed at the headline ratio; BR-15 restated to say the adaptation asymmetry cuts both ways and to point at ER-2. Why: BR-15 discloses that the classical system re-tunes at every SNR while the learned system is trained once and frozen, and frames the asymmetry as favouring the baseline at high SNR. It favours the baseline at *low* SNR too, and that is where H1 lives: the baseline may fall back to BPSK rate 1/3 for roughly 3 dB of extra reach while the learned model is frozen at a 7 dB training point and evaluated down to −8 dB, a 15 dB mismatch. H1 is probably still safe, because the classical arm is pinned at chance below its own waterfall, but this is the mechanism by which the primary hypothesis could fail for a design reason rather than a physical one — and `params.channel.train_snr_db_set` already existed in §4 serving nothing but the optional requirement nobody was going to run. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-46** — **Thirty rubric marks were exposed by how objectives are worded, and the review dates cannot be trusted.** Changed: PR-8 gains three normative clauses — objectives stated in §2's completion terms and never as outcomes, restatement at the Second Review if G-10 takes DEC-16's fallback, and a one-slide §17 distillation in the Second and Third packages; new PR-9 requires a written deployment-scenario note; `params.deliverables.objectives_stated_as`, `objectives_modification_point` and `review_dates_status` added; §16 carries the date-confirmation item. Why: verified against the rubric spreadsheet, the sub-mark weights are First 30 → 10, Second 50 → 30, Third **60** → 40 and Report 20 → 20 — EXT-4 reported the Third Review as 55, which is wrong, though its conclusions from it hold. The Third Review scores `Objectives Met` at 10 sub-marks, defined as whether objectives set at the first review or modified at the second were met. §2 makes completion independent of which way the result falls, but a rubric grades objectives rather than completion, so an objective phrased as an outcome would convert DEC-16's fallback — which §2 declares a complete Tier 1 — into an objective visibly not met, and everything §2 gets right could be undone by one slide at W4. The rubric explicitly permits modification at the second review, which is after G-10, so the ordering already favours the project and only needs to be used deliberately. `Methodology` asks for the approach decided *based on the results obtained*, which is what §17 already is. `Application` rests on a Streamlit demo of a simulation unless something is written. The dates on the spreadsheet are a 2023 template — its own submission deadline reads December 2023 — so they must be confirmed before PR-2's Gantt is committed. Raised by: EXT-4 (the trap and the deliverables), INT-2 (the corrected weights and the template dates).
- **AM-47** — **The recurring failure in this document now has a process rule.** Changed: §17's preamble states that an amendment changing a parameter's meaning MUST state which sections were re-read against it, and that §13 and every `verify` clause are always on that list. Why: AM-14 established that the generator's arithmetic check cannot catch cross-section inconsistency and that sections must be read against each other by hand. That lesson has now cost four further defects in two amendment rounds — AM-26, AM-31, AM-33 and the schema half of AM-38 — every one of them an amendment correctly applied to one section and never propagated to the schedule or to a verify clause. A rule that names the two places always most likely to be stale is cheaper than finding them by review. Raised by: EXT-4 (the observation), INT-2 (the rule).
- **AM-48** — **The encoder was not implementation-ready a week before it is built.** Changed: `params.learned_system` gains `encoder_arch`, `encoder_downsample_factor`, `encoder_stem_channels`, `encoder_body_channels`, `encoder_residual_blocks`, `encoder_activation`, `encoder_norm`, `encoder_complex_packing`, `encoder_output_complex_channels`, `decoder_arch`, `decoder_upsample`, `arch_fallback` and `arch_freeze_gate`. Why: `conv_downsample_to_k_symbols` named a shape and nothing else — layer topology, complex packing, activations, normalisation, decoder branching and any ratio-specific layers were all open degrees of freedom, and W2 builds the skeleton while G-7 profiles it. The freeze uses a property worth recording because it is load-bearing rather than incidental: a single 4x-downsampling encoder satisfies SR-2 and SR-3 **simultaneously for all three datasets**, because the required output channel counts are 24, 16, 8, 4, 2 and 1 for the six ratios *identically* on Imagenette at 40×40, STL-10 at 24×24 and CIFAR-10 at 8×8. SR-2's prohibition on dataset-specific branching is therefore satisfiable by construction rather than by discipline; it is also why AM-41's `r_1_48` rung costs the encoder nothing, and why ER-9's own output width can be a single differing hyperparameter rather than an added layer. Raised by: EXT-5 (the gap), EXT-4 (the channel-count property).

**Amendment round 3 — 2026-07-27, W1 open.** The packetisation and baseline half of the `EXT-4`/`EXT-5`/`INT-2` adjudication, separated from round 2 because each entry here lands together with re-run evidence rather than ahead of it. `spec/evidence/check_packetisation.py` is new and is the artifact BR-10's verify clause requires: pure arithmetic, no GPU and no network, emitting a per-configuration record over the whole grid in under a second. It is deliberately separate from `spike_ldpc.py`, which stays as the archived W0 measurement (AM-24) — what changed since is the packetisation contract, not the measurement.

- **AM-49** — **The bit accounting was not TS 38.212 conformant in three ways, and its own novelty claim depends on it being so.** Changed: baseline.tb_crc_bits replaced by `params.baseline.tb_crc`, a conditional block with `threshold_payload_bits` 3824 selecting CRC16 below and CRC24A above; `params.baseline.code_block_max_bits` changed from a scalar 8448 to per-base-graph values, 8448 on BG1 and 3840 on BG2; `params.baseline.base_graph_selection_rule`, `base_graph_pinned_at_seam`, `bg2_min_coderate` and `payload_solver` added; `params.baseline.budget_rule` replaced from a closed form with a solver; BR-10 and BR-14 rewritten; `spec/evidence/check_packetisation.py` added and run. Why: TS 38.212 §7.2.1 uses CRC24A only above 3824 payload bits and CRC16 below, where this document carried an unconditional 24; §5.2.2 caps a code block at 3840 bits on BG2, where it carried 8448 unconditionally; and §7.2.2 selects the base graph once per **transport block** from (A, R) *before* segmentation, where the W0 spike derived it *afterwards* from the per-code-block rate — which is also what `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` does when left to infer, so the seam must now pass the graph explicitly. Recomputed across the grid: 17 of 72 live configurations are entitled to CRC16 and 14 select BG2, but **segmentation changes in none of them**, so BR-3's equal-channel-uses claim and BR-10's zero-slack result both survive intact and the cost is one byte of payload in 17 cells. That is why this is a defect in DEC-13's *claim* — "complete container and physical-layer bit accounting" — rather than in any measured number. Two results worth recording: BR-10's canonical case reproduces exactly under the corrected rules (C = 6, K_r = 7135, E summing to 51 200, now with lifting size 352 and 609 filler bits), and **AM-24's one-bit BG1 clamp survives** — all six clamped configurations have A above the CRC threshold and a rate above 0.25, so BG1 is correct for them under the standard's own selection rule and the clamp was not an artifact of the inverted ordering. Raised by: EXT-5 (the CRC and base-graph constants), INT-2 (the selection-ordering defect, the recomputation, and the confirmation that AM-24 stands).
- **AM-50** — **Half of G-2 had no objective pass condition.** Changed: G-2 now cites `params.evaluation.ber_match_statistic` and `ber_match_tolerance_db`, and requires the reference data and checksum, (K, N), base graph, lifting size, modulation, decoder algorithm and offset value all to be pinned; TS 38.211 added to `params.deliverables.standards`; PR-3 must state that plain BPSK is a generic AWGN choice rather than NR's π/2-BPSK. Why: "BER matches published curves within tolerance" names no tolerance, no reference file, no decoder settings and no comparison statistic, so the statistical half of the gate that gates every reported comparison was a judgement call while its golden-vector half was a test. Waterfall displacement at a fixed BLER is the standard way to compare and turns it into a number. Separately, this project claims 5G NR LDPC and uses TS 38.211's modulation mapping without citing it, and NR's uplink BPSK option is π/2-BPSK rather than the plain BPSK simulated here — a small imprecision that is free to state and awkward to be caught on. Raised by: EXT-5.
- **AM-51** — **The outer loop of the largest cached artifact in the project was entirely unspecified.** Changed: `params.baseline` gains `j2k_impl`, `j2k_impl_version`, `j2k_binding`, `j2k_wavelet`, `j2k_progression_order`, `j2k_resolutions`, `j2k_code_block_size`, `j2k_tile_size`, `j2k_search_method`, `j2k_search_bounds`, `j2k_search_tolerance_bytes`, `j2k_search_max_iters`, `j2k_cache_key`, `j2k_nonmonotone_policy`, `downsample_axis_px`, `downsample_selection`, `jpeg_impl`, `jpeg_chroma_subsampling`, `jpeg_optimise_huffman` and `bler_characterisation_trials`; BR-1 freezes the codec configuration and the cache; BR-4 states its analytic composition function. Why: AM-8 established that `opj_compress` has no exact-byte control and that BR-1 drives a cached search, but no search parameters, bounds, tolerance, iteration cap or codec flags were ever added — and `params.baseline.jpeg_quality_grid` serves only the *secondary* curve. BR-4's sweep is codec rate × 4 code rates × 3 modulations × the SNR grid × the ratio ladder, landing at W4 and blocking the First Review package, with its outer axis undefined. Defaults would have made it lossless reversible 5/3, which is wrong for a lossy baseline. The cache statement is load-bearing rather than an optimisation: the emitted codestream depends only on the image and the budget, so without it a naive implementation re-encodes on the order of 200,000 times in ER-1 alone. The downsampling axis is added because low-bitrate classical codecs often gain substantially from it and withholding it would be the artificial handicap DEC-16's governing rule forbids. BR-4's composition function was likewise never written down while every selection in the project flows through it, and it makes per-quality validation accuracy a required measured input that nobody had scheduled. Raised by: EXT-4 (the search parameters and the cache), EXT-5 (the codec flags, the downsampling axis and the composition function).
- **AM-52** — **The grid was sparsest exactly where the cliff it measures actually falls.** Changed: `params.channel.test_snr_grid_db` gains −7, −5 and −3, giving 1 dB spacing from −8 to −2 and 21 points in total; `grid_rationale` rewritten to describe all three waterfalls rather than only QPSK's. Why: `grid_rationale` justified density from QPSK rate 1/3 to QPSK rate 5/6 and never mentioned BPSK at all, although DEC-16 leaves BPSK in `params.baseline.modulations`, BR-4 selects it at the noisy end, and AM-15 relies on it for roughly 3 dB of extra reach. The W0 spike measured rate 1/3 at BER 0.0 at Es/N0 −4 dB and 0.31 at −8 dB, so the waterfall sits inside a region the grid sampled every 2 dB — meaning the classical cliff that H2 measures, and that §1's third claim rests on, could have been smeared across a gap or missed between points. This is the cheapest change in the adjudication relative to what it protects: about 17% on ER-1's evaluation, against a decision that gate G-8 makes about whether four hours fits twice. Raised by: EXT-4.
- **AM-53** — **H2 measured a cliff on a curve that cannot cliff, and had no test.** Changed: new BR-16 requires a fully fixed separated curve — codec configuration, LDPC rate and modulation all frozen at `params.baseline.fixed_mcs_design_snr_db`; `params.evaluation.cliff_reference_system`, `h2_test`, `h2_thresholds_apply_to` and `h2_threshold_check_gate` added; §2's H2 now names the fixed curve as its classical reference, decides by paired difference-in-differences through ER-10, and requires the learned-blind threshold check at G-8; §2's prose narrowed from "separated coding has a cliff" to a claim about this particular fixed configuration. Why: BR-4 re-tunes codec rate, code rate and modulation at every SNR, so its curve is the upper envelope of many systems — an envelope degrades in *steps* as it falls back to more robust settings and only cliffs once it runs out of them, whereas `params.evaluation.cliff_drop_pp` was preregistered against a single configuration's waterfall. BR-9's fixed-*modulation* curve does not fix this, because source rate and LDPC rate still adapt within it. Separately H2 stated numeric thresholds but never said how uncertainty governs support, while §2 claims every hypothesis is decided by ER-10. The threshold check is placed at G-8 because the classical half of H2 is knowable there and **learned-blind**: if the largest fixed-MCS validation drop is already below 30 pp, that is a finding recorded while no learned model exists, and any amendment made then is genuinely blind to the hypothesis it affects — which it would not be at W7. Raised by: EXT-5 (the adaptive-envelope objection and the missing test), EXT-4 (the learned-blind threshold check).
- **AM-54** — **The classical baseline was tuned by a classifier the project then replaced.** Changed: `params.reference_classifier.br4_selection_passes`, `br4_selection_terminates_after_pass` and `headline_scorer` added; BR-4 runs two passes and stops; BR-12 names which scorer carries the headline. Why: BR-4 selects codec and MCS configurations under BR-8's clean-trained classifier, which is the only one that exists at that point; BR-12 then fine-tunes on the artifacts *those selections* produce and rescores the results. The loop was never closed, so the reported classical arm sat at a configuration chosen by a scorer nobody reports — and because the artifact-finetuned classifier tolerates heavier compression it would plausibly prefer a different point, compressing harder and spending the savings on stronger channel coding. Reporting the baseline at the weaker scorer's choice understates it, which is the direction ER-8 and DEC-16's governing rule both prohibit, and it is the first thing an examiner asks once the two classifiers are described. Two passes closes it; terminating at two is preregistered so the loop cannot be iterated until it flatters a result; and the second pass is cheap because `params.baseline.j2k_cache_key` makes it classifier forward passes over already-emitted codestreams rather than a re-encode. Raised by: EXT-4 (the ambiguity), EXT-5 (the circularity), user (the two-pass decision).
- **AM-55** — **The H4 control was arithmetically infeasible at every operating point but one.** Changed: transmit_feature_dim removed from `params.digital_semantic_control.shared_with_learned` and from the conceptual allowlist; `transmit_dim_grid`, `transmit_dim_units`, `transmit_dim_realised_by`, `width_selection`, `selection_search`, `selection_search_is_cross_product` and `channel_interface_comprises` added; `entropy_table_bytes_counted` changed from true to false; ER-9 rewritten; §2's H4 language softened from causal attribution to consistency under the tested control; new G-11 gates W9. Why: sharing the transmitted feature dimension pinned ER-9 at 2k real values while the digital budget supplies only `Q_m × R / 2` bits per value — 1.67 at 16-QAM rate 5/6, 0.5 at QPSK rate 1/2 and **0.167 at BPSK rate 1/3**, against a 2-bit floor in the quantiser grid. Every configuration except 16-QAM at rate 5/6 was impossible, and that one decodes only above roughly 11 dB, entirely outside H1's region — so the control would have been at outage and scored at chance across exactly the range H4 is tested in, making H4 unfalsifiable, trivially easy to support, and the primary novelty claim of DEC-13 a system broken by construction. That is the AM-15 failure class at larger scale. The fix keeps the encoder architecture identical and lets ER-9 choose its own transmitted dimension, because deciding how many values to send against how precisely to send each one **is** the digital channel interface — an analog system makes no such choice — so it remains a difference in `differs_only_in` rather than an addition to it. The search is two-stage rather than a cross product so W9's training count stays bounded. Feasibility is now verified rather than assumed: `spec/evidence/check_packetisation.py` asserts a feasible pair exists at every live configuration, and at the tightest — STL-10 at the low ratio, BPSK rate 1/3, 94 payload bytes — seven pairs fit. The entropy table stops being charged per image because it is fitted offline and installs with the model exactly like network weights, quantiser scales, JPEG 2000 coding rules and LDPC matrices, none of which are charged; charging it weakened the control on every image, again in the direction that makes H4 easier. G-11 exists because W9 builds this entire system, plus ER-2, in one week with no gate on it, while §16 already warns ER-9 must not be the first thing cut. Raised by: EXT-5 (the infeasibility), user (the same-encoder-own-width decision), INT-2 (the per-value arithmetic, the entropy-table direction and the feasibility check).

**Amendment round 4 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: `INT-3`, a self-audit of the changes made in rounds 2 and 3 before the last documentation-only commit. It found two defects in that work, one of them introduced by AM-53 four hours earlier — recorded here because the value of §17 is that it shows corrections happening, including corrections to corrections.

- **AM-56** — **H2 could select its comparison window on a different curve from the one it evaluates.** Changed: `params.evaluation.cliff_window_selection` renamed in value from largest_classical_drop_on_validation_split to `largest_fixed_mcs_drop_on_validation_split`; §2's H2 now selects the window on `params.evaluation.cliff_reference_system` explicitly and states that the selection curve and the evaluation curve are the same one; §16 records ER-9's unspecified dimension mechanism as a carried gap. Why. AM-53 moved H2's *evaluation* onto BR-16's fixed separated curve but left its *window selection* clause reading "the pair over which the **classical** system loses the most top-1 accuracy" — and after AM-53 there are three classical curves, the adaptive one, BR-9's fixed-modulation one and BR-16's fixed-MCS one. `cliff_reference_system` appeared later in the requirement, where it reads as governing evaluation rather than selection. An implementer could therefore have selected the steepest window on the adaptive envelope and then measured the fixed curve across those endpoints, which are not the same SNRs where the fixed curve actually falls — understating the measured drop and failing H2 for a bookkeeping reason rather than a physical one. That is the same silent-weakening class as AM-15 and AM-55, arrived at from the opposite direction: here the *hypothesis* is handicapped rather than the control. Separately, `params.digital_semantic_control.transmit_dim_realised_by` names a mechanism — output channel count and adaptive pooling — without specifying the factorisation or the order of the two reductions, which is the gap class AM-48 closed for the learned encoder; it is carried in §16 rather than solved, because ER-9 is not built until W9, and G-11 is its natural checkpoint. Raised by: INT-3.

**Amendment round 5 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: `EXT-6` (`audit/JUDGE_codex`), a final pre-implementation gate audit returning **project GO, W1 NOGO — temporary hold**, and `INT-4`, the adjudication run against it. This round is unlike the four before it: **every checkable numeric claim reproduced exactly**, including the packetisation defect counts, the corrected canonical case, the grid arithmetic, the runtime figures, and an H4 power calculation none of the earlier reviews attempted. `INT-4` re-derived each one rather than adopting it — the packetisation against an independently written solver, the base-graph rate floor and the modulation interleaver against the pinned `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` source rather than its documentation, the review dates by rendering a scanned PDF that carries no extractable text, and the torchvision loader against current upstream documentation. One framing claim was **rejected**: that `run_id` "cannot pair systems". It is a structured tuple key, so pairing on every field except the system is perfectly well defined; the real and separate defect is that the key omitted `split`, the config and checkpoint hashes, the classifier variant and every system-specific setting, so distinct runs *collided*, while the per-image schema carried no join column at all. One claim was **sharpened**: the audit inferred that a 21/18 rescaling of the ER-1 projection needed replacing with code-block-aware arithmetic, which is right, but the direction is the opposite of what a reader would assume — the added grid points sit at the noisy end where BR-4 selects BPSK, whose channel-bit budget is a quarter of 16-QAM's and which needs two code blocks rather than eleven, so the worst-case figure **overstates** there. The verdict itself was not disputed: the corrected arithmetic preserves every feasible configuration, and the defects are contract and evidence defects that are cheap now and expensive once config, schemas and result files encode them.

- **AM-57** — **The primary estimand was not uniquely executable, and three of the four hypothesis tests did not test the claims their names make.** Changed: §2 gains an estimand paragraph ahead of the hypotheses; `params.evaluation.estimand`, `estimand_conditioning`, `seed_aggregation`, `bootstrap_unit`, `bootstrap_resample_scope`, `per_cell_resampling_prohibited`, `paired_test_role`, `h1_point_qualifier`, `h1_run_calibration_binds_decision`, `h1_run_calibration_alpha`, `h1_run_calibration_multiplier`, `h4_uses_h1_rule`, `h4_comparator`, `h4_mde_gate`, `h4_mde_simulation`, `h3_low_snr_point_db`, `h3_high_snr_point_db`, `h3_requires_magnitude_contraction`, `h2_did_reported_as`, `h2_thresholds_frozen`, `confirmatory_primary` and `secondary_inferential` added; `params.evaluation.h1_run_calibration` changed from sign_flip_permutation_over_image_trajectories to `centered_multiplier_bootstrap_over_image_trajectories`; `params.evaluation.h2_test` changed from paired_difference_in_differences to `intersection_union_of_two_one_sided_margins`; `params.evaluation.h2_thresholds_apply_to` changed from point_estimates to `one_sided_95_interval_bounds`; §2's family-wise note corrected from 13 points, 11 runs and 1.7e-4 to 16, 14 and 2.19e-4; H1, H2, H3 and H4 each now name their exact comparator; ER-10 rewritten; two new §16 risks. Why: five separate defects, each of which could manufacture a verdict for bookkeeping reasons. *(a)* ER-10 said the image bootstrap was "nested inside the seed hierarchy", but the same test images appear in all three cells, so images are **crossed** with cells — and at least five mutually incompatible readings of that sentence were available, several returning different verdicts on identical data. The estimand is now conditional on the three preregistered models, averages cells within an image, and bootstraps stable image IDs once with whole trajectories attached. *(b)* H1's permutation calibration was archived but explicitly did not affect the decision, so a measured null above 0.05 would have been recorded and ignored — that is measurement, not calibration; it now binds, and the null is centred to match the weak mean null actually being tested rather than the sharp exchangeability null a raw sign flip assumes. *(c)* H2 conjoined two point thresholds with a difference-in-differences test and never said which decided; drops of 20 pp and 0 pp give a 20 pp difference-in-differences while the classical arm misses the 30 pp cliff entirely, so the two margins are now an intersection-union test, which also needs no Bonferroni. *(d)* AM-39 caught a gap starting negative but not one running **+5 pp → −30 pp**, which starts positive, slopes downward, and describes the learned system being overtaken and then left behind; H3 now also requires the gap to contract in magnitude, with both endpoints named because "the low-SNR end" named no grid point on an irregular grid. *(e)* H4 invokes the H1 rule, so repairing H1 without saying so would have left H4 holding the old ambiguity. Also recorded: H4's minimum detectable effect is about 1.4 pp at 10% discordance and 3.2 pp at 50%, against a residual plausibly of 1–2 pp, so a prospective precision simulation is required before W8 spends training on it. **Rejected:** refitting H3 over the high-SNR region alone.
