It is immune to a humped gap reading flat, but it abandons the half of the grid where the effect is largest and leaves six points; the full-grid form is kept and the false-negative risk carried explicitly in §16. Raised by: EXT-6 (all five), INT-4 (the H3 scope decision and the power arithmetic).
- **AM-58** — **The artifact offered as proof of TS 38.212 conformance had four arithmetic defects while reporting zero failures, and W1's data, pairing and environment contracts were not frozen enough to be reproducible.** Changed: `spec/evidence/check_packetisation.py` rewritten and its record regenerated; `params.baseline.payload_solver_clamp`, `min_coderate_predicate`, `segmentation_requires_exact_division`, `systematic_length_rule`, `modulation_bit_interleaver`, `modulation_bit_interleaver_arg`, `modulation_bit_interleaver_required`, `ldpc_decoder_offset`, `ldpc_decoder_llr_clip`, `ldpc_decoder_schedule`, `ldpc_decoder_vn_update`, `ldpc_bler_reference_source`, `ldpc_bler_reference_must_match`, `cb_crc_polynomial`, `crc_spec`, `constellation_mapping`, `constellation_normalisation`, `per_packet_power_rescaling_permitted`, `realised_symbol_energy_logged`, `demapper`, `demapper_noise_variance_convention`, `standards_claim`, `standards_claim_excludes`, `packet_count_grid`, `packet_count_role`, `packet_count_gate`, `fixed_mcs_packet_count` and `downsample_axis_never_upscales` added; `params.baseline.outage_policy` changed from uniform_random_label to `frozen_constant_class_selected_on_validation` with `outage_class_selection`, `outage_class_tie_break`, `outage_class_frozen_before`, `outage_policy_sensitivity` and `outage_rng_key`; `params.baseline.j2k_cache_key` made content-addressed; `params.baseline.downsample_axis_px` made dataset-specific; `params.artifacts.run_id_key` extended and `run_id_form`, `analysis_cell_id_key`, `noise_id_key`, `pair_id_key`, `pair_id_excludes`, `rng_stream`, `rng_purposes`, `per_image_storage`, `per_image_manifest`, `inference_summary_file`, `inference_summary_schema` and `freeze_manifest_file` added; `params.artifacts.per_image_schema` gains every join column; dataset provenance, split-manifest and preprocessing keys added; a new `params.environment` section; `params.digital_semantic_control.framing_selector_bits`, `raw_escape_required`, `raw_escape_rule` and `budget_sized_against` added; new SR-20, SR-21 and SR-22; DEC-12, G-1, G-2, BR-1, BR-4, BR-10, BR-13, BR-14, BR-16, ER-9, SR-17, SR-18, SR-19 and PR-3 rewritten; §1's energy claim narrowed; W1 and W10 schedule rows changed. Why: the evidence script reported zero failures while 92 of its 215 rows had a non-byte-aligned `A` under a solver whose own parameter promises byte alignment, 21 had a non-integral `B'/C` silently rescued by `ceil` — TS 38.212 §5.2.2 defines `K' = B'/C` exactly, and NR obtains integrality from the TS 38.214 transport-block-size quantisation this project does not use — 47 of 103 BG2 rows computed filler from the selection `K_b` instead of the encoded `K = 10Z`, and the base-graph rate floor was tested strictly against the *smallest* `E_r`, i.e. on the block least likely to violate it, when `params.baseline.ldpc_impl` raises only on `r < floor` and BG1's mother code is exactly 1/3. Correcting all four keeps 215 configurations feasible and **every** headline-dataset configuration feasible, moves source capacity in 18 rows by −8 to +1 byte, and reduces the recorded clamps from six to three; the canonical case becomes A = 42,624 bits, 5,328 source bytes, B' = 42,792, K' = 7,132, Z = 352, filler 612 per block and 3,672 in total. The proof obligation is also broadened from the 72 configurations today's three provisional ratios name to all 144 on both headline datasets, since ER-3 may select any rung — which immediately surfaced that ER-9's admissible width/width pairs fall to one at the bottom rung. Separately: the modulation bit interleaver of §5.4.2.2 is applied by the library **only** when the modulation order is passed to the encoder, and the W0 probe never passed it, so omitting it would have quietly mis-mapped coded bits onto 16-QAM's unequally reliable bit channels; the offset-min-sum offset was declared pinned by G-2 but pinned by no parameter, defaulting to 0.5; `cb_crc_bits` did not name CRC24B; DEC-12's "the test split is touched once" was contradicted by four requirements that read test by design, and by W10, which put a test-derived headline curve in the same week as a gate that can restate the objectives; and BR-13 rejected a deterministic outage fallback on the ground that it "breaks pairing", which is false — a constant prediction is the most pairable fallback available, and the random draw it preferred desynchronises across systems that outage on different images. Raised by: EXT-6, with the library behaviour, the corrected solver and the broadened obligation verified independently by INT-4.
- **AM-59** — **AM-52's enlarged grid was never propagated, and the academic calendar was knowable all along.** Changed: `params.deliverables.review_weeks` third changed from 16 to 17; `params.deliverables.w1_start_date`, `review_dates`, `review_marks`, `review_dates_source`, `internal_report_freeze_week`, `contingency_week`, `contingency_week_allocation`, `hardware_alternative_clause`, `hardware_alternative_artifact`, `hardware_alternative_dossier_contents`, `hardware_alternative_decision_due_week`, `hardware_alternative_acceptance`, `proposal_registration_status` and `proposal_registration_owner` added; `review_dates_status` closed; `params.compute.er1_projected_ldpc_decode_hours_one_ratio` 2.07 → 2.42 and `..._two_ratios` 4.14 → 4.83, with `er1_projected_ldpc_decode_basis`, `er1_projected_ldpc_decode_hours_floor_one_ratio`, `er1_projected_ldpc_decode_hours_floor_basis`, `er1_image_transmissions_per_ldpc_arm`, `er1_j2k_test_decodes`, `er1_classifier_forwards_two_variants`, `schedule_cost_gate` and `schedule_cost_compared_as` added; `params.bandwidth.low_ratio_rule` corrected from smallest_ratio_at_least_two_rungs_below_headline to `exactly_two_ordered_ladder_rungs_below_headline`, with `low_ratio_boundary_rule`, `ceiling_definition`, `ceiling_rule` and `upper_grid_saturation_check` added; `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_corpus_split`, `artifact_finetune_corpus_breadth`, `artifact_finetune_recipe`, `fallback_ladder`, `fallback_ladder_selection_split`, `fallback_ladder_max_epochs`, `fallback_ladder_stop_rule` and `fallback_ladder_param_cap_applies` added; §13's table extended to W17 with G-10 moved to the start of W9; DEC-16, ER-3, BR-12, G-8, G-10, G-11 and §16 updated; PR-9 rewritten and PR-10 added. Why: AM-52 added three SNR points and changed nothing that counts them, so §2 still said 13 points and 11 runs, §16 still said 18 points, 424k classifier forwards and 212k JPEG 2000 decodes, and the ER-1 projections were an eighteenth-of-a-grid short — the true figures are 16, 14, 247,275 decodes, 494,550 forwards and 2.42/4.83 hours, with a 0.44 h floor because the headline projection assumes the worst-case eleven code blocks that only 16-QAM rate 5/6 needs. The review dates had been recorded as pending against a circular described as unreadable; it is a scanned document with no extractable text, which is not the same thing, and rendering its two pages resolves all four dates directly: First Review 18–22 August (W4), Second 29 September–3 October (W10), Final **17–21 November (W17, not the W16 this document carried)** and report due 20 November — inside Final Review week, hence an internal freeze two weeks earlier and W16 allocated deliberately rather than left as buffer. The same document's clause 5 expects a hardware implementation or, failing that, "significant design aspects with an application to real world problems", which a one-page deployment note is thin protection for; and it makes proposal submission part of **registration**, while the only copy in this repository is a blank template. Also corrected: `low_ratio_rule` literally selected the ladder *bottom* rather than the `r_1_12` its own provisional value records; ER-3's "high-SNR ceiling" and "within `t`" named no computable quantity; BR-12's artifact corpus had no named split, so it could have been built from the validation images BR-4's second pass then selects over; G-1's "switch backbone or extend training" fallback named no order, bound, selection split or stopping rule; and DEC-16 still claimed adaptive modulation leaves "the cliff H2 depends on" untouched, which AM-53 superseded by moving H2 onto a curve that does not adapt at all. Raised by: EXT-6 (all of it), INT-4 (the code-block direction, the date arithmetic, and reading the circular).

**Amendment round 6 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: `INT-5`, a cross-document consistency audit run immediately after round 5, checking every hand-written file in the repository against the amended specification and against the generated views. Round 5 was large and touched the schedule, so this pass existed to find what it had left inconsistent — and it found one substantive defect of its own making, which is why the round exists rather than being folded into the previous one.

- **AM-60** — **The test split was released three weeks before anything was frozen, and two experiments still read it during the week that had just been moved off it.** Changed: new **G-12** at W11, the test-release gate; `params.evaluation.test_access_gate` changed from G-10 to `G-12`, with `test_campaign_is_single` and `test_campaign_covers` added; §13's W10 row rebuilt so ER-4, ER-11 and ER-12 are *built and rehearsed on validation* there rather than run against test, and its W11 row now carries the whole single campaign; ER-6 rewritten; `docs/crossover-explained.md`, `README.md`, `AGENTS.md` and `NEXT.md` corrected against the amended §13 and §2. Why: AM-58 moved W10's rehearsal onto validation to close a selection channel, and AM-59 moved G-10 to the start of W9 so the crossover decision would not be taken in the week its own review package is written — but `test_access_gate` was left pointing at G-10, which now *precedes* W10, so SR-22's guard would have released at W9 and the entire point of both changes would have been lost. Worse, ER-11 and ER-12 remained scheduled at W10 while reading the test split at sweep strength under ER-6, so the split would have been opened in the very week it had been sealed. The underlying error is one this document has now made twice: **a rule and the schedule that has to obey it were edited in the same round without re-reading one against the other**, which is exactly what AM-47's process rule exists to prevent and exactly what it failed to catch, because §13 was re-read for *content* rather than against every parameter the amended text now cites. The repair also closes a structural gap that predates both: opening the test split is the single most irreversible act in the project and was the only such act with no gate on it. Also corrected in the same pass, none of them requiring their own entry: §17's preamble now states that `EXT-n` identifiers are labels rather than an ordinal count of review passes, because `README.md` and `AGENTS.md` had counted them differently; `docs/crossover-explained.md`'s hypothesis table, multiplicity arithmetic, operating-point rule and G-10 week were all stale against rounds 5's amendments; and `README.md`'s review-pass count and status paragraph were rewritten against the current checks. Raised by: INT-5.

**Amendment round 7 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: `INT-6`, closing the one implementation decision AM-58 left open when it required a lockfile without naming what produces it. Recorded as its own round because it is a decision the author took rather than a defect anyone found.

- **AM-61** — **SR-21 required a hashed lockfile and named no tool to build it.** Changed: `params.environment.lock_tool` = `uv`, `lock_tool_version_min` = 0.11, and `lock_source` = `requirements.in` added; SR-21's verify clause now names the resolver; `AGENTS.md` records the commands. Why: `params.environment.lock_format` fixes the *artifact* — pip requirements with hashes — but not the resolver, and "one documented command from a clean checkout" is not documented until the command exists. Both candidates emit the required format, so this is a decision rather than a correction: `pip-tools` is the conservative choice and adds no tooling, `uv` resolves substantially faster and handles the separate CUDA wheel index more cleanly, which matters here because `params.environment.torch_index_url` is the exact mechanism AM-23 showed can silently yield a CPU build. **`uv` chosen by the author on 2026-07-28**, and it is already present on the primary device at 0.11.32. The format is unchanged either way, so `params.environment.lock_file` stays portable and a reader without `uv` can still install it with plain `pip install --require-hashes`; the tool is pinned for reproducibility of *resolution*, not as a runtime dependency. Raised by: INT-6 (the gap), author (the choice).

**Amendment round 8 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: `INT-7`. Round 6 recorded that a rule and the schedule obeying it had been edited without being read against each other; the same class of drift then recurred *within the session that recorded it*. This round replaces the process rule's reliance on discipline with a check.

- **AM-62** — **AM-47's propagation rule was enforced by remembering to, and three rounds running that was not enough.** Changed: `tools/check_doc_consistency.py` added and recorded in `AGENTS.md`; §17's process rule below now names it. Why: AM-47 requires an amendment that changes what a parameter *means* to state which sections were re-read against it. It has been honoured in intent and missed in fact three times — AM-52's enlarged grid left §2's family-wise arithmetic, §16's cost prose and the ER-1 projections stale for three rounds (AM-59); AM-58 and AM-59 each moved half of the test-access rule and left the halves pointing at each other (AM-60); and the G-10 → G-12 correction from AM-60 failed to reach the prose summarising it, in the same session. `gen_spec_views.py --check` never saw any of these, because it validates `SPEC.md` against itself and regenerates the derived views — the **hand-written** files were guarded by nothing. The new tool derives ground truth from `params.generated.yaml` and `spec/evidence/packetisation_record.json` and enforces the repository's existing convention mechanically: **a superseded value may appear only in a block that cites the amendment which superseded it**, which is exactly what "corrections carry an `(AM-n)` back-reference" already demands, so append-only history passes and unlabelled staleness fails. It also checks that every `AM-n` and `G-n` reference resolves, that `params.` citations outside `SPEC.md` resolve, and that current requirement and amendment counts agree with `SPEC.md`. Two design notes worth keeping, both found by testing the checker against injected drift rather than by trusting it: it works on **blocks** rather than lines, because a back-reference routinely sits a line or two below the value it explains; and a cited `AM-n` grants an exemption only if that amendment **exists**, since a mistyped or invented reference would otherwise disable the rule entirely. That second one is the AM-58 lesson applied to this tool itself — a check that passes is not evidence that the thing checked is right, including when the thing checked is the check. Raised by: INT-7; author (that it be a committed tool rather than a scratch script).

**Amendment round 9 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: the author, closing the last item that no audit could resolve.

- **AM-63** — **Proposal registration confirmed complete; PR-10 closed.** Changed: `params.deliverables.proposal_registration_status` from unverified_repo_copy_is_blank_template to `complete_confirmed_by_author_2026_07_28`; PR-10 marked closed with its reasoning retained. Why: AM-59 opened this because the circular makes submitting the proposal to the guide part of the **registration** process, and the only copy in this repository is the blank attachment — which is evidence of nothing, since the template would be blank whether or not a completed copy went to the guide by another route. The author confirms registration is complete. Recorded rather than assumed for two reasons: the status is a parameter, so changing it silently would breach this document's own convention; and this was the single carried risk with **no graceful degradation** — every other failure mode in this project has a fallback ladder, a recorded alternative outcome, or a gate that catches it, whereas an unregistered project scores nothing however well the engineering goes. Note what this does **not** close: `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_acceptance` is a separate obligation whose acknowledgement is recorded when PR-9's dossier is delivered, and `params.deliverables.hardware_alternative_decision_due_week` still places that conversation before the First Review. Raised by: AM-59 (the gap), author (the resolution).

**Amendment round 10 — 2026-07-28, W1 open.** Source: `INT-8`, prompted by the author asking what the First Review actually requires. Reading the rubric spreadsheet against the repository found that a third of that review has no artifact behind it, and that PR-8 required a committed package without saying where it lives or what it is derived from.

- **AM-64** — **PR-8 required a review package with no location, no snapshot, and no readiness condition — while a third of the First Review's marks rested on two artifacts that do not exist.** Changed: `params.deliverables.review_package_dir`, `review_snapshot_mechanism`, `review_snapshot_tag_format`, `review_1_ready_when`, `review_1_rubric_criteria`, `review_1_submarks_each` and `review_1_slot_minutes` added; PR-8 gains clause (d) and an extended verify clause. Why: the rubric spreadsheet scores the First Review as **six criteria at five sub-marks each — Motivation, Objectives, Hypothesis, Problem Survey, Subject Knowledge, Time Plan — 30 sub-marks scaled to 10**, which confirms AM-46's weighting from the sheet's own structure. Two of those six are **Problem Survey**, which is PR-1's literature review, and **Time Plan**, which is PR-2's Gantt — and neither exists, so ten of thirty sub-marks currently have nothing behind them at a review three weeks out. Recording the readiness condition converts that from something to remember into something checkable. The snapshot mechanism is an annotated **tag** rather than a branch, because a review branch diverges from `main`, displays as N commits behind, and invites a merge that must not happen, while a tag is immutable, exportable with `git archive`, and attributable with `git show`; a markdown snapshot was also rejected, since it duplicates content that then rots. Two details worth keeping: `Presentation` is **not** a First Review criterion — it first appears at the Second — so these marks are for substance rather than delivery; and the rubric spreadsheet's own dates are the 2023 template AM-46 flagged and AM-59 superseded, so `params.deliverables.review_dates` governs. This entry does **not** change what PR-1 or PR-2 require, only when they become blocking. Raised by: author (the question), INT-8 (the rubric reading and the gap).

**Amendment round 11 — 2026-07-28, W1 batch 1 built.** Source: `IMP-1`, the first commit of project code. All three entries are findings from *building* the environment lock rather than from reading about it — the same pattern as AM-24, where the W0 spike's defects were invisible on paper. None changes an experimental parameter; all three change what the lockfile must contain, and each was discovered by a command failing.

- **AM-65** — **The lockfile MUST cover the spec tooling, because a lockfile-exact install removes what it does not name.** Changed: `params.environment.lock_covers_spec_tooling` = true and `params.environment.pyyaml` = 6.0.3 added; SR-21 gains the constraint. Why: `params.environment.lock_tool` installs by synchronising the environment *to* the lockfile — packages absent from it are uninstalled, not merely left alone. The spec tooling (`tools/gen_spec_views.py`, `tools/check_doc_consistency.py`) needs PyYAML and, per AM-61's decision to install alongside the existing virtual environment rather than beside it, shares that environment. A runtime-only lockfile would therefore have had the **first command of W1 uninstall PyYAML and break all three of the checks that guard this document** — silently, since nothing would fail until the next spec edit. Two alternatives were rejected: installing without synchronising leaves the environment a superset of the lockfile, so SR-21's "reproducible from a clean checkout" stops being checkable and a stray package can linger undetected; and a second virtual environment for the runtime stack contradicts AM-61 and would make every documented command ambiguous about which interpreter it means. `requirements.txt` is deliberately **unchanged** and remains the dependency-light bootstrap that lets the generator and the evidence scripts run on a machine with nothing else installed. Raised by: IMP-1 (found before it fired, by reading what synchronising does), author (chose the superset).

- **AM-66** — **The lockfile MUST embed its own index URLs, or nothing can install it.** Changed: `params.environment.lock_emit_index_url` = true and `params.environment.lock_index_strategy` = unsafe-best-match added; SR-21 gains both constraints. Why: this is the trap AM-23 predicted, one level further in. Two separate flags are needed and *neither* is the default. At resolution, the default strategy stops at the first index carrying a package **name** — the public index carries `torch`, just not the CUDA-suffixed local version — so a plain resolve fails outright, which is at least loud. At install, the emitted lockfile by default records **no index at all**, so it resolves against the public index only and the CUDA wheels cannot be found. This one was not loud: it surfaced as an unsatisfiable-requirement error whose text names only the version, giving no hint that the index is missing rather than the wheel. It also **falsifies SR-21's portability clause** — a lockfile that omits its indices cannot be installed by plain `pip install --require-hashes` either, so the clause was untestable as written and would have passed review by inspection. Both flags are now recorded as parameters rather than as shell history in a documentation file, because the lockfile is regenerated whenever a pin moves and the flags must move with it. Raised by: IMP-1 (the install failed).

- **AM-67** — **The CPU-only install path is materialised rather than promised.** Changed: `params.environment.cpu_lock_source` = requirements-cpu.in and `params.environment.cpu_lock_file` = requirements-cpu.lock added; SR-21 names them. Why: SR-21 already required a CPU-only path for `params.environment.cpu_install_path_required_for` and named no artifact for it, which made it a sentence rather than a deliverable — and G-1 accepts the install path, so it must exist by the end of W1 regardless. Building it now costs one compile against the public index and fixes one lockfile convention instead of two written weeks apart, the second under demo deadline pressure. The CPU pins differ from the CUDA pins in the **local version only**; the upstream releases are identical, so the two paths do not silently diverge in behaviour. Note what this path does *not* do: it is for `analysis` and `demo`, it trains nothing, and it is **not expected to pass the test suite** — the CUDA assertion test carries no skip marker and no environment-variable escape hatch on purpose, since a variable exported once in a shell profile would disarm the only check that catches a CPU build on the machine that actually trains. Raised by: IMP-1, author (chose to build it now).

**Amendment round 12 — 2026-07-28, W1 batch 2.** Source: `IMP-2`, implementing the configuration layer and reconciling the one schedule contradiction adjudicated before the batch.

- **AM-68** — **SR-1 required a configuration file but did not make its own verify clause executable.** Changed: new `params.config` section; SR-1 now fixes the committed experiment-choice-file policy, resolved-config archive, canonical SHA-256 hash form, literal-lint scope, exclusions, exempt values and reasoned annotation token. Why: without a location, format, resolution policy or hash rule, two runs could make different choices while both claiming to be config-derived; without lint parameters, the hard-coded-literal check would hard-code its own policy. A committed file per concrete run was rejected because the declared sweeps create thousands of unreviewable files; committed experiment choices plus sweep axes, with the fully resolved per-run configuration archived beside results, preserves reviewability and reproducibility. Raised by: IMP-2, author (the file and lint policy).
- **AM-69** — **Two fields in the run identity had no defined source.** Changed: `params.config.analysis_version`, its bump rule and `params.config.dataset_version_rule` added; SR-18 now defines dataset version from the SR-20 archive SHA-256 and analysis version from the explicit config value. Why: `params.artifacts.run_id_key` names `dataset_version` and `analysis_version`, but neither existed anywhere in the parameter tree, so SR-18's run ID was not constructible. `config_hash` and `checkpoint_id` are runtime-computed by design and are not gaps. Raised by: IMP-2.
- **AM-70** — **The H4 detection-floor risk named an impossible deadline.** Changed: §16 now says the prospective precision simulation runs before the test split opens at G-12, not before W8 training. Why: the simulation measures validation discordance between learned and er9_digital, ER-9 is not built until W9, and `params.evaluation.h4_mde_gate` is G-11 in W9; it therefore cannot precede W8. This is the same defect class as AM-33, where G-8 was asked to decide a crossover before a learned curve existed. The schedule and gates were already internally consistent and remain unchanged; the correct safeguard is the wording that already followed the stale phrase — the precision limitation is known before the test split opens. Raised by: author, IMP-2.

**Amendment round 13 — 2026-07-28, W1 batch 4.** Source: `IMP-3`, implementing the preprocessing contract and resolving the identity ambiguity exposed before the split manifests exist.

- **AM-71** — **"Canonical source bytes" admitted two incompatible stable-sample identities.** Changed: `params.datasets.stable_sample_id_rule` now reads `sha256_of_original_per_sample_source_bytes_truncated_16_hex`, and SR-17 defines those bytes as the exact per-sample payload before decode, colour conversion or preprocessing; for container datasets it is the sample payload rather than the whole containing file. Why: reading "canonical source bytes" as SR-19's canonical RGB bytes would make every resize, crop, interpolation or colour-policy amendment change every sample ID, retroactively invalidating the committed SR-17 manifests and every `pair_id` and `noise_id` derived from them. The rejected alternative would therefore turn a preprocessing change into an identity migration across all cached artifacts and results. Hashing the immutable source payload keeps sample identity orthogonal to the versioned preprocessing contract, while `dataset_version` already identifies the downloaded archive. §13 and every verify clause were re-read against the clarified meaning; the W1 ordering and gate remain correct, and SR-17's verify clause now tests invariance to preprocessing changes. Raised by: author (the ambiguity and required resolution), IMP-3 (the interpretation and implementation).

**Amendment round 14 — 2026-07-29, W1 batches 1–4 remediation.** Source: `IMP-4`, a full sweep of the implemented W1 contracts before loaders, manifests or results make them expensive to change. These five amendments are one remediation round; §13 and every verify clause were re-read against all five, and the W1 ordering and G-1 scope remain unchanged.

- **AM-72** — **`config_hash` fingerprinted a thin selection record rather than the experiment it claimed to identify.** Changed: `params.config.fingerprint_schema_version`, `fingerprint_parameter_roots` and `fingerprint_excluded_roots` added; SR-1 and `RunConfig` now archive immutable snapshots of every scientific/runtime root and hash canonical JSON of `{fingerprint_schema_version, resolved, parameters}`. Administrative `config`, `demo`, `hardware_tier23` and `deliverables` are excluded; `analysis_version` and `dataset_version` remain resolved fields. Why: changing preprocessing, architecture, baseline packetisation, evaluation semantics, dependency versions or artifact identity rules previously left `config_hash` unchanged unless the small resolved choice mapping also moved, so scientifically different arms could share a run fingerprint. Any fingerprinted change now intentionally invalidates every run identity. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; SR-1's hash tests now cover every included root, schema version and mapping order. Raised by: IMP-4.
- **AM-73** — **The CPU-only lock resolved a CUDA dependency stack.** Changed: `params.environment.torch_cpu` = `2.13.0+cpu`, `torchvision_cpu` = `0.28.0+cpu` and `torch_cpu_index_url` = the official CPU wheel index added; SR-21 requires the hashed lock and a clean plain-pip install to reject normalised `cuda-*`, `nvidia-*` and `triton` distributions and to report `torch.version.cuda is None`; `tools/verify_cpu_lock.py --clean-install` added. Why: unsuffixed `torch==2.13.0` from PyPI pulled `cuda-bindings`, `cuda-toolkit` and related distributions, so the file called CPU-only was neither structurally nor operationally CPU-only. CUDA assertions stay unchanged because the CPU path is for analysis/demo, not the training test suite. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; G-1 still accepts the CPU path through SR-21. Raised by: IMP-4.
- **AM-74** — **The preprocessing and RNG contracts still admitted unbound construction and library-default drift.** Changed: SR-19 makes `canonicalize_source(source_bytes, dataset)` the only supported product factory through a private decoder registry and read-only protocol; SR-18 fixes exact identity fields for all four RNG purposes and defines init `component_path`; SSIM now passes every public result-affecting argument explicitly and binds scikit-image 0.26.0's effective 11-sample Gaussian support numerically without passing undocumented `truncate` or claiming explicit `win_size` control. Why: a public decoded-pixel constructor bypassed the source-byte identity boundary, arbitrary RNG identity mappings could create incomparable streams under the same purpose, and SSIM defaults could move a reported metric on a dependency update. Real decoders remain deferred to the loader batch. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; SR-18 and SR-19's verification now includes AST/import boundaries, exact-key rejection and the Gaussian fixture. Raised by: IMP-4.
- **AM-75** — **Determinism was open-ended and SR-21 overstated what the repository provisions.** Changed: SR-12 permits only the explicit cuDNN deterministic/benchmark mappings and verifies read-back; SR-21 now says hashed locks reproduce Python dependencies while OpenJPEG 2.5.4 is externally provisioned and exactly verified, records optional `openjpeg_version`, and requires J2K preflight before artifact creation. `pacman -S openjpeg2` is the reference installation; `openjpeg2 2.5.4-1`, observed 2026-07-29 on this machine, is dated evidence only. Why: the structural `cudnn_*` attribute mapping could silently accept a newly named but semantically unaudited setting, while a Python lockfile cannot provision a system OpenJPEG library and the package revision is distribution-specific. Learned-only runs may record null when OpenJPEG is unavailable; J2K runs may not. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; no J2K implementation is added in this round. Raised by: IMP-4.
- **AM-76** — **The documentation checker guarded a fixed list of eight files and treated an unmarked historical plan as current without a usable exclusion contract.** Changed: the process rule and checker now scan current root Markdown, `configs/**/*.md`, `docs/**/*.md`, `spec/evidence/**/*.md`, `spec/SPEC.md`, `requirements.txt` and `requirements*.in`, never generated locks, generated spec views, audits or course material; coverage is named “current hand-written documentation”. A historical plan is excluded only with the exact opening marker `<!-- capstone-doc-status: historical-plan -->` and exact visible banner `> **Historical snapshot:** This plan is retained for provenance and may contain superseded commands or status. See [`NEXT.md`](relative/path/to/NEXT.md) for current repository state.`, whose target must resolve relative to that plan to root `NEXT.md`; every malformed case remains scanned and emits an explicit banner finding. Stale rules added for the old partial-fingerprint and false CPU-only claims. Why: adding a current doc silently removed it from consistency coverage, while excluding plans by directory or marker alone would let live instructions evade the guard. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; the existing batch-2 plan receives the exact provenance banner. Raised by: IMP-4.

**Amendment round 15 — 2026-07-29, W1 dataset provenance and manifests.** Source: `IMP-5`, implementing the bounded loader/manifest batch before the reference classifier.

- **AM-77** — **Dataset provenance and pre-freeze manifest construction made executable.** Changed: DEC-12, SR-17, SR-20, SR-22 and G-1 now permit one provenance-only pre-G-12 scan of published test labels and original payload bytes while explicitly forbidding test decode, canonicalization, preprocessing, pixel exposure, model-facing loading, inference, accuracy and selection; model-facing test access remains sealed behind SR-22 and G-12. The source payload is fixed as exact encoded JPEG bytes for Imagenette-160, the exact per-image `train_X.bin`/`test_X.bin` record before axis transpose or PIL for STL-10, and the exact 3,072-byte per-image CHW batch record before HWC or PIL for CIFAR-10; paths, names, positions, labels, whole archives/containers/batches, PIL serializations and decoded/canonical pixels are excluded. `params.datasets.class_index_source` is generalized to dataset-specific authoritative metadata, with sorted Imagenette directory identifiers, STL-10 `class_names.txt` metadata order and CIFAR-10 `batches.meta` label-name order mapped deterministically to contiguous zero-based labels. Manifest bytes are fixed by `params.datasets.manifest_encoding`, `manifest_newline`, `manifest_columns`, `manifest_splits`, `manifest_sort`, `manifest_hash` and `manifest_duplicate_id_policy`; the carve fixes `split_draw`, and each dataset now records `archive_filename`, `archive_bytes`, `manifest_filename`, `manifest_sha256`, `source_payload` and its specific `class_index_source`, with archive/extraction roots under `params.datasets.archive_dir` and `extracted_dir`. Why: the earlier contracts required committed source-bound manifests but did not define the only safe pre-freeze way to identify the published test rows, the exact container-record boundary, a metadata mapping for non-folder datasets, canonical hashable CSV bytes, or the archive byte-length/filename evidence needed to distinguish the fetched object. One public registry now rejects test while the isolated provenance path can materialize identity-only rows. §13, every verify clause, DEC-12, SR-17–SR-22, G-1, the W1 schedule row and the current hand-written documentation were re-read; no classifier, checkpoint, inference or test evaluation is introduced. Raised by: author, IMP-5.
- **AM-78** — **Reference-classifier training made deterministic, resumable and uniquely selectable.** Changed: `params.reference_classifier` now fixes the from-scratch Torchvision implementation, null weights, clean seed/variant, loss, Nesterov, deterministic keyed batch order, no dropped batches or mixed precision, exact warmup-plus-cosine scheduler controls, validation/checkpoint selection and schema; `params.artifacts` adds `batch_order` identity, classifier artifact locations and exact-file-byte checkpoint identity. Learning rate is set at epoch start: for zero-based epoch `e`, total epochs `E`, warmup `W`, base rate `L`, warmup start factor `S` and minimum `M`, `lr(e)=L*(S+(1-S)*e/max(W-1,1))` for `e<W`; otherwise with `j=e-W`, `T=E-W`, `lr(e)=M+(L-M)*0.5*(1+cos(pi*j/max(T-1,1)))`. Why: the recipe, validation-only choice and checkpointing were prescribed but result-affecting trainer boundaries remained ambiguous, allowing non-reproducible initialisation, order, scheduler endpoints, ties or resumes. The dataset ladder, base 100-epoch recipe, fallback ladder, validation-only selection, test seal and pretrained-weight prohibition are unchanged. Raised by: W1 reference-classifier implementation batch.
- **AM-79** — **G-2's statistical reference and W3 fixture were still unnamed at implementation time.** Changed: `ldpc_golden_vector_asset_sha256` now pins the complete srsRAN `phy_testvectors.tar` release asset rather than only its three extracted LDPC archives; the lifting-size set, mother-code columns, punctured columns and base-graph/K_b thresholds are made executable parameters; `ldpc_bler_reference_source` now names the MIT-licensed Lcrypto 5G LDPC simulation platform at immutable commit `2fde4c43bae04c0d8397b3e7e46eaa6070e16b3c`; `ldpc_bler_reference` fixes its codeload checksum, decoder and graph files, literal-port reconstruction, K = 128, N = 256, BG2, Z = 22, rate 1/2, per-modulation Eb/N0 grids, 5,000 blocks per point, seed, confidence level and log-BLER interpolation; `progressive_packetisation_sensitivity` freezes the required 1/2/4-packet design while deferring execution until G-8 supplies the validation-selected headline ratio. Why: TS 38.212 contains no curve, a graph image cannot support a numeric gate, inner-archive hashes did not authenticate the release object that the fetcher actually downloads, and running a “headline-ratio” sensitivity before G-8 would either pre-empt the blind ratio selection or invent an operating point. The reference source explicitly implements 5G NR graphs and flooding OMS with configurable offset and iteration limit; the committed reconstruction disables only its optional parity-stop so both arms execute exactly 50 iterations, and applies the already-fixed mapper/demapper to reconstruct BPSK, QPSK and 16-QAM under one code. No bandwidth ratio, codec point, learned model or evaluation split changes. Raised by: W3 implementation and G-2 preflight.

**Amendment round 16 — 2026-07-31, W4 PB_2C corrective provenance and accounting repair.** Source: `IMP-6`, the audit of the provisional PB_2 bounded integration.

- **AM-80** — **Two of CIFAR-10's three configured JPEG 2000 encode axes were invalid codec configurations that no rule resolved.** Changed: `params.baseline.downsample_axis_px` for CIFAR-10 becomes the single native 32 px rung, dropping 24 px and 16 px; BR-1's dataset-specific-axis clause records why. Why: `params.baseline.j2k_resolutions` is a flat 6, which requires every tile dimension to be at least `2**5 = 32` px, so OpenJPEG hard-errors at 24 px and 16 px for *every* image and *every* budget, before rate control ever runs — these are setup faults, not useful low-rate candidates, and a per-axis reason of `codec_configuration_error` is not a channel result. BR-1/AM-51 froze `j2k_resolutions` flat and AM-58 made the axis ladder dataset-specific in different rounds, and the two were never checked against each other because the transparency-bitrate probe encoded Imagenette-160 only, whose four rungs are all at least 64 px. Retaining the native rung is chosen over an axis-dependent or clamped wavelet-resolution rule because CIFAR-10 is a DEC-1 plumbing smoke dataset, dynamic clamping would introduce a new codec rule and further cache-identity complexity without helping either headline dataset, and the change keeps invalid configurations out of the BR-4 candidate table before that sweep is ever composed. Consequence recorded deliberately: `downsample_axis_px` participates in the content-addressed codec configuration hash, so **every J2K cache key changes**; see AM-82 for the one campaign this affects. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; no bandwidth ratio, SNR grid, code rate, modulation, learned model or evaluation split changes, and no Imagenette-160 or STL-10 axis moves. Raised by: W4 PB_1C C1.5 (which recorded the conflict and deliberately left it open), adjudicated at W4 PB_2C.
- **AM-81** — **BR-11 named `header_bytes` and `payload_bytes` but never defined them arithmetically, and the implementation averaged them over delivered rows only.** Changed: BR-11 now fixes `bytes_sent = source_bytes = A/8`, defines `header_bytes` as all structural codestream bytes (SOC, every main-header marker segment, every SOT marker segment, every tile-part header through and including SOD, EOC, and the equivalent structural bytes of every tile-part) and `payload_bytes` as all tile-part data bytes after SOD and before the next tile-part boundary, requires `emitted_codestream_bytes = header_bytes + payload_bytes`, keeps `payload_filler_bytes = bytes_sent − emitted_codestream_bytes` reported separately rather than folded into either column, and fixes the aggregate denominator as every row that emitted a codestream — `delivered` and `decode_failure` — excluding the two infeasibility verdicts where no codestream exists; BR-11's verify clause now requires a known-answer parser test with a non-zero `Psot`, a multi-tile-part case and a `Psot = 0` case, a decode-failure-only aggregate retaining both columns, and exact reconciliation of both byte identities; `params.config.analysis_version` is bumped from 1 to 2 and SR-18 records why. Why: the columns were an interpretation rather than a quotation, so a reader could equally have taken `payload_bytes` to mean `payload_bits/8`, and PB_2 flagged the ambiguity rather than resolving it. Calling the tile-part data "entropy-coded sample data" is wrong because it may also carry packet-header information. The delivered-only denominator meant a cell in which every row failed to decode reported no overhead at all, which is precisely the regime where format overhead dominates the budget and where BR-11's rationale bites hardest; the columns must be null only when no codestream was emitted. Redefining an aggregate column's meaning and denominator is an analysis-implementation change under `params.config.analysis_version_bump_rule`, so the version bump is required rather than optional, and it intentionally re-namespaces every `run_id` and `config_hash`. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; no requirement about accuracy, coverage, PSNR, SSIM, PAPR or any hypothesis estimand changes, and `params.baseline.container_policy` is unchanged. Raised by: W4 PB_2C.
- **AM-82** — **AM-80 moves the codec configuration hash, and the transparency-bitrate probe verifier re-derived that hash from HEAD rather than binding what the campaign actually ran.** Changed: the probe's codec configuration is bound as history and verified by reproducing the recorded snapshot's own hash from the archived, byte-pinned snapshot, and the difference between that historical hash and the current one is permitted only by exactly one recorded off-measurement-path drift record naming this amendment, the archived probe evidence commit, the superseded and current codec configuration hashes, the exact changed parameter path with its old and new values, the fact that the probe encoded `imagenette160` exclusively, and a code-backed reachability argument that the configured-axis selector reads only its own dataset's ladder. Why: `params.baseline.downsample_axis_px` participates in the content-addressed codec configuration hash, so AM-80 changes it for every dataset even though it removes only CIFAR-10 rungs; the probe is a completed 68,000-cell validation-only campaign whose Imagenette-160 encodes cannot have depended on a CIFAR-10 rung, so re-running it would spend real compute to reproduce identical numbers while silently deleting the evidence that a parameter moved. This follows the G-2 precedent exactly: drift is recorded and byte-pinned, never regenerated away. The record is deliberately singular and fail-closed — it is not a drift allowlist, it does not make codec hash changes acceptable in general, and any further codec drift, any change to an Imagenette axis or to a shared J2K setting, a missing archived snapshot, or a failed reachability argument must fail the verifier rather than be inherited. §13 and every verify clause were re-read; the probe's recorded cells, budgets, cache keys, bitrates and transparency conclusions are unchanged, no campaign is re-run, and G-1, G-7 and G-2 are unaffected because they bind archived configurations or bind the generated parameter file as history. Raised by: W4 PB_2C, on discovering that AM-80 alone would have failed `tools/verify_transparency_bitrate_probe.py`.
- **AM-83** — **A monolithic “primary device” model could neither admit the qualified Pascal host nor prevent environment changes from being confounded with scientific choices.** Changed: DEC-4, SR-1, SR-11, SR-12 and SR-21 are amended; SR-23 and the execution-profile registry/policy/qualification/writer-authentication parameters are added; fingerprint schema 2 includes the frozen profile; `local_4060_cu130` and `confessor_pascal_cu126` are independent profiles rather than a replacement chain; profile-specific provenance, fail-closed dependency/GPU authentication, no silent mid-run switching, sweep/comparison/training/test scheduling rules, sole-writer custody and non-scientific cross-profile qualification are mandatory. Historical schema-1 artifacts retain exact archived parameter/config bytes and the local profile through one narrow amendment-bound additive compatibility rule that rejects all unrelated drift and never reinterprets them as Pascal. Authenticated GitHub HTTPS write access, internal hashes/contracts, durable checkpoint commits, successful push and local/remote parity remain mandatory for scientific writers; commit signing is optional prospectively, while historical signing requirements, signatures and incidents remain facts. Why: available compute is not experimental interchangeability; hardware, CUDA, PyTorch and numerical kernels may affect outcomes, while Git object identity plus authenticated transport and internal content authentication provide the required durable custody without provisioning project-specific private signing keys. Raised by: owner-directed two-profile architecture and 2026-08-15 authentication clarification.
- **AM-84** — **The paused local G8_C campaign must not be spliced into a Pascal final table.** Changed: BR-17 records the owner-directed migration before BLER-table freeze: preserve and authenticate the actual `local_4060_cu130` campaign boundary byte-for-byte as valid superseded history, including its trailing incomplete transaction; create a clean `confessor_pascal_cu126` successor at 0/3,213; execute all 3,213 identities at 5,000 trials each under unchanged G8 physics; allow old evidence only for provenance, diagnostics and cross-profile qualification; and permit it zero successor-table coverage. The successor uses two UUID-bound workers with canonical modulo-two sharding, confessor as sole writer, authenticated durable recovery, and cannot open full-strength science until merge, main parity, lock recreation, final qualification, authenticated HTTPS push and a pushed zero-coverage marker. Why: migrating the final campaign off the laptop is an operational decision, not a scientific invalidation of the 748 authenticated accepted local units, and mixing profiles inside one final characterization table would confound execution environment with the campaign. Raised by: owner decision before G8_C table freeze.
- **AM-85** — **The Pascal profile completed its non-scientific eligibility gate.** Changed: the Pascal profile role moves from pending qualification to eligible production execution after both UUID-bound devices passed synthetic CUDA/LDPC/DJSCC/checkpoint/restart checks, exact RNG byte hashes matched the local profile, paired decoder diagnostics passed the preregistered disagreement and displacement criteria, and OpenJPEG fixture codestream/decoded-pixel parity passed; the two Pascal UUIDs remain one named profile with exact UUID provenance per work unit. Why: eligibility must follow measured qualification rather than the expected hardware tuple, while preserving the rule that Pascal is not a replacement for local RTX4060/cu130. Raised by: qualification evidence on `confessor`, 2026-08-15.
- **AM-86** — **Mandatory Git publication after every Pascal successor batch would make a coding agent part of the availability path even though scientific validity is already carried by crash-atomic, campaign-bound per-unit evidence on the sole writer.** Changed: SR-23 and BR-17 receive one narrow custody-cadence exception for the owner-authorized `confessor_pascal_cu126` G8_C successor: immutable authenticated request/result/state evidence may accumulate continuously in its separate mutable runtime; Git publication may occur after the unattended campaign or at an owner-selected manual checkpoint; and loss of not-yet-published bytes is an explicitly accepted owner custody risk. Authenticated evidence, final complete-coverage verification, and the existing sole-writer/profile/grid/PHY/protected-access rules remain the scientific validity boundary; complete reconcile/commit/authenticated-HTTPS-push/fetch-parity is still mandatory before BLER-table freeze or release to G8_D. The exception changes neither campaign identity nor any measurement, parameter, seed, numerical, qualification or historical-evidence rule, and it does not generalize to another campaign or waive final publication. Why: an unattended deterministic campaign must not require a continuously logged-in agent merely to preserve validity, while the owner may deliberately trade intermediate remote durability for operational simplicity. Raised by: owner custody decision before the first Pascal successor work unit, 2026-08-16.

**Amendment round 17 — 2026-08-23, G8_E closed after pass one; G8_F unopened.** Source: the post-pass-one, pre-F0 protocol audit of BR-12's artifact-corpus breadth.

- **AM-87** — **BR-12's “preregistered feasible quality band” was not executable, discovered after G8_E pass one and before any G8_F materialization, training or pass two on 2026-08-23.** Changed: `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_corpus` and `artifact_finetune_corpus_breadth` no longer claim a preregistered band; the new scope, projection, admission, deduplication, multiplicity, ordering and image-infeasibility parameters define the post-pass-one/pre-G8F complete authority-projected artifact-request universe; BR-4, BR-12, G-8 and W6 record the corrected order and mandatory owner-audit/F0 boundary. Why: the old text fixed neither artifact-quality identity nor band axis/endpoints/width, feasibility level, candidate-to-artifact projection, ordering, deduplication, PHY alias multiplicity or per-image codec-infeasibility treatment, and selected outage candidates made “selected qualities” inconsistent with “feasible artifacts”; no frozen source supplied those missing rules. Pass-one validation results were already visible, so choosing a narrow neighbourhood now would let observed winners determine training support. The correction instead uses immutable pass-one references only where AM-6 already required them — to recover dataset/ratio scope at or below the training SNR — and expands that scope through the pre-pass-one candidate/measurement authorities, packet budgets and codec identity to the complete finite exact-deduplicated projection; score, rank, margin, pass-two, learned and test outcomes never enter. One attempt per quality × training stable ID, typed codec-infeasible omission with explicit coverage and no substitution, and HOLD on every other failure make image feasibility executable without validation/test leakage. Discovery timing: after immutable G8_E pass one, before G8_F F0 authorization, corpus materialization, artifact-classifier training and pass two; G8_F scientific execution, learned training and test access were all zero. Pass-one and E2/E3/E4/E7 evidence remain byte-unchanged, test remained sealed, and no prior result requires recomputation. A broad training support generally favours the classical comparator by reducing codec-domain shift over more distortions, but finite-capacity interference makes the net effect empirically ambiguous; the rule is adopted to remove discretion, not to force either outcome. Frozen pre-pass-one structures: G8_E candidate and measurement authorities, their logical-to-structural mapping and packet budgets, the G8_D codec configuration identity, the training manifest/G1 identity and the original AM-6/AM-59 dataset/region/split scope; E4 feasibility is audit/estimate-only and does not choose membership. §2, §13, every verify clause, DEC-12, BR-1, BR-4–BR-6, BR-8, BR-11–BR-13, ER-3, G-8, G-12 and current hand-written documentation were re-read; no scientific campaign, image decode, classifier inference, optimizer step, pass two or test access occurred. Raised by: owner-directed G8_F/BR-12 protocol repair audit.

**Amendment round 18 — 2026-08-24, post-AM-87 and pre-F0; G8_F execution remains zero.** Source: the owner-directed proportionality audit of AM-87's exhaustive multiplicity.

- **AM-88** — **AM-87's complete 120-quality support is preserved, but its one-attempt-per-quality-per-training-image Cartesian multiplicity is superseded before F0 by a deterministic balanced finite sampler.** Changed: `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_support_plan` keeps AM-87's immutable support-plan bytes and exact quality identities; `artifact_finetune_sampling_plan`, `artifact_finetune_sampler_version`, `artifact_finetune_sampler_seed`, `artifact_finetune_assignment_algorithm`, `artifact_finetune_variants_per_training_image`, `artifact_finetune_assignment_information`, `artifact_finetune_assignment_forbidden_information`, `artifact_finetune_balance_rule`, `artifact_finetune_multiplicity` and `artifact_finetune_traversal_order` freeze a metadata-only pre-F1 assignment of exactly six distinct supported qualities to every training stable ID. Stable IDs are seed-keyed within each class; the 120 AM-87 quality IDs receive one seed-keyed global permutation; ascending class chunks traverse that cycle continuously, which gives the arithmetically minimum global and per-class count ranges, with no duplicate pair. Assignment reads only training stable ID, class, AM-87 quality order, seed and sampler version; pass-one score/rank/margin, selected PHY details, E4/validation outcomes, F1 outcomes, pass two, learned results, test and runtime order are forbidden. A typed image-level codec infeasibility omits its assigned pair without replacement or resampling; every unexpected codec/decoder/runtime/identity failure remains HOLD. Why: BR-12 needs representative JPEG 2000-domain training to avoid unfair clean-classifier domain shift, not exhaustive enumeration of every legitimate quality on every image. AM-87 correctly removed discretion from support, but its Cartesian product was a brute-force sufficient construction rather than a scientific requirement: 120 × 8,469 = 1,016,280 attempts and a conservative five-to-six-day, over-100-GB envelope. Six variants per image preserve every quality and every training image with exact class/global balance while reducing nominal attempts exactly 20-fold to 50,814. Timing: discovered and decided after immutable pass one and AM-87, before F0 authorization, corpus objects, classifier inference/training, optimizer steps, pass two or test access; all remain zero. G8_C, G8_D, G8_E E2–E7, pass one, G1 and AM-87 support evidence require no recomputation and remain byte-unchanged. Raised by: owner-directed AM-88 sampler repair.

**Amendment round 19 — 2026-08-24, F1 GREEN/CLOSED and before F2 optimizer step 1.** Source: the owner-authorized F2 implementation preflight, resolving only output-affecting recipe inheritance that the existing nested recipe left open.

- **AM-89** — **BR-12 fixed eight fine-tuning scalars but did not uniquely fix the parent initialization, warmup/cosine endpoint, transforms, assignment-row order, validation checkpoint selection or resume boundary.** Changed: `params.reference_classifier.artifact_finetune_recipe` is self-contained and freezes the exact G1 best clean checkpoint as parent of a distinct `artifact_finetuned` ResNet18, train seed 0, Torch SGD without Nesterov, cross-entropy, the existing keyed random-resized-crop/horizontal-flip applied to exact authenticated F1 reconstruction pixels, five-epoch linear warmup into the AM-78 zero-based epoch-start cosine schedule ending at zero, deterministic keyed permutation over all materialized assignment rows with no drop, no AMP, four workers, clean canonical Imagenette validation every epoch, maximum validation top-1 with earliest-epoch tie-break, atomic checkpointing every epoch and fail-closed epoch-boundary resume with no older-checkpoint fallback; BR-12 records that inheritance explicitly. Why: the old nested map named optimizer, LR, momentum, weight decay, schedule family, epochs, batch size and label smoothing only. Multiple output-affecting implementations could therefore comply, including random initialization, no warmup, a different cosine endpoint, clean-image substitution, deduplicated-object ordering or a different best-checkpoint rule. The narrow completion reuses the already-frozen clean-classifier controls where mathematically applicable, chooses no alternatives after observing F2 validation, and changes neither AM-87 support nor AM-88 assignments, F1 bytes, pass one, any validation artifact score, test access or another campaign. §2, §13, every verify clause, DEC-12, BR-4, BR-8, BR-12, G-8, G-12 and current hand-written documentation were re-read; at freeze, artifact-classifier optimizer steps, F2 validation inference, F3 cached-sweep rescoring, pass two, fallback, learned training and test access are all zero. Raised by: owner-authorized G8_F/F2 pre-optimizer contract audit.
